Every P1 fixture keeps its dialect; if one would move (`f04-feed-modes.nc` has `G50 S` and `G96`), WP6.2 tunes the weights so it stays a mill file; if that is not possible, it records the move in its hand-off note and G10 decides (P1 fixtures are not edited).
- The mill profile no longer treats `G70–G73 P/Q` as block references.
- Renumber rewrites local references instead of only warning; remove-block-numbers keeps referenced numbers by default.
- `tool_list`'s "a turret lathe needs a lathe profile" warning appears only on a mill profile.
- Dock → Quit and logout with a dirty document show the unsaved-changes alert.
- The status bar has a machine item on Fanuc documents ("Machine: none" until the owner defines machines); the settings dialog has a Machines page. H6 updates the P1 scenarios that count status items or settings pages.
- The script context carries `machine`, and its `profile` is the effective profile (identical to P1's for the P1 profiles with no machine).

**Wave A**

#### WP6.1 Profile validation, registry info and the resolved code databases

- **Owns:**
  - `src/lib/core/profiles/{resolve,validate,detect}.ts` and their tests (P6 wrote `resolve.ts`; WP6.1 owns it from Wave A on)
  - `src/lib/core/codes/{resolve,load,lookup}.ts` and their tests
  - `src/lib/stores/{profiles,codes}.ts` and their tests
  - `src/lib/contrib/profileSelect.ts`, `src/lib/i18n/en/profiles.ts`
  - `tests/unit/resolved.test.ts`
- **Depends on:** P6.
- **Deliver:**
  - Validation of the new fields: `machineType`, `modal.initial` (group names are strings, codes normalize), `addresses.incremental`/`diameter`/`angular`/`feedUnitWords`, `toolCall.ignore`, `numbering.references[].rewrite`, every `sets` value and `CodeParam.unit` against §7.2, and "a child sets its own `id`, `name`, `shortName`". `modal.units`, `modal.diameter` and `modal.sources` are **accepted** on the way in (`applyMachine` writes them before validation, AD-31) but reported when a profile **source file** carries them.
  - Validation of `machineParams` (§7.1): preset ids unique, one default, every `NumberInput` well-formed (`mode` one of `increment` / `calculator` / `scale`; increments and units are decimal text `0.0…01` or `1`; `incrementDeg`/`incrementSec` likewise; known classes only, and an inch increment only on a class that follows `units`); a variant's default is one of its choices; every `codes` names a database that resolves; overlays touch only `modal`, `toolCall`, `numbering`, `addresses`; detection patterns compile; `modalGroups` are group names of the database; **the profile's own `syntax.decimalPointSignificant` agrees with its default preset** — `true` only when that preset's length class is `increment` — so "none" equals the JSON.
  - `profiles.effective(profileId, eff)` (P6's version) hardened: the cache is keyed by `eff.key`, dropped on a registry change, and an overlay or variant that fails validation is reported once and falls back to the profile defaults.
  - Variant detection `detectVariants(cp, text)` in `core/profiles/detect.ts`: per variant the winning choice and its margin over the runner-up (≥ 3 to count as `detected`, otherwise the variant's default); read from the first 400 masked lines. **A variant pattern scores its weight once (presence), not per line** — unlike profile detection (P1 detect.ts rule 2), because a marker that a post repeats in every block (system B's `G99 G83 …` cycle-return lines, which match the system-A `G98`/`G99` rule) would otherwise outvote a single decisive marker such as one `G92 S`.
  - `ProfileInfo` carries `machineType`, `origin`, `parent` and `hasMachineParams`; the status picker groups children under their parent ("Fanuc (ISO) · mill / lathe").
  - Detection tie-break: `priority`, then user over built-in, then the current or default profile.
  - The code-database service serves resolved databases (`extends`, `remove`, aliases re-checked) by profile and by id (`byId`, for the variant databases); lookup and completion see them.
- **Tests:** the merge table (nested objects, arrays replace, `null` replaces, `extends` kept, `$schema` dropped); parents after children; unknown parent, cycle, depth 5; a child that inherits `id`; database override, `remove`, an alias colliding after the merge; detection ties with three inline profiles; `machineParams` validation (each rule above, one failing case each); variant detection margins on inline texts, including a text that repeats one variant's marker six times against a single marker of the other (presence scoring: the single marker wins); `effective` caching (same key → same object; a new key → a new compiled profile).
- **Acceptance:** with only the built-ins, `profiles.list()` and every P1 test are unchanged apart from the new lathe entry; resolving all built-ins takes ≤ 20 ms and one effective compile ≤ 5 ms in node.
- **Covers:** "User profiles with `extends`" (mechanism); code-database inheritance; AD-31 (profile side).

#### WP6.2 Fanuc lathe content, the mill corrections and fixtures

- **Owns:**
  - `src/lib/data/profiles/{fanuc-gcode,fanuc-lathe}.json`, `src/lib/data/profiles/index.ts`
  - `src/lib/data/codes/{fanuc,fanuc-lathe,fanuc-lathe-b}.json`, `src/lib/data/codes/index.ts`

The same `count` entries on the mill cycles of `fanuc.json` (§8.3), so a repeat count `K`/`L` is never mistaken for an arc centre or a length.
  - The mill corrections of §8.3: the `G70–G73` rule removed; mill markers and `priority: 1`; `G43`/`G44`/`G49` to group `lengthComp`; `G50` on the mill reworded as `verify`; `G92`/`G76` keep `pitchFeedAmbiguous` on the mill (a lathe program opened with the mill profile by hand must stay protected); `min` stays until M8; the mill's `machineParams` data from P6 reviewed.

- **Acceptance:** G10 passes on `tests/fixtures/resolved/{profiles,codes,effective}/fanuc-lathe*` and on the mill changes.
- **Covers:** "Fanuc lathe child profile"; X1 (content).

#### WP6.3 Reference-aware renumbering

- **Owns:**
  - `src/lib/core/transforms/{renumber,references,removeBlockNumbers}.ts` and their tests
  - `tests/fixtures/transforms/{renumber,remove-block-numbers}/**` (new cases)
  - `src/lib/contrib/ncNumbering.ts`, `src/lib/i18n/en/ncNumbering.ts`
- **Depends on:** P6.
- **Deliver:**
  - Renumber builds an old → new map per program (per `restartAtProgramStart` segment) and rewrites the values of reference words whose rule has `rewrite !== false` (§7.1). The written form is kept: zero padding as written, no decimal point.
  - A reference is **not** rewritten, and is reported, when its target is missing, when the old number occurs more than once in that program, when the target lies outside the renumbered scope, or when its rule says `rewrite: false` (`M99 P`: the target may be in the caller, F42).
  - The preflight asks only about references that will not be rewritten.
  - Remove-block-numbers gains `keepReferenced` (default true when the profile has reference rules): a number that a reference points at stays.
  - The summary reports rewritten, kept and unresolved references; the unresolved ones go to Results with their lines.
- **Tests:** goldens for `G71 P100 Q200` + `G70 P100 Q200`, `G73`, `GOTO 100`, `IF […] GOTO100`, `M98 Q50` (rewritten), `M98 P2000 Q50` (reported), `M99 P30` (reported), a duplicate target, a missing target, a selection with a reference outside it, two programs in one file, `P0100` padding; remove-block-numbers keeping `N100`/`N200`; 100k lines ≤ 1 s.
- **Covers:** "Reference-aware renumbering" (from P3, local numeric references); the Phase 1 renumber-reference finding (P1 G8 M4).

#### WP6.4 The Python modal interpreter

#### WP6.9 How the control reads numbers (TS and Python)

- **Owns:** `src/lib/core/machines/numbers.ts` and its test, `src-tauri/resources/scripts/_nc_machine.py`, `tests/python/test_machine.py`, `tests/fixtures/machines/numbers.json`.

Named cases the review checks by hand: **`scale`** — `X0.1` under 10 µm = 0.001 mm, `X1000` = 10 mm, `F23.456` = 0.23456 mm/rev, `F234.56` under 1 µm = 0.23456 mm/rev, and `X1` = `X1.0` = 1 mm under 1 mm; **angles and dwell ignore `units`** — `C90000` under IS-B is 90° in a metric and in an inch program, `G4 X2500` under IS-B is 2.5 s in both; **the class order** — one case per step of the AD-31 list (a `G83 … Q6000` `increment` parameter beating the length rule, a `G4 F2` dwell in an `fNotFeed` block, an `F` lead in a `G76` block read as per revolution although the modal feed mode says per minute, a `C` angle, a feed by modal unit, an axis, and `K3`/`L2` counts with no value); **no machine** — the readings of a point-less `X50` under the Fanuc presets differ (no value), those of `X50.` agree (50 mm), and every Okuma length word differs. The API text for the scripts README in the hand-off note.
- **Tests:** the golden file in vitest and unittest; `machine_params({})` and a context without `machine` give the profile defaults with every source `profile`; `write_back` keeps a point, keeps a point-less word point-less while the result is whole and reports the rounding otherwise, in all three readings.
- **Acceptance:** G10 (the number rules, against F52 and §8.8).
- **Covers:** AD-31 (numbers); X11 (c, d, the number half).

---

### M7: Never lose work (backup, recovery, session, per-file memory, read-only)

**Goal:** a crash, a power cut, a Windows logoff or a bad save never costs the owner an edit, and gEdit comes back the way it was left.

**Prelude P7:**
1. Settings keys of §7.11 in `core/settings/schema.ts`, labels in `i18n/en/settings.ts`.
2. Contracts §7.9: `DocMeta.readOnly`/`readOnlyReason`, `FileOps.restoreDocument`/`setReadOnly`/`onWillClose`, `BookmarkService.set`, `UiState.files`, `FileMemoryStore` (with `machineId` and `machineFor`), `SessionService`, `RecoveryService` (the snapshot metadata carries `machineId`); the stubs for `restoreDocument`, `setReadOnly` and `onWillClose` in `app/fileOps.ts`, `set` in `monaco/bookmarks.ts`, and `stores/fileMemory.ts`, so WP7.4 and WP7.5 can call them before WP7.3 lands.
3. Rust stubs `backup.rs`, `recovery.rs`, `session.rs` (§7.10), registered; `setup_app` calls `recovery::start_session` and `session::grant_on_startup`; `paths.rs` `ensure_dirs` for `backups/` and `recovery/` (0700).
4. `platform/commands.ts` wrappers; `recoveryPut` sends raw bytes plus the `x-gedit-recovery` header.
5. Harness: `h_crash()` (the app sends itself `SIGKILL`) and `h_recovery_dir()` in `tests/runtime/harness/harness.rs`, helpers in `tests/runtime/lib/api.js`.
6. TS stubs `app/recovery.ts`, `app/session.ts`; commit.

**Intentional behavior changes in M7:**
- Saving an existing file first writes a history backup (default).
- A file with the read-only attribute opens locked.
- At start, leftover recovery sessions open the restore dialog, and the last session is restored (the harness starts with a fresh `HOME`, so earlier scenarios see neither).

**Wave A**

#### WP7.1 Rust: backup and session

- **Owns:** `src-tauri/src/{backup,session,state,paths}.rs` and their tests.
- **Depends on:** P7.
- **Deliver:** AD-21 backup and AD-22 session per §7.10 and the §4 rules; `state.rs` exposes one read-modify-write under a lock for `recent`, `ui` and `session`.
- **Tests (cargo):** history naming and pruning to N; same-millisecond collisions get a suffix; `sibling` overwrites and refuses a symlink or a directory; a not-allowed path is refused; a missing file → `Ok(None)`; the mode and count read from `settings.json` (off, sibling, history); the session keeps allowed paths only, caps at 50, and re-grants given and canonical paths; unknown `state.json` members survive.
- **Covers:** "Backup" (backend), "session restore" (backend).

- **Tests (cargo, injected clock):** raw round trip of 5 MB; the current session is never listed; a stale `alive` → leftover, a fresh one → not; pruning by age and by size; an id with `..` or `/` refused; the caps.

#### H7 Harness M7

- `m7-backup`: save twice; the history holds the earlier bytes (`h_hex`); `sibling` mode; `off`.
- `m7-recovery-1/2`: edit two documents, `recovery.flushNow()`, `h_crash`; run 2 (same `--home`) shows the dialog; Restore gives the exact text, dirty, on the original path; a fixture rewritten between the runs shows the external-change banner; Save then drops the entry; Discard empties the leftover.
- `m7-session-1/2`: three tabs, cursor lines, bookmarks, a manual lathe choice on a mill-looking file, a machine on one lathe tab and an explicit "none" on another while a default machine exists; run 2 restores them all (the "none" tab stays none).
- `m7-readonly`: a `chmod 444` fixture opens locked, typing is refused with the message, Save goes to Save As; `file.toggleReadOnly` on a normal file.
- `m7-perf-recovery`: a 10 MB dirty document; the snapshot runs off the keystroke path and blocks the main thread ≤ 100 ms; typing p95 unchanged.

**Gates:** G0–G6 (`m0`–`m7`); G7 (the snapshot budget; a save of a 10 MB file with history backup ≤ 1 s longer than without; a 10-tab session restores ≤ 3 s; P1 budgets); G8 (capability diff empty; folder permissions; `is_allowed` on every path command; standing rules 6, 8, 10).

**Commit:** `M7: Backup on save, crash recovery, session restore, per-file memory and read-only files`

### M8: Okuma OSP and Sinumerik turning

**Goal:** the owner's Okuma and Siemens turning programs are recognized, highlighted without false errors, navigable by tool, and safe to scale.

**Prelude P8:**
1. Take in any public Okuma and Sinumerik programs the owner has handed over (§9.2); D34 and D35 are decided (§10.1).
2. Contracts: `grammar` widened to `okuma` | `sinumerik`; the `syntax` fields of AD-24 and `addresses.speedLimitWords` (§7.1); token kinds `label` and `call` (§7.5); `CodeEntry.fNotFeed` (§7.2). The enum additions in `core/profiles/validate.ts` and the new patterns compiled once in `core/profiles/compile.ts` (`re.assignment`, `re.labels`, `re.systemVariables`, `re.header`), the same mechanical kind of change P1's P3 made there.
3. Skeleton profiles `okuma-osp.json` and `sinumerik.json` (syntax sections from the notes, detection weight 0, a minimal `machineParams` with the default preset and `diameter: "on"`, so the machine item and the effective view work from the start) and database stubs `okuma.json`, `sinumerik.json`, all registered; `fanuc-gcode.json` loses `min` (F22). **The `syntax` sections written here are pinned for the milestone:** the tokenizer, grammar and content WPs all test against them; a content WP that needs a syntax change writes a hand-off note instead of editing it.
4. Grammar stubs `core/grammar/{okuma,sinumerik}.ts` (returning the `iso` grammar until replaced) and the dispatch in `core/grammar/index.ts`; per-grammar `wordPattern` in `monaco/languages.ts` (keeps `T010101`, `SB=`, `NLAP1`, `CYCLE81`, `$AA_IM`, `R10` together).
5. `tests/gen/gen-large.mjs --dialect okuma|sinumerik`; the detection golden files `expected/detect/{okuma,sinumerik}.json` (empty); commit.

**Intentional behavior changes in M8:** `.min`, `.sub`, `.ssb` files open as Okuma; `.mpf`, `.spf` as Sinumerik.

### M9: Real programs read right

**Goal:** gEdit reads the owner's real programs the way their machines read them: the right dialect, every token, the tool words, the incremental axes and the class of every number. Every later feature reads values (the checks, the extents, address arithmetic, compare), so this milestone comes first and has its own gates and reviews (third revision, D69). It is measured on the committed owner-public programs in CI and on the owner's local programs by G11 (X13).

**Status (2026-10-04): shipped. Implemented on `m9/int` (2026-10-03), on `main` since 2026-10-04, and v0.3.0 is published (§5.3).** All of P9, WP9.1–WP9.7 and H9 are in, and a review of the integrated branch (twelve findings, all fixed or recorded) came after them, then a second review round of five more NC findings (NC-F1, NC-F2, NC-F3, NC-F6 and NC-F7, all fixed; §7.16 #101–#105). The gates on `m9/int`: `npm run check` 0 errors and 0 warnings, 5,589 unit tests (1 skipped), Python 591 tests on 3.13, 3.12 and 3.9, 265 cargo tests, the build, the runtime suite `m0`–`m9` (88 scenarios) on a GitHub macOS runner, and G11 on the owner's machine (17 programs: detection 17 of 17, scale feed and scale speed at 100 % byte-identical 17 of 17; the unknown-token check lists ten programs and the map check one, which are owner questions, §10.2). What shipped, in one list: the Sinumerik milling profile; code databases grown by 46 (Fanuc mill), 32 (Klartext), 19 (Sinumerik) and 16 (Okuma) entries, with the two flags; the tokenizer rules; the `U`/`W` and tool-word variants; the number classes, the per-unit feed limits and the `G71`/`G72` guard; the Klartext defined cycle; Remove Comments keeping `;$PATH=`, Save As re-detecting, main blocks renumbered; the Y-axis lathe marker and the Sinumerik priorities; G11 and the owner-public check as tests. The decisions M9 took are §7.16 #57–#105 and §10.3 D71–D75; what it left is §10.2 and the M10 prelude (§6 M10).

**Where the work comes from.** The roadmap proposals R2, R3, R4 and R6, which the owner accepted on 2026-09-27 into the prelude of the old M9 (§10.1); the values-read-wrong items of TODO "Bugs and risks > NC correctness"; the last "Next up" item (a Fanuc-lathe `G71`/`G72` `F` without `P` under a wrong dialect guess); the detection leftovers of 2026-09-27; and G11, which is still a stub (TODO "Tech debt and test gaps").

**Prelude P9:**
1. Record the re-cut (D69) and the releases (D70), both accepted on 2026-10-01, and the accepted R2, R3, R4 and R6 (§10.1) in the commit body.
2. **Roadmap R2, accepted by the owner 2026-09-27:** a new `sinumerik-mill` profile extending `sinumerik` — `M6` tool changes with a preselect (a `T` alone), diameter off, `G17`/`G94` power-on, detection weights for milling syntax (`M6`, `G17`, `CYCLE8…(`, `T…="…"`, `MSG(`, counting the ordinary `G`/`M`/move lines the turning profile leaves out on `Y`) — and about 20 database entries (`CYCLE800`, `CYCLE832`, `TRAORI`/`TRAFOOF`, `G75`, the `ORI…`/`DYN…` codes), sized as the source review's §5 R2 sizes it. Mill-turn (`DIAMON`, `LIMS`, `SETMS`, `TRANSMIT`) stays with the turning profile — a mill-turn program is still read as turning. The user guide's own sketch of this profile ([dialects.md](../user/dialects.md#milling-on-okuma-and-sinumerik)) is the starting shape. P9 writes the **skeleton**: `data/profiles/sinumerik-mill.json` (its `syntax` inherited, detection weight 0, the `machineParams` of the turning profile with `diameter` off) and, if the entries do not belong in the shared `sinumerik.json`, a `data/codes/sinumerik-mill.json` database extending `sinumerik` (AD-17), both registered; which of the two holds each entry is decided here and written into WP9.1's hand-off.
3. **Roadmap R3, accepted by the owner 2026-09-27:** two database flags — `wordsAreData` (`dec/scaling` already delivered it for `G65`/`G66`/`G10` and the other macro/offset/setting words; whether the same flag, or a second one, also covers a block's plain axis words as data is open for the prelude to decide) and a new flag marking a code that opens or closes a coordinate frame. WP10.3 (extents) and WP10.4 (address arithmetic) read these flags instead of hard-coded `G53`/`G28` lists. P9 writes both into §7.2 (`CodeEntry`), validated in `core/codes/load.ts` and mirrored in the Python reader, and records the choice in §7.16. In the same step it pins a lower/upper flag on `sets.speedLimit` (§7.2), which WP9.5a fills in.
4. **Roadmap R4, accepted 2026-09-27, remaining parts:** tokenizer rules in TS and Python on shared goldens, one golden per case — the Fanuc macro function and print names (`FIX[`, `POPEN`); Okuma two-letter `=` words and four-digit option M codes; the Sinumerik `:123` main block and the call-rule exclusions (`CUT3DCC`, `ORIRESET(`); Klartext `TIP-TIP` and the `PLANE` words. (The Klartext decimal comma and the Fanuc program names are already done: `dec/klartext`'s `syntax.decimalSeparatorAlt`, `dec/names`'s `syntax.programNames`.) Any new `syntax` field goes into §7.1 and §7.16; the `syntax` sections and the call rules of the five existing profiles are written here and **pinned for the milestone**, as P8 did, so WP9.3 and the content WPs test against the same data and a WP that needs a change writes a hand-off note instead.
5. **Roadmap R6, accepted 2026-09-27, smallest useful version:** a Fanuc lathe machine choice "`U`/`W`/`V`/`H` are incremental" (on in system A, off on some system-B machines), and a tool-word variant for the `T` split (Fanuc offset digits 1/2/3, Okuma three-digit offsets on machines with 200 or more offsets); both as variants and overlays of the existing machine parameters (AD-31), no new consumer. The rest of R6 (extra axis letters, an IS-A preset, the ×10 option, the point-less feed-per-revolution unit) stays with §11 #26. P9 pins the variant ids and choices in §8.8; WP9.4 writes the data.
6. The contract of the Klartext **"defined cycle"** state for §7.4 (AD-19): a `CYCL DEF` defines a cycle that each call runs and no call ends, and only the next definition replaces it, driven by the database's `sets` with no dialect name in code, next to Fanuc's active cycle; recorded in §7.16. Python only (`_nc_modal.py`); the TypeScript interpreter follows the same goldens in Phase 3 (P3.1).
7. The G11 manifest format (§9.2) gains the aggregates X13 reads (detection, unknown tokens outside comments with an allow-list, the map, scale feed and scale speed at 100 %), written into `tests/real/README.md`; a golden format for the owner-public known gaps (`tests/fixtures/expected/owner-public/known-gaps.json`: each remaining unknown token or map difference with its reason).
8. **Spike, size S (§5.2 rule 12):** run `tests/runtime/suite.sh` on a GitHub macOS runner (a CI job started by hand, `workflow_dispatch`, no secrets). The result — works, works with changes, or does not work and why — goes into the P9 commit body. If it works, a follow-up adds the job to `ci.yml`, and G6 then stops depending on the owner's screen. It is not a gate of M9.
9. `npm run check && npm test && cargo check && python3 -m unittest discover -s tests/python -t .`; commit.

**What P9 decided (2026-10-03; the hand-off is `SP/handoff/m9-p9.md`):**
- **Item 2 — the milling entries live in the shared `sinumerik.json` database; there is no `sinumerik-mill.json` database.** The Siemens language is one language on both kinds of machine (one programming manual, the same G codes and cycles), and the differences between milling and turning are the profile's: the tool rule, the power-on plane and feed type, the diameter mode. A mill-turn program stays with the turning profile (R2) and needs `CYCLE800`, `TRAORI` and `CYCLE832` just as much, which a milling-only child database would hide from it. A child database (AD-17) is for a code that **means something else** on the child, and no Siemens milling entry does; one would only add a resolved file per variant and a second place to review. The skeleton `data/profiles/sinumerik-mill.json` extends `sinumerik`, names no `codes` of its own (it inherits `sinumerik`), sets `machineType: "mill"`, its own filter name, detection weight 0 (extensions 0, one milling marker with weight 0, so it never wins) and `machineParams.diameter: "off"`; it is registered after `sinumerik`. Its empty detection golden and a token golden of 69 milling lines (inherited syntax, read as P9 reads it) are committed.
- **Item 3 — a second flag, not a wider `wordsAreData`**, and a frame flag: `CodeEntry.axisWords?: 'data' | 'machine'` and `CodeEntry.frame?: 'open' | 'close'`; `wordsAreData` keeps its meaning and is read as covering the axis words too. Why: `G50 X… Z… S…` (lathe A) and `G92 X… S…` set coordinates with axis words that are data **and** carry a clamp the speed script must still see, so making the whole block data would have changed what `scale_speed` does with every clamp; and `G53`/`M91`/`G75`/`SUPA` hold positions that are not data at all, only not in the program's frame, which WP10.3 lists and WP10.4 refuses. The lower/upper direction is `CodeSets.speedLimitBound?: 'upper' | 'lower'` beside `speedLimit` (absent = `'upper'`), not a widened `speedLimit`, so every reader that asks "is this `S` a speed?" (`speedLimit === true`) stays right without a change. All three are typed, validated in `core/codes/load.ts`, read by `axisWordsOf`/`frameOf`/`speedLimitBoundOf` (`core/codes/lookup.ts`) and by `axis_words_of`/`frame_of`/`speed_limit_bound_of` (`gedit_nc`), with one contract test per language; no shipped entry sets them yet (§7.2, §7.16 #47–#48).
- **Item 4 — four new opt-in fields and three values in existing fields**, all in §7.1 "The M9 syntax pins", with the pin rule and its test (§7.16 #50–#55).
- **Item 5 — the variants** in §8.8 "The M9 variants".
- **Item 6 — the defined cycle** in §7.4 (§7.16 #49).
- **Item 7 —** `tests/real/README.md` "What the checks do" and "What the run reports"; `tests/fixtures/expected/owner-public/known-gaps.json`, measured at the P9 commit.
- **Item 8** is a separate task (the CI spike); its result goes into the M9 commit body.

**Intentional behavior changes in M9** (H9 may update earlier scenarios and goldens only for these):
- A Sinumerik milling program opens as `sinumerik-mill`: a `T` alone is a preselect, `M6` is the tool change, and the tool list gives one row per tool. A turning or mill-turn program keeps `sinumerik`.
- Words that were misread change their token or class: `FIX[#2]` is no `X` word, `POPEN` no five words, an Okuma `TL=` no local variable, `M1292` a code, a Sinumerik `:123` a main block number, `TIP-TIP` and the `PLANE` words keywords. A token golden changes only for these cases.
- Values change where a number class was wrong (the list in WP9.5); every changed golden names its item.
- Save As to another extension re-detects the dialect; Remove Comments keeps `;$PATH=`.
- Detection moves where WP9.6 says (the Y-axis lathe, the Fanuc lathe / Sinumerik tie); every committed fixture keeps its folder's dialect.

**Wave A**

#### WP9.1 The Sinumerik milling profile (R2)

- **Owns:** `src/lib/data/profiles/sinumerik-mill.json`, the milling and 5-axis entries of the shared `src/lib/data/codes/sinumerik.json` (P9 chose the shared database, see "What P9 decided"; there is no `sinumerik-mill.json` database) together with their `axisWords` and `frame` flags, `src/lib/data/profiles/index.ts`, `tests/fixtures/nc/sinumerik-mill/**`, `tests/fixtures/expected/detect/sinumerik-mill.json`, `tests/fixtures/expected/outline/sinumerik-mill/**`, the Sinumerik 5-axis and high-speed entries R3 names ("Sinumerik as R2 brings"). For this Wave A only, also the Siemens **milling** programs of the owner-public set, which change profile: `git mv` (byte-exact) of the milling `.mpf` files from `tests/fixtures/nc/owner-public/sinumerik/` to `owner-public/sinumerik-mill/` (the folder is the profile id), their lines in `tests/fixtures/README.md`, `tests/fixtures/expected/detect/owner-public.json`, and their entries of `tests/fixtures/expected/outline/owner-public/_known-gaps.json` with the outline goldens that replace them (`expected/outline/owner-public/sinumerik-mill/**`). The new `known-gaps.json` of X13 stays WP9.6's, which measures it again after Wave A.
- **Depends on:** P9.
- **Deliver:** the profile of R2 and the prelude's skeleton filled in: the tool rule (`T` alone a preselect, `M6` the change, `T="NAME"` and `T1` both read), `machineParams` with diameter off, `G17`/`G94` power-on, the milling detection weights; about 20 entries (`CYCLE800`, `CYCLE832`, `TRAORI`/`TRAFOOF`, `G75`, `ORI…`, `DYN…`) with their parameter classes, in our own words from `syntax-sinumerik.md`; synthetic milling fixtures (§9.1) with detection and outline goldens.

#### WP9.2 5-axis and high-speed codes and the two flags (R3)

- **Owns:** `src/lib/data/codes/{fanuc,heidenhain}.json` (the new entries and the two flags), `src/lib/core/codes/{load,lookup}.ts` and their tests (the flags; P9's `src/lib/core/codes/flagsContract.test.ts` included), the flag reading in `src-tauri/resources/scripts/gedit_nc.py` and its three readers in `_nc_modal.py` (`axis_words_of`, `frame_of`, `speed_limit_bound_of`, for Wave A; WP9.5b owns the rest of the file), `tests/python/test_code_flags.py`, a data test for the flags (`src/lib/data/codes/flags.test.ts`, new).
- **Depends on:** P9.
- **Deliver:** the flags are P9's (`CodeEntry.axisWords`, `CodeEntry.frame`, `wordsAreData` read as covering the axis words; §7.2): this WP **sets** them. ---

### M10: Check before the machine

**Goal:** the program can be checked before it goes to the machine — the program checks and the extents, lathe-aware and machine-aware — and the daily edits that change positions are safe: a Z shift by address arithmetic, and block skip on a prove-out section. All three scripts run on the Python interpreter that shipped in M6 and read the values M9 made right.

**Why here** (third revision, D69): address arithmetic sat in the old M11 behind the TypeScript modal interpreter, although as a script it needs only the Python interpreter; it is a daily task on a lathe, and a scrap risk on Klartext and Sinumerik cycle depths (source review R8). It belongs with the checks and the extents, which read the same values and the same database flags (R3).

**Status (2026-10-04): implemented on `m10/int`, landing on `main` after the owner's check; v0.4 follows (§5.3).** All of P10, WP10.1–WP10.4, H10 and I10 are in, then two reviews of the integrated branch: eleven NC findings (NC-1 to NC-11) and twelve code findings (CODE-1 to CODE-12, CODE-2 being NC-11), all fixed or recorded (§7.16 #123–#133; the ones that need the owner are §10.2 M10-1 to M10-6). The gates on the branch: `npm run check` 852 files, 0 errors and 0 warnings; 5,700 unit tests (1 skipped); Python 803 tests on 3.13, 3.12 and 3.9; 265 cargo tests (last run before the review fixes, which touched no Rust and no script header); the build; G11 on the owner's machine (17 programs: detection, tool list, scale feed and scale speed, program checks, extents and address arithmetic with an addition of 0 all 17 of 17, no crash of any script; the unknown-token, map and round-trip results are the M9 owner questions, §10.2). The runtime suite `m0`–`m10` (92 scenarios) ran once on the GitHub macOS runner before the review fixes: **89 passed**, and `m1-keys` (the new `Mod+F7` was not in its table of default keys), `m10-blockskip` (the scenario expected a segment to end on the blank line after it) and `m10-perf` (the 300,000-line extents run, which the script's own `timeout = 300` made longer than the setting the scenario judged by) failed. The three are fixed (CODE-1, CODE-2, CODE-3 and CODE-4; §7.16 #132) and **have not run again**: that is the first thing the next run on the runner shows. What shipped, in one list: block skip insert and remove (position by dialect, levels, never twice), select tool segment on `Mod+F7`; **Program checks** (31 checks), **Extents** and **Address arithmetic** as bundled scripts, with the code database growing the members they read (§7.2); the R8 role on the drilling cycles, with the refusals; the modal state's frame, tool centre point control and Klartext plane; Klartext cycles 202, 208 and 262; the Fanuc `G15`/`G16` and the Sinumerik `G290`/`G291`; the user documentation (WP10.5). The decisions M10 took are §7.16 #106–#133 and §10.1 "Decided for M10"; what it left is §10.2 and TODO.

**Prelude P10:**
1. **Roadmap R8, smallest version** (accepted 2026-10-01, §10.1): a parameter role "absolute position on the tool axis" in the code database (§7.2, `CodeParam`), set on the drilling cycles' parameters that hold one (Klartext `Q203`, Sinumerik `RTP`/`RFP`/`DP`, and the like, each in the G10 table), so a shift on the tool axis moves them with the axis words; **everything else address arithmetic cannot judge is refused and listed** — a cycle call with such a position but no role, a machine-coordinate block (`M91`, `M92`, `G75`, `SUPA`, `G53`), a block inside a tilted frame (R3's frame flag), simultaneous rotary moves without a TCP mode. The role's name and type are pinned here and recorded in §7.16. R8 was accepted on 2026-10-01, so the prelude ships the role and the refusals together; the refusal alone (every drilling cycle with a tool-axis position refused and listed) was the fallback while it was open.
2. The command ids and keys of §7.13 for WP10.1 with `findConflicts` clean; the i18n keys for the ribbon entries.
3. `tests/fixtures/scripts/address_arithmetic/README.md` with the golden format (the `machine` member as in §7.4); commit.
4. **What M9 leaves for this prelude** (each is in §10.2 with its reason):
   - **The TCP frame question.** One member of `TRAORI` and `TRAFOOF` in `sinumerik.json` and their rows in `tests/fixtures/codes/flags.json` (a sentence in `flags.test.ts` pins today's state): `TRAORI` is `frame: open` and `TRAFOOF` `close` while Fanuc and Klartext tool centre point control is no frame (§7.16 #57), so address arithmetic would refuse every block under `TRAORI` and judge blocks under `G43.4` or `FUNCTION TCPM`. Recommended: no `frame` on `TRAORI`, `close` kept on `TRAFOOF`, and one shared TCP on/off flag for `TRAORI`, `G43.4`, `G43.5`, `M128` and `FUNCTION TCPM`; `CYCLE800`, `G68.2`, `PLANE` and cycle 19 stay frames. Nothing reads `frameOf` yet, so today it changes no reading. **Review NC-F12 (2026-10-03) recommends the same: `TRAORI` is tool-centre-point control, so it is treated like `G43.4`, `G43.5`, `M128` and `FUNCTION TCPM` and not as a frame; `TRAFOOF` keeps `close` for `TRANSMIT`, `TRACYL` and `TRAANG`; one shared TCP on/off flag is worth considering.** Decided on 2026-10-04 as recommended (§10.1, "What P10 decided").
   - **A `CYCL CALL` after a definition the database lacks** (`CYCL DEF 202`, `208`, `262`) reads as "nothing defined" (§7.4 rule 8); WP10.2 must not report it as an error while a generic `CYCL DEF` came before. Adding the three entries is the remedy; their `Q206` would then start being scaled by Scale Feed.
   - **Klartext polar moves** (`LP PR… PA…`, `CP`) carry no axis word, so they are not positioning blocks under `M89` (§7.4 rule 4); and the rotary axes `A` and `B` of `sinumerik-mill` are not in its `addresses` (WP10.3 decides whether extents need them).
   - **Remove Block Numbers** with "keep numbers that are pointed at" off writes no per-line row for a `GOTOB :20` jump, though the question before the run counts it (WP10.2 or a fix batch).
   - **`G931`** (travel time) has its own feed unit and is never scaled (§7.16 #96); a real conversion belongs with the checks.

**What P10 decided (2026-10-04; the hand-off is `SP/handoff/m10-p10.md`; the owner and orchestrator decisions of that day are in §10.1):**
- **Item 1 — the R8 role is `CodeParam.position?: 'tool-axis' | 'none' | 'other' | 'mode'`** (§7.2, §7.16 #106). `'tool-axis'`: an absolute coordinate on the tool axis, moved with the axis words by a shift on the tool axis, which is `Z` while the plane in force is `XY` (any other plane, or an unknown one, refuses: Fanuc's drilling axis there is a parameter, a Sinumerik cycle can choose its own). `'none'`: reviewed, no absolute position. `'other'`: an absolute position the role does not cover — the block is refused whenever its entry has one. `'mode'`: a mode argument; absent, empty or `0` keeps the reading, any other value refuses. **Absent: not reviewed**, which refuses a cycle call that writes it. Set (G10 table §8.10): the `R` of the Fanuc mill drilling cycles (`G73`, `G74`, `G76`, `G81`–`G89`, `G84.2`, `G84.3`); Klartext `Q203` of cycles 200, 201, 202, 203, 205, 206, 207, 208, 209, 240, 262; Sinumerik `RTP`, `RFP`, `DP` of `CYCLE81`–`CYCLE89` and `CYCLE840`, and `FDEP` of `CYCLE83`, with every other parameter of those entries reviewed (`'none'` or `'mode'`) and the trailing arguments a newer control writes described (`_GMODE`, `_DMODE`, `_AMODE`, `_AXN` as `'mode'`). `'other'`: the points of `CYCLE800`, and **`CYCL CALL POS` and `CYCL CALL PAT`** — the cycle manual says the tool-axis coordinate of `CYCL CALL POS` acts as an additional datum shift on top of the cycle's `Q203` (and a start point the cycle defines adds to its `X`/`Y`), so moving both would move the hole twice; a pattern's points are outside the block. The lathes and Okuma carry no role (the `R` of a Fanuc lathe drilling cycle is incremental or absolute by a machine parameter), so every cycle there is refused and listed. The refusals and their vocabulary are `tests/fixtures/scripts/address_arithmetic/README.md`.
- **Item 2 — the WP10.1 commands** keep the ids and keys of §7.13 (`nc.blockSkip.add`, `nc.blockSkip.remove`, `nav.selectToolSegment` on `Mod+F7`); the i18n keys of their titles and ribbon groups are pinned there and stubbed in `src/lib/i18n/en/{ncBlockSkip,segments}.ts`. `tests/unit/commandPins.test.ts` runs the registry's `findConflicts` over every shortcut the shipped contributions declare plus the pinned rows, on both platforms: clean.
- **Item 3 —** `tests/fixtures/scripts/address_arithmetic/README.md`: the case folder (the `machine` member as in §7.4), the form ids, the envelope message, and the refusal vocabulary — every finding carries `reason` (the app ignores members it does not know), one finding per refused block (the first reason that applies) and per skipped word. The report formats the plan left open are pinned the same way: `tests/fixtures/scripts/program_checks/README.md` (one row per finding with `checkId`, the check ids) and `tests/fixtures/scripts/extents/README.md` (rows by scope and axis, machine positions apart, positions inside a frame not resolved).
- **Item 4 — what M9 left:** **the TCP frame question** is decided (§10.1, 2026-10-04): one shared flag `CodeSets.tcp: 'on' | 'off'` on `TRAORI`/`TRAFOOF`, `G43.4`/`G43.5`/`G49`, `M128`/`M129`, `FUNCTION TCPM`/`FUNCTION RESET TCPM`; `TRAORI` lost its `frame: 'open'`, `TRAFOOF` keeps its close (§7.16 #107). The modal interpreter tracks **the frame in force and TCP** for every script (§7.4 rules 13 and 14), with two data changes that make it safe: a close ends only the frames of its own `group` (the Fanuc frame families got groups of their own; `CYCLE800` is group `tilt`), and `CYCLE800()`, `TRANS`, `ROT`, `SCALE`, `MIRROR` written without values close (`CodeEntry.frameWithoutValues`, §7.16 #109). **A call after a definition the database lacks**: the three cycles the owner's programs write, 202, 208 and 262, are in `heidenhain.json` with their roles (§7.16 #110); WP10.2 reports "no cycle defined" only when no cycle-group code came before (its README). **Klartext polar moves** stay no positioning blocks; WP10.3 resolves them from the pole or counts them "not resolved". **The rotary axes**: `sinumerik-mill` lists `A`, `B`, `C` as axes and angles, Klartext `A`, `B`, `C` as angles (§7.16 #111). The Klartext plane comes from the tool axis of `TOOL CALL` (`sets.planeFromAxisWord`, §7.4 rule 15, §7.16 #108), so `Q203`'s tool axis is known. **Remove Block Numbers and `GOTOB :20`** has no M10 owner and goes to the fix batch after M10 (TODO). **`G931`** gets no conversion in M10.
- **Decision 3 (§10.1):** `S1=` and `S[1]=` are the main spindle's speed in the modal state and the tool list where `addresses.mainSpindle` is 1, as in `scale_speed` (§7.16 #112). Two goldens change for it, named: `modal/sinumerik/packed.json` lines 22 and 28 (speed `900`), `scripts/tool_list/sinumerik-tools` (`T5` speed `900`).
- **Owns (Wave A), confirmed disjoint:** each WP owns exactly its files below; **nobody in Wave A owns** `_nc_modal.py`, `gedit_nc.py`, `_nc_machine.py`, the code databases, the profiles, `tests/fixtures/{codes,modal,machines}/**` or the scripts README — a WP that needs a change there writes a hand-off note for I10. WP10.1 additionally owns its contributions' tests and `tests/unit/commandPins.test.ts`; WP10.2–WP10.4 own the README of their fixture folder from Wave A on (P10 wrote it as the contract).

**Intentional behavior changes in M10:** the Tools tab and the script list gain program checks, extents and address arithmetic; the NC tab gains block skip and tool-segment select (H10 updates an earlier scenario only where it counts those entries — `m4-scripts`/`m5-common` `BUNDLED`, `m5-scripts`). From P10: the modal state's `frame`, `tcp` and the Klartext plane (new keys, no earlier golden compares them); `S1=`/`S[1]=` as the main spindle's speed (the two goldens above); the hover of the Fanuc scaling, mirror, polar and cylindrical codes and of `CYCLE800` names their new group; the Klartext cycles 202, 208 and 262 are described (hover, completion; Scale Feed lists their feeds as it lists cycle 200's); the lathe database no longer has `G43.4`/`G43.5`; the Sinumerik milling grammar paints `A` and `B` as axis words.

#### WP10.2 Program checks script


- **Owns:** `src-tauri/resources/scripts/program_checks.py`, `tests/python/test_program_checks.py`, `tests/fixtures/scripts/program_checks/**` (P10's `README.md` included: the report format and the check ids).
- **Depends on:** P10 (the M6 interpreter, the M8 dialects, the values M9 made right).
- **Deliver:** a `report` script with a bool parameter per check, all on by default and all profile-, database- and machine-driven: a cutting move with the spindle stopped or never started; a tool change without a following spindle start; a tool change while a cycle is active; the `M0`/`M1` list; the program frame (start/end markers missing or duplicated, code after the end); **a dimension word whose reading depends on the machine**, judged by the effective machine (AD-31): with increment input a warning per point-less word that names the value it is read as ("`X50`: 0.050 mm on machine 'Lathe 2'"), with calculator-type or `scale` input no finding (the machine reads every literal one way), and with **no machine** a warning that lists the readings of the profile's presets, the assumed default first — one per word where only point-less words differ (Fanuc), and one for the program where every number does (Okuma: "the unit system decides every value; choose a machine"); lower-case addresses outside comments; unclosed comments; non-ASCII characters and blocks longer than `syntax.maxLineLength` (the reporting half of character cleanup); **lathe**: `G96` without an earlier clamp (`sets.speedLimit`), an offset cancel (`T..00`) followed by a cutting move, `G70`–`G73` `P`/`Q` targets missing or in the wrong order, a subprogram call inside a `P…Q` profile, a thread cycle under `G96` (warning); the Okuma and Sinumerik items from `syntax-okuma.md` §9 1–3, 9, 13, 14 and `syntax-sinumerik.md` §9 1–3, 8, 12. **Roadmap R7, accepted by the owner 2026-09-27,** adds, each data-driven like the rest of this script: Fanuc — `%` inside a comment, a program reaching `%` with no end code, a word over eight digits, `G65` sharing its block with other words, mismatched `DO`/`END` numbers, the `M29` rigid-tap rules, the 5-axis modal-state rules; Klartext — a tool call or `M91`/`M92` while TCPM is on, a cycle call with no cycle defined, `PLANE` with no `MOVE`/`TURN`/`STAY`, a code the TNC 640 refuses; Sinumerik — a cut after `G332` with no new `S`, `G75` under tool-radius compensation, `G291`; Okuma — the `G137`/`G138`/`G140` rules and the two-turret spindle rule. R7 also **drops** the planned Okuma "`T` not 4 or 6 digits" error — it would flag every machining-centre program and every lathe with 200 or more offsets (already corrected in `syntax-okuma.md` §9 item 14).
- **Tests:** a golden finding list per check and dialect; the decimal-point check under none, an IS-B machine and a calculator machine on `l06-decimal.nc` and under each Okuma preset; no finding on the clean exit fixtures.
- **Acceptance:** G10 (check semantics).
- **Covers:** "Program checks"; "character cleanup" (report part); X8.

#### WP10.3 Extents script


- **Owns:** `src-tauri/resources/scripts/extents.py`, `tests/python/test_extents.py`, `tests/fixtures/scripts/extents/**` (P10's `README.md` included: the report format).
- **Depends on:** P10 (R3's flags and R6's `U`/`W` choice from M9).
- **Deliver:** minimum and maximum per axis, per tool and overall, in **effective values** (`resolve_value`: a word is converted by its class and reading, and a word with no resolved value — including every machine-dependent word while no machine is chosen, AD-31 — is "not resolved" and counted, never guessed); arc extremes in the active plane (I/J/K and R; lathe `G18` with X as a diameter); incremental words resolved (`G91`, lathe `U`/`W`, Klartext `I` prefix); work-offset changes start a group; `G28`/`G53` listed separately; lathe multi-pass cycles and variables reported as "not resolved"; **one X column, in diameter values**: on a profile with `addresses.diameter`, every X value is converted to a diameter by AD-19 rule 11 (a radius word — `DIAMOF`, or `DIAM90` with incremental distance — counts double) and the column is labelled "X (diameter)", so a program that switches `DIAMON`/`DIAMOF` never mixes the two in one minimum or maximum; the table's header names the machine or says "no machine: readings assumed from the profile defaults", and the diameter mode with its source.
- **Tests:** goldens for mill, lathe (diameter), Klartext and one arc case per quadrant; `l06-decimal.nc` under IS-B, calculator and none; a Sinumerik program switching `DIAMOF` (the same X minimum whether the section is written as a radius or as a diameter) and one with `DIAM90` plus `G91`.
- **Acceptance:** G10.
- **Covers:** "Extents"; X8. Roadmap R3 and R6, accepted 2026-09-27: extents reads R3's two database flags (words-are-data, frame open/close) instead of guessing from a hard-coded `G53`/`G28` list, and R6's `U`/`W`-incremental machine choice instead of assuming incremental on every system.

#### WP10.4 Address arithmetic script


- **Owns:** `src-tauri/resources/scripts/address_arithmetic.py`, `tests/python/test_address_arithmetic.py`, `tests/fixtures/scripts/address_arithmetic/**` (P10's `README.md` included: the golden format, the form ids and the refusal vocabulary).
- **Depends on:** P10 (R8's role and refusals), M9 (R3's flags, R6's `U`/`W` choice).
- **Deliver:** add, subtract, multiply or divide on chosen addresses; only literal values; `G`/`M`/`N`/`O`/`T` excluded; incremental words and blocks skipped and reported (`G91` mode, `addresses.incremental`, the Klartext `I` prefix); arc centres unchecked by default when adding, included for multiply; `G53`/`G28` blocks skipped; `=` expressions skipped; the P1 number format. **Reading numbers (AD-31):** the operand of add/subtract is in the program's units; each word's effective value comes from `resolve_value`, the result goes back through `write_back` in the word's own form (a point-less increment or unit word stays a whole number of increments or units, rounded half away from zero and reported when rounded; calculator mode writes the P1 way); multiply and divide are unit-free; `unit: 'increment'` and `count` parameters (the §8.2 table: dwell times, repeat counts `K`/`L`, block and program numbers) are excluded unless chosen explicitly; a word with **no resolved value** — including every machine-dependent word while no machine is chosen — is skipped and reported ("the reading depends on the machine; choose a machine"), never converted with an assumed default. **Diameter and radius:** on a profile with `addresses.diameter` the form asks whether the X operand is a diameter or a radius value, and each word is converted by its own mode (AD-19 rule 11), so a Sinumerik program that switches `DIAMON`/`DIAMOF` is shifted correctly; `IC()` and other expression words are skipped as expressions. The form notes the machine ("Machine 'Lathe 2': numbers without a point are increments of 0.001 mm; X is a diameter") or "No machine chosen: words whose reading depends on the machine are left alone".
- **Tests:** the spec edge cases; a lathe `Z −0.5` with `W` words reported; Okuma `X=V1+2` skipped; `Z1000` → `Z500` (IS-B), `Z999.5` (calculator), unchanged and reported with **no machine**, and `Z10.` → `Z9.5` in all three; `Z1000` + `0.0005` under IS-B → `Z1001` reported as rounded (half away from zero); under an Okuma 10 µm machine `Z1000` − 0.5 → `Z950` and `Z10.` − 0.5 → `Z-40.` (the unit scales a word with a point too); multiply by 2 on a `G83 … K3` block leaves `K3` alone (a `count` parameter, not an arc centre); a Sinumerik file with a `DIAMOF` section: a +0.5 diameter operand moves the `DIAMON` words by 0.5 and the `DIAMOF` words by 0.25.
- **Acceptance:** G10.
- **Roadmap R3, R6 and R8:** the skipped blocks are the ones whose code carries R3's frame flag or words-are-data flag, not a hard-coded `G53`/`G28` list; the incremental words follow R6's `U`/`W` choice; a drilling-cycle parameter with R8's tool-axis role moves with a shift on the tool axis, and every call P10 names as not judgeable is refused and listed, never shifted in part.
- **Covers:** "Address arithmetic"; roadmap R8 (smallest version); X8 (part); X11 (c, the arithmetic half).

### M11: Compare and search

**Goal:** a re-posted program can be compared without numbering noise and merged; any word can be found and replaced by value.

**Status (2026-10-05): on `main`; v0.5 follows (§5.3).** All of P11, WP11.1–WP11.4, H11 and I11 are in, then three reviews of the integrated branch: 13 NC findings (NC-1 to NC-13), 21 code findings (CODE-1 to CODE-21) and a skeptic pass over all 34 (33 held up, one was refuted: CODE-9). The confirmed ones are fixed, each with a regression test that fails without the fix (§7.16 #143–#146; the owner questions M11-1 to M11-5 were answered on 2026-10-07, §10.1, and M11-1 added the cycle-name option, §7.16 #148). **Left open on purpose, for later:** CODE-18's disabling of the copy buttons when there is no change at the cursor (the buttons are `aria-disabled` in review mode and for a file original; the cursor is not in a store); CODE-2's gating of the *Also in comments* field (the label says it is for text searches only); CODE-4's worker with a timeout for a regex that runs away (the guide says gEdit cannot stop one, so save first). **Runtime fixes** (the first full local run of the runtime suite found two app bugs): Find Whole Address kept the word under the cursor instead of its pattern, because Monaco seeds an empty search from the cursor word; and switching raw to review, or any review toggle, raised `Illegal value for lineNumber`, because the scratch models were refilled while the diff editor still showed them (§7.16 #147). **Gates:** `npm run check` 0 errors and 0 warnings; 5,960 unit tests (1 skipped); Python 803 tests on 3.13, 3.12 and 3.9; the build; the licenses and versions check. The runtime suite `m0`–`m11` (98 scenarios) on the GitHub runner: 98 passed, none flaky, after a first run that found two model races (§7.16 #147: the compare scratch models, and a folding or outline request that outlived its document's model; both fixed by ordering, with regression tests). What shipped, in one list: NC-aware search and replace (word queries by value as written, conditions, whole address, regular expressions, text in comments on request, all open documents, program-number references, replace in place or into a new tab with a count, Find Whole Address); compare review mode with per-profile defaults and notes; merge in both directions; export of differences; compare two files; saved options; the regex help page. The compare leftovers of TODO are all done: the review-mode defaults (P11, §8.11), program numbers in search (#136), the race that a 250 ms delay only held off (the scratch models are reused and filled while detached, and a copy waits for the diff to be current), the saved options (#137); WP8.9 was never used (D38), and its Okuma `SB=` search case is a test.

**Prelude P11:** contracts §7.6 (search) and §7.7 (compare); stubs `core/search/index.ts`, `core/compare/index.ts`; the `CompareService` additions as stubs in `app/compare.ts`; `profile.compare` typed without `tolerance` (a carried `tolerance` is ignored); the command ids and keys of §7.13 with `findConflicts` clean. **The compare and search leftovers of TODO "Ahead" land here too:** review-mode defaults for Klartext, Okuma and Sinumerik in their profiles' `compare` blocks (a Klartext cycle name is dialog-language text, not a comment, so a re-post in another language changes every cycle header — the default must say what it ignores), written here and pinned; commit.

**What P11 decided (2026-10-05; the hand-off is `SP/handoff/m11-p11.md`):**
- **Contracts (item 1).** `src/lib/core/search/types.ts` and `src/lib/core/compare/types.ts` hold §7.6 and §7.7 with six recorded changes (§7.16 #134–#139). `core/search/index.ts` and `core/compare/index.ts` export every function with its signature and throw "not implemented" until WP11.1 and WP11.2 land; nothing calls them before then. `compareDefaults` is implemented, because the golden of the defaults reads it. The rules every option obeys are written at the top of `core/compare/types.ts`, so WP11.2 does not have to find them here.
- **`CompareService` (item 2).** `mode`/`setMode`, `options`/`setOptions`, `copyChange`, `exportDiff` and `openFiles` are typed in `app/types.ts` and stubbed in `app/compare.ts`: the two stores hold what they are told, the three actions reject. **The name clash:** the P1 controller already had `options` for the raw view's two toggles (`inline`, `ignoreTrimWhitespace`). Those are now `view` (type `DiffViewOptions`, was `DiffOptions`), so `options` is §7.7's review toggles alone (§7.16 #138). `CompareView.svelte` and `app/compare.test.ts` follow the rename; nothing behaves differently. WP11.3 keeps the raw view's "Ignore whitespace" as Monaco's trim option in raw mode; in review mode the five toggles take its place.
- **Review-mode defaults (item 3; the G10 table is §8.11, the golden `src/lib/data/profiles/compareDefaults.test.ts`).** One rule decides every value: **review mode never hides a difference the machine would see.** Block numbers, whitespace and the number format are on for every profile, because the normalization keeps what the control could read apart (a referenced block number, a blank between two words, a decimal point under an increment reading). Comments are on for every profile, and each profile lists the comments its control reads in a new `compare.keepComments` (§7.16 #134): the Fanuc program title in the `O` block, an alarm or stop message (`#3000=`, `#3006=`) and a `%` inside a comment, which ends the program on input; the same `%` rule on Okuma (verify); the Sinumerik `;$PATH=` header and the cycle-screen markers `*RO*`/`*HD*`. Case is on for Sinumerik only: its control does not tell case apart outside tool names (strings, which keep their case); the Fanuc code table has no lower case and drops it on input, and Okuma and Klartext leave it open (verify). The Fanuc values P1 carried change (comments off → on, case on → off, `tolerance` gone); no feature read them before. `fanuc-lathe` inherits `fanuc-gcode`'s block and `sinumerik-mill` `sinumerik`'s through `extends`; both are pinned resolved.
- **The Klartext cycle name** (the TODO leftover). The words after `CYCL DEF <n>` and the label words of the old numbered cycles (`CYCL DEF 9.1 DWELL 1.5`) are dialog-language text the control writes, not comments; whether a control ignores them on import is open (`syntax-heidenhain.md` open question 8). P11 decided that review mode does not ignore them and that M11 adds no option before the owner's answer (§10.2 M11-1 then). **The owner answered on 2026-10-07 (§10.1): "Default no, but make it a setting."** So review mode has a sixth toggle, `ignoreCycleNames`, **off by default**, offered only by a profile that declares where its cycle names stand (`compare.cycleNames`, two patterns in `heidenhain-klartext.json`; §7.16 #148), and saved with the other toggles (`ui.lastParams.compare`, only when changed). With it off, a re-post in another dialog language shows each cycle header as changed, one line per cycle with its number visible; with it on, exactly the name and the label words go, never the cycle number, a value, an axis word, a `Q` parameter or a continuation line. The `;` labels of the parameter lines go with `ignoreComments` either way. §8.11 and the user guide say so.
- **Number format and the effective machine.** A side is normalized with its own effective profile and machine; `normalizeLines` takes the machine as an added optional parameter, because the compiled profile alone cannot tell a machine's number input from the profile's default (`applyMachine` writes the same `decimalPointSignificant` for both) (§7.16 #135). The decimal point is kept wherever **any** reading that applies is an increment reading in any class (`pointSignificant`): with a machine, its number input; with none, every preset the profile declares (`readingsOf`'s list, `core/machines/numbers.ts`). This is stricter than per-class reading (an IS-B feed `F100` against `F100.` shows as a difference although both are 100 mm/min) and never hides one; the class of a word needs the modal state, which TS does not have before Phase 3. So with no machine: Fanuc mill and lathe keep the point (IS-B and IS-C are declared), Okuma does not (all three presets read the point alike), Sinumerik does not (one `calculator` preset, §7.16 #25), Klartext does not (no presets; `decimalPointSignificant: false`).
- **Commands and keys (item 4).** §7.13's ids and keys are unchanged. Pinned in `tests/unit/commandPins.test.ts` with their titles: `search.findAll` (`Mod+Shift+F`) — `search.findAll` "Find All…", `search.replace` — `search.replace` "Replace All…", `search.wholeAddressInFind` — `search.wholeAddressInFind` "Find Whole Address…", category `search.category`, ribbon `{ tab: 'home', group: 'search.group' }` "Search" (orders 130–132, after the Edit group); `compare.copyToModified` (`Mod+Alt+Right`) — "Copy Change to Current Document", `compare.copyToOriginal` (`Mod+Alt+Left`) — "Copy Change to Original", `compare.exportDiff` — "Export Differences…", `compare.files` — "Compare Two Files…" (ribbon `{ tab: 'tools', group: 'compare.group' }`, order 11), `compare.toggleReview` — "Review Mode", category `compare.category`. The rows are marked `pending`: a prelude pins them before their WP registers them, a pending row may be missing but must match once declared, and the WP that registers it deletes the mark and adds its key to `tests/runtime/lib/shortcuts.js`. `findConflicts` over every shipped shortcut plus the pins is clean on both platforms. **Monaco 0.55.1's standalone keymap** binds neither key (its nearest are `Mod+Shift+Alt+Left/Right`, column selection, and the format action on `Shift+Alt+F`); the diff editor binds only `Escape`, `F7` and `Shift+F7`. **macOS** has no system shortcut on `Cmd+Shift+F` or `Cmd+Option+Left/Right` (Mission Control uses `Ctrl`+arrows), and the native menu's accelerators are Quit, Hide, Minimize and full screen (`Ctrl+Cmd+F`). **Windows and Linux:** `Ctrl+Alt+Left/Right` is AltGr with an arrow on some layouts (the dispatcher ignores AltGr, D21), some Windows graphics drivers rotate the screen on `Ctrl+Alt`+arrow, and some Linux desktops switch workspaces on it; the two copy commands stay in the compare toolbar and the palette, and §13 lists the check. The copy keys are "only while a comparison is open" through `enabled: (c) => c.compareOpen`; pressed in the editor with no comparison, the registry's usual "not available" status message appears, which WP11.3 may keep.
- **The saved compare options (item 5)** go into `state.json` as `ui.lastParams.compare` (`COMPARE_MEMO_KEY`, shape `CompareMemo` in `core/compare/types.ts`), not into a settings key (§7.16 #137): they are toolbar state, per profile, and the P1 per-form memory already persists with a 1 s debounce and a flush on quit. Saved per profile are only the toggles the user changed away from that profile's defaults, so a later change of a built-in default reaches every toggle the user never touched; "Profile defaults" in the toolbar deletes the entry. The mode and the raw view's two options are saved too; the first comparison of a fresh install opens raw, as in P1.
- **Test ids (item 5)** are §7.12's M11 rows; the search and replace forms' field ids are pinned there too, so H11, WP11.1 and WP11.3 agree.
- **O-number references in search** (the TODO leftover). A word query on the program-number address finds the program's own line and every call that names it: a new `CodeParam.programNumber: 'plain' | 'packed'` on the Fanuc `G65 P`, `G66 P`, `G66.1 P` (plain) and `M98 P` (packed: `M98 P52000` is five times `O2000`, so it is found under `O2000` and under `O52000`; `syntax-fanuc.md` §7), inherited by both lathe databases; golden `src/lib/data/codes/programNumbers.test.ts` (§7.16 #136). Replace never follows a reference (cross-file references are §11 item 15). The §7.6 example regex misses `G1.`, which its own sentence says must match; the corrected form is in `core/search/index.ts`.
- **What a WP needs to start (item 6).** Search tokenizes with `tokenizeLine(line, cp, prev)` (`core/nc/tokenizer.ts`; carry `state` for Klartext `~`) and masks comments for text queries with `maskComments(line, cp)` (`core/nc/mask.ts`: comments blanked, strings kept, same offsets). Values are `NcToken.value` (`NumericLiteral`, `core/nc/types.ts`) compared as decimal text, never as JS numbers. The effective view of a document is `machines.effective(docId)` → `{ profile, cp, codes, machine }` (`stores/machines.ts`, §7.15); the readings come from `readingsOf` / `resolveValue` / `valueOf` in `core/machines/numbers.ts` and the presets from `cp.profile.machineParams.numberInput.presets`. Block-number references come from `referencesOn` / `referenceAddresses` / `blockKeyOf` / `comparesByText` (`core/transforms/references.ts`); the comment-header rule is `removeComments.ts`'s, and `keepComments` covers it. The tool word's place is `cp.profile.addresses.tool`, the machine type `cp.profile.machineType`. A Results row may carry its own `docId` (§7.16 #139).
- **Owns (Wave A and B), confirmed disjoint with four additions:** WP11.1 also owns `src/lib/components/panels/ResultsPanel.svelte` and its test (the row `docId`), its row in `src/lib/contrib/README.md`, the `search.findAll` row of `tests/runtime/lib/shortcuts.js` and the `pending` marks of its three pins; WP11.2 also owns one added export of `src/lib/core/transforms/lineDiff.ts` (the Myers diff itself, without the same-length shortcut that `computeLineEdits` takes first, which would turn an insertion plus a deletion into a run of changed lines); WP11.3 also owns `src/lib/app/compare.test.ts`, the two compare rows of `tests/runtime/lib/shortcuts.js` and the `pending` marks of its five pins (Wave B, after WP11.1); H11 owns the rest of `tests/runtime/**`, `m2-compare` included. **Nobody in M11 owns** the profiles, the code databases, `core/{profiles,codes,nc,machines}/**`, `app/types.ts` or `stores/**`: a WP that needs a change there writes a hand-off note for I11.

**Intentional behavior changes in M11:** the compare toolbar gains controls (H11 updates `m2-compare` for exactly that). From P11: none visible — the review-mode defaults and `keepComments` are data no feature reads before WP11.2; the resolved fixtures (`tests/fixtures/resolved/**`) gain the `compare` blocks and the `programNumber` members, regenerated and named. From WP11.3: a temporary model that review mode or a file comparison creates may be reused across comparisons instead of disposed (the deterministic fix for the 250 ms margin in `monaco/diff.ts`), which changes what `m2-compare` may count as "no temporary model left behind".

**Wave A**
