### M12: Multi-channel programs

**Goal:** on the owner's twin-turret and sub-spindle machines, gEdit knows which channel the owner is looking at — whether the post writes the channels as sections of one file or as one file per channel — shows the map and the tool list per channel, walks from one wait to the next, and tells the owner before the program goes to the machine which sync ids do not match between the channels. They define their machines' channels and wait codes once, in the machine configuration, in their own patterns; gEdit ships no sync code set of its own (AD-32, D58).

**Status (2026-10-08): on `main` (`fc9cccb`); the v0.6 draft follows (§5.3).** The prelude P12, WP12.1–WP12.7, the tester, H12 and I12 are in. **Prelude decisions:** the owner chose on 2026-10-07 to start before D64 with documented defaults per control, marked `verify`, as machine settings and presets (M12-a), and to define the wait codes as a plain list per machine, such as `M100-M199, M300`, with a live preview, a `P` form dropdown, a checkbox for stops and ends, and a regular expression only as the Advanced form (M12-b, the owner's wait-code design; §7.16 #149–#157, §8.9). **Reviews of the integrated branch:** 15 NC findings (4 major, 4 minor, 7 nit) and 19 code findings (1 high, 5 medium, 9 low, 4 info), then a skeptic pass over all 34: 27 held up (8 of them with a lower severity), 5 were only plausible, 2 were not defects, and 4 sub-claims were refuted; no blocker, and after the pass 2 NC findings stayed major (NC-01, NC-02) and 3 code findings medium (CODE-1, CODE-2, CODE-4). **Two fix batches** (§7.16 #167), each fix with a regression test that fails without it: the NC batch (a Fanuc wait whose paths name different path sets; a Sinumerik `SETM` that answers a `WAITM`; a count difference inside a loop is only information; channel numbers read by value; a variable `WAITM` mark; `M 900`; one finding per mark for an unknown channel; the Okuma and Fanuc presets) and the code batch (a guard against regular expressions that can run away, with a line cap of 1,000 characters and a deadline on every line; a machine block without `syncMarks` opens; the loop lines the map and the check read; `{{stem}}` in the file-name template; a fresh channel set for scripts and navigation; the sibling lookup after Save As; a keyed list in the forms; the tester on a 300 ms timer; `..` refused in the sibling lookup). **Integration round 2** found one more bug through a test written for NC-04: a label on the wait's own line (`N10 M901 … GOTO10`) was not counted as a loop, so the count difference was reported as an error (§7.16 #168). **Runtime scenarios** (H12): `m12-channels-config`, `-single`, `-multi-1`, `-multi-2`, `-check`, `-nav`, `-off` and `-perf` (23 checks); the suite is now `m0`–`m12`, 106 scenarios; `m6-machines-manage-1` changed for the new Channels step. The first full local run passed 104 of 106; the two failures were `m12-perf` (four budgets) and `m1-perf-open` (load noise, 3 of 3 alone). **Performance fixes** (§7.16 #169), found by `m12-perf` on a 300,009-line program with 20,000 waits: the program map listed every wait as a row (20,109 rows in the page), and a regular expression with a lookbehind runs in a slow engine mode on WebKit. Now the map shows at most 250 wait rows per channel, then one row with the count (111 rows), unchanged rows are reused, the edited lines patch the last resolution instead of a full one, and a pattern with a lookbehind is pre-filtered by the same pattern without it. Before and after: the grouped map after an edit 6.9–14.4 s to 0.17 s (budget 0.2 s); first resolution 237–261 ms to 68–94 ms (budget 150 ms); typing, 95th percentile drawn, 64–98 ms to 17 ms (the same as without channels); resolution in the engine 219 ms to 35 ms; `m12-perf` itself 5–7.5 min to 135 s. An abandoned resolution is no longer silent: the status item says "Channels: too slow to read", the service tries once more after a second, and the check says "not checked". **Left open on purpose, for later:** a Worker with a timeout for the user's own patterns (the guard and the line cap make a runaway unlikely, they do not make it impossible); NC-05 (a wait code written as an argument of a macro call, `G65 P9010 M901`, reads as a wait; the profile has no list of macro-call codes to read); NC-15 (it needs one sentence from a manual); NC-09 and NC-14 (deferred by the skeptic); a jump from a `WAITM` to the `SETM` that answers it; and the file-name template of the extension-less path-1 file, which waits for D64. **Owner:** D64 is now data: their codes per machine, the `P` form, and how the files are named, including the extension-less path-1 file; D65 keeps its default (no check while typing); M12-1 to M12-5 are recorded with their recommended defaults (§10.1). **Gates:** `npm run check` 0 errors and 0 warnings; 6,407 unit tests (1 skipped); Python 833 tests on 3.13, 3.12 and 3.9; 286 Rust tests (fmt and clippy clean); the build, the licenses and versions check. The runtime suite `m0`–`m12` (106 scenarios) on the GitHub runner: 106 of 106 on `main` (run 37739910357; the owner chose to land without the throwaway-branch run).

**Placement (D63, renumbered by D69).** M12 sits after M10 and M11 because it needs everything before it and nothing after it: the machine configuration, its file, its validation, its page and the per-document effective view (M6); the per-file machine choice, the session and the recovery that bring two channel documents back together (M7); the Okuma and Sinumerik profiles, since two of the owner's three multi-channel machines speak them (M8); the values read right (M9); and M10's Results conventions and check vocabulary, next to which the wait-code check belongs. M13 (user profiles and the exit) neither needs it nor is needed by it, except that the exit run checks X12 last. The owner has not yet named the wait codes the owner's machines use (D64), and the design deliberately does not wait for them; R5 (below) is the one open decision it needs before it starts.

**Prelude P12:**
1. Contracts **§7.17** (`src/lib/core/channels/types.ts`: `ChannelRef`, `ChannelSection`, `ChannelMember`, `ChannelSet`, `ChannelProblem`, `SyncRule`, `SyncSemantics`, `SyncHit`, `SyncFinding`, `ChannelParams`; in `app/types.ts` — the one file P12 alone edits — `ChannelService`, the `DialogService.openFiles` `defaultPath?` and `FileMemo.channelId?` of §7.14; `MachineParams.channels` and `MachineParamsDecl.channels` in `core/machines/types.ts` and `core/profiles/types.ts`; `ScriptContextV2.channels` and `BuildContextInput.channels` in `core/scripting/types.ts`; `channelSiblings` in `platform/commands.ts`).
2. `core/profiles/types.ts`: `OutlineKind` gains `'channel'` and `'sync'`, **with the rule that a profile's own `outline` rules may not use them** (they are produced by the channel service, never by a pattern); `core/profiles/validate.ts` rejects them with a JSON path. Every exhaustive `switch` over `OutlineKind` is made to handle both, the mechanical change M8 made for `TokenKind` (§7.14).
3. **Implement** (§5.2 rule 2), with unit tests, the small pure functions every Wave A WP depends on: `core/channels/resolve.ts` (`findSections`, `siblingNames`, `channelAt`) and `core/channels/marks.ts` (`findMarks`). WP12.1 owns them from Wave A on.
4. Stubs: `core/channels/check.ts` (`checkSyncMarks` → no findings), `stores/channels.ts` (a `ChannelService` whose `forDoc` always answers `layout: 'none'`), and the call in `src/lib/app/bootstrap.ts` — `channels.start()` after `machines.load(configLoad)` — wired here, because the prelude is the only WP that edits `bootstrap.ts` (P1 §4.2). **With the stub every P1 and M6–M11 unit, Python and harness test passes unchanged**, which is the tripwire for the map and status-bar changes of WP12.5.
5. `core/machines/types.ts`: `channels?: ChannelParams` inside `MachineParams`, additive; `MACHINES_VERSION` stays **1** (D59, §4 "Persistence deltas"). `core/machines/file.ts` already keeps unknown members, so a file written by an M12 build round-trips through an M6 build unchanged; `core/machines/validate.ts` accepts the block and reports it as unvalidated until WP12.3.
6. Rust stub `src-tauri/src/channels.rs` (`channel_siblings`, §7.10), registered under the single `generate_handler![` (P1 AD-15); the `platform/commands.ts` wrapper.
7. Python stub `src-tauri/resources/scripts/_nc_channels.py` with the §7.17 signatures, re-exported by the `gedit_nc` facade (F37); `tests/python/helpers.py` gains `channel_context(golden=…)`, which reads the generated context of a golden exactly as `effective_context` does (P6 item 12), so Python never resolves channels itself.
8. Test ids of §7.12, the command ids and keys of §7.13 with `findConflicts` clean, `h.app.ctx.channels`, and `tests/fixtures/channels/README.md` with the golden format; commit.

**Roadmap R5, smallest version, in the same prelude** (accepted 2026-10-01, §10.1): in §7.17, a `decode` choice on the "partners from the line" rule (`split`, `digits` with 0 as channel 10, `bitmask`) and the partners of a mark with no `P` (`whenAbsent`), because the owner's 3-path set is a Fanuc lathe program whose waits carry digit `P` words, and the current rule would report every one of them as naming an unknown channel. The rest of R5 (an "alone in its block" rule, a "required on" pattern, a finding for two marks whose partner sets disagree, `WAITE` as an end-of-program wait that is never counted, `SETM`/`CLEARM` non-blocking, program checks and extents per channel) comes as data-only options if the owner accepts it. The `c02` fixture is rewritten in the real shapes (§9.1). R5 was accepted on 2026-10-01, so P12 ships `decode` and `whenAbsent`; `split` alone, with a user-guide note that a digit or bit-sum `P` word is not read, was the fallback while it was open.

**What P12 decided (2026-10-07; the hand-off is `SP/handoff/m12-p12.md`; the owner's decisions of that day are in §10.1, M12-a and M12-b):**
- **Contracts (item 1).** `src/lib/core/channels/types.ts` holds §7.17 with R5 and the owner's plain forms; it is the binding text (§7.16 #149–#157). `ChannelService` is in `app/types.ts` (with `AppContext.channels`, `DialogService.openFiles({ defaultPath })` and `OutlineService.toolLines(id, channelId?)`); `FileMemo.channelId` was already there (M7). `ScriptContextV2.channels` (`ScriptChannels`) and `BuildContextInput.channels` are in `core/scripting/types.ts`; `channelSiblings` and `SiblingInfo` (with `error`, #152) in `platform/commands.ts`.
- **`MachineParams.channels` (item 5).** Stored in the record and **never in the effective params** (#149), so `effectiveKey`, `applyMachine` and every M6–M11 golden are unchanged. `core/machines/validate.ts` gains `validateChannels` (the §7.17 rules; stub `[]` until WP12.3) and `channelBlock` (`absent` / `valid` / `invalid`; a present block answers "not checked by this build yet" until WP12.3). `validateMachine` never reports the block, so a broken one costs the channels and never the record (X12 d). `validateProfile` runs `validateChannels` over every built-in preset, so a preset can never be a block the Machines page would refuse.
- **R5 (accepted 2026-10-01), as built.** `decode` = `split` (default) / `digits` / `bitmask`, `whenAbsent` = `none` (default) / `all` / `fixed`, on a `line` rule and on the new `word` rule (#155). `digits` reads each digit as a path number, `0` as path 10, skips a leading `0` (the control does), order free; `bitmask` reads bit *n−1* as path *n*. A decoded number is matched as its text against ids and aliases, so paths named `1`, `2`, `3` need nothing else. The partner-set comparison (`samePartners`) and `SETM` answering a `WAITM` (`answers`) are built after the review (§7.16 #167) as rule flags; the rest of R5 (an "alone in its block" rule, a "required on" pattern, `WAITE`, program checks and extents per channel) stays data-only and unbuilt.
- **The owner's plain forms (M12-b).** `SyncRule.match` `{ kind: 'codes', codes: 'M100-M199, M300' }` is the primary form: `parseWaitCodes(text, { letters })` → `{ ranges, errors }` with plain messages that quote the item (`channels.codes.*`: not a number, backwards, letter mismatch, wrong letter for this control, too large, no letter, empty, too many); `describeWaitCodes(ranges)` → `{ text: 'M100 … M199, M300', count: 101 }` for the live preview; a word matches when its letter is in the list, it is not preceded by a letter, and it has no decimal point (`M0101` = `M101`; `M101.5` and the `M1` of `M1001` are not). `partners` `{ kind: 'word', address: 'P', decode, whenAbsent }` is the dropdown. `ChannelParams.stopsAndEndsWait` is the checkbox: the profile's `stop`/`end` outline lines become blocking id-less marks of every channel under `STOPS_AND_ENDS_RULE`. The control's allowed letters are `machineParams.channels.waitLetters`. `regex` and `line` remain, as the Advanced forms.
- **Defaults per control (M12-a; the G10 table is §8.9, pinned in `src/lib/data/profiles/channelPresets.test.ts`).** `fanuc-lathe`: `fanuc-2path`, `fanuc-3path-digits`, `fanuc-3path-bitmask` (one file per path, the path number at the end of the extension; waits `M900-M999` from the owner's local multi-path set, aggregate; `P` per parameter 8103 bit 1). `okuma-osp`: `okuma-2turret` (`G13`/`G14` sections as aliases, `P1-P9999` **ordered**, `M100` **counted**). `sinumerik` (and `sinumerik-mill` by `extends`): `sinumerik-2channel` (one program per channel tied by hand, `WAITM`/`WAITMC` rendezvous through split partners, `SETM`/`CLEARM` non-blocking, `WAITE` not a mark). Every preset `verify: true` with its source, offered on the Channels step, never applied without a machine. `fanuc-gcode` and Klartext declare none. This reverses §8.9's "no preset" (#154); `fanucLathe.test.ts`, `okuma.test.ts` and `sinumerik.test.ts` now assert `verify` instead of absence. The resolved fixtures of the three profiles (and their effective variants) were regenerated.
- **`OutlineKind` (item 2).** `channel` and `sync` added; `validateProfile` refuses them in a profile's outline with their own message; `monaco/providers/symbols.ts` (the one exhaustive `Record`) maps them to Namespace and Event; the map's `kindLabel` names them (`programMap.kinds.channel`, `.sync`); the icons are WP12.5's.
- **Implemented (item 3), with tests:** `core/channels/resolve.ts` (`findSections`, `channelAt`, `markerChannel`, and the shared `channelRefs`, `resolveChannelToken`, `splitChannelTokens`, `compileChannelPattern`, `complementRanges`, `budgetClock`), `core/channels/marks.ts` (`findMarks`, `decodePartnerText`, `maskForMarks`), `core/channels/siblings.ts` (`siblingNames`, `fileChannel`, `fillTemplate`), `core/channels/codes.ts` (`parseWaitCodes`, `describeWaitCodes`, `mergeRanges`, `isWaitCode`, `waitCodeWordRe`). The section end line belongs to the section; adjacent ranges merge; a start that names an undeclared channel is a problem and its lines are `outside`; `marker` reads raw lines (#151); a mark is found on the masked line with strings blanked too (`MSG("WAITM(…)")` is no mark); one mark per line, first rule wins; 20,000 marks kept and the rest counted.
- **Stubs (items 4, 6, 7).** `core/channels/check.ts` (no findings; its header lists what WP12.2 must know), `stores/channels.ts` (every document `layout: 'none'`; `noChannels()`), `bootstrap.ts` `startChannels` right after the machines (#157), `src-tauri/src/channels.rs` (`SiblingInfo`, `MAX_NAMES`, `MAX_NAME_CHARS`; refuses every call until WP12.4) registered under the one `generate_handler![`, `_nc_channels.py` re-exported by `gedit_nc` (every function answers empty), `tests/python/helpers.py` `channel_context(golden=…)` reading `resolved/channels/index.json` (the generator entry comes with WP12.6), `tests/unit/helpers/profiles.ts` (`profileOf`, `cpOf` for tests).
- **Commands and keys (item 8).** §7.13's ids and keys are unchanged and pinned `pending` (§7.13 P12 paragraph). `Mod+Alt+P` needs Monaco's macOS "toggle preserve case" removed (#153). The check goes into `channels.toolsGroup` on the Tools tab (#156). The stub `src/lib/i18n/en/channels.ts` holds the titles, groups, category, the resolution problems and the wait-code list messages. Test ids are §7.12's M12 rows, unchanged; `h.app.ctx.channels` exists (`context.test.ts` M12 row). `tests/fixtures/channels/README.md` holds the golden format, with the new rule kinds.
- **What a WP builds against.** WP12.1: the functions above (it owns them now) and the fixtures of §9.1 — **`c02` in the real shapes**: wait `M9xx` codes with digit `P` words, packed and spaced, one `P`-less wait, as the three-path preset reads them; the M12 fixtures of §9.1 that say `M130`/`M131` may use any `codes` range. WP12.2: `checkSyncMarks(perChannel, p, o)`; `SyncHit.partners` may contain the mark's own channel and may be empty; `STOPS_AND_ENDS_RULE` marks are `count` with `all` partners; ids compare by digit value when both are all digits (`M0101` = `M101`). WP12.3: `validateChannels` / `channelBlock` (fill them; the preset test then also proves every preset valid), `parseWaitCodes` / `describeWaitCodes` for the field, `channelFields`; its i18n keys go into `machines.ts` (namespace `machines.channels.*`), not `channels.ts`. WP12.4: the `channels.rs` doc comment is the checklist. WP12.5: `channelBlock`, `findSections`, `fileChannel`, `siblingNames`, `markerChannel`, `findMarks`, `checkSyncMarks`, `channelSiblings`, `fileMemory` `channelId`; the keybinding removal of #153; the ribbon groups of §7.13. WP12.6: `ScriptChannels`, `BuildContextInput.channels`, and the `resolved/channels/**` generator in `tests/unit/resolved.test.ts`.
- **Owns (Wave A and B), confirmed disjoint with these additions:** WP12.1 also owns `src/lib/core/channels/codes.ts` and its test; WP12.2 also owns `src/lib/core/channels/check.test.ts`; WP12.3 also owns `src/lib/data/profiles/channelPresets.test.ts` (it adds "every preset validates clean") and, for a preset value the review changes, the `machineParams.channels` blocks of `fanuc-lathe.json`, `okuma-osp.json`, `sinumerik.json` with the resolved fixtures they regenerate; WP12.5 also owns `src/lib/stores/channels.test.ts`, the `pending` marks of its eight pins and their rows in `tests/runtime/lib/shortcuts.js`, `src/lib/contrib/README.md`'s row for `channels.ts`, and `src/lib/monaco/providers/symbols.ts` should it change the two kinds; WP12.6 (Wave B) also owns `tests/unit/resolved.test.ts` (the channel entries) and `tests/python/helpers.py` `channel_context`. **Nobody in M12 owns** `app/types.ts`, `bootstrap.ts`, `core/profiles/**` (but the three presets above) or `core/channels/types.ts`: a WP that needs a change there writes a hand-off note for I12.

**Intentional behavior changes in M12** (H12 may update earlier scenarios and goldens only for these):
- A document whose effective machine has a **valid** `channels` block gains a channel status item, and its program map groups its rows under one `channel` row per channel (each carrying the items of all of that channel's ranges) with the blocking sync marks as `sync` rows, plus one "Outside the channels" group. **Without such a machine nothing changes** (X12 c): no item, no grouping, the P1 map. A machine whose channel block is broken behaves like a machine without one, except for the problem shown on the Machines page and in the status item; nothing else about that machine changes (X12 d). H12 updates the M6 scenarios that count status items.
- `outline.items(id)` may return `channel` and `sync` rows; `OutlineIndex` itself is unchanged, so every committed outline golden stays as it is.
- The Tools tab gains "Check wait codes" next to the M10 checks; the NC tab gains the channel navigation group.

**Wave A**

#### WP12.1 Channel resolution and the channel fixtures

- **Owns:**
  - `src/lib/core/channels/{resolve,marks,siblings,codes}.ts` and their tests (P12 wrote all four; WP12.1 owns them from Wave A on)
  - `tests/fixtures/nc/channels/**`, `tests/fixtures/expected/detect/channels.json`, `tests/fixtures/expected/outline/channels/**`, `tests/fixtures/channels/resolve/**`, `tests/fixtures/README.md`
- **Depends on:** P12.
- **Deliver:**
  - `findSections(lines, cp, params)`: the sections of a `single-file` document with, per channel, **every** 1-based inclusive line range it holds, in document order (a section ends at `sectionEnd`, else at the next start, else at the last line). A channel may start a section any number of times and the sections may interleave — a turret-selection program alternates `G13 … G14 … G13 … G14 …` and the control separates it the same way — so "a channel appears twice" is **not** a problem: it is a row in the tester ("channel 1: 4 sections, 212 lines") and nothing more. The `channel` capture resolves against ids **and aliases** and may name several channels at once when it contains `sectionSeparator` (`+S1/S3/S4`, one common section run by three processes): the range is then listed under each of them, through the same splitting code path as a `line` partner capture. Every line no channel owns — before the first section, between a `sectionEnd` and the next start, after the last section — goes into `outside`, in document order. Problems: a start naming an undeclared channel or alias, a declared channel that is nowhere, overlapping ranges.
  - `siblingNames(baseName, params)`: the file names of the other channels of a `multi-file` document — pure, no I/O (F57). Per channel, its own `list[].fileName` template if it has one, else `fileNameFor`, else the `fileName` pattern with the `channel` capture replaced; so a set whose members share no one pattern (`%_N_1000_MPF` / `%_N_2000_MPF`; `PART.MPF` / `PART_GS.MPF`, where one name carries no channel token at all) is expressible, and a template that cannot be derived is reported rather than guessed. A base name that matches nothing answers `null` ("this is not a channel file") — which is not the end of the story, because the user may still assign the document to a channel by hand (WP12.5). Name comparison is case-insensitive on macOS and Windows, as `docs.byPath` already is.
  - `markerChannel(lines, params)`: the channel a `multi-file` document says it is, from the optional `marker` pattern over the first 400 masked lines, resolved against ids and aliases; it wins over the file name when both answer, and a disagreement is a problem, not a silent choice. For a set whose names carry no channel token it is the only automatic answer.
  - `findMarks(lines, cp, params, channel)`: the sync marks of a channel's lines, on **masked** lines only (a mark inside a comment is not a mark), by `prefix` (a literal prefix plus the id as `idDigits` digits at a word boundary) or by `regex` (a `mark` capture; absent and not required for a `count` rule, whose marks carry `mark: ''`), with the partner channels resolved from `all`, `fixed` or `line` (the `channels` capture split on the rule's separator, each piece matched case-insensitively against a declared channel **id or alias**; an unmatched piece is kept as `unknown` for the check to report). A hit on a line no section owns keeps `channel: ''` and is reported by the check as "outside every channel", never keyed to a channel. Rules run in declaration order and the first rule that matches a line owns it.
  - Caps and budgets: ≤ 32 channels, ≤ 32 sync rules, ≤ 20,000 marks per document (the rest counted and reported, never dropped silently), each pattern ≤ 1,000 characters and inside the P1 AD-11 subset (standing rule 15); a resolution that passes 500 ms on one document is abandoned with a status message and `layout: 'none'`, so a pathological pattern cannot wedge the editor.
  - The synthetic fixtures of §9.1 (M12 rows) with their detection and outline goldens (spec goldens where WP12.5 has not landed, §5.2 rule 3) and their README provenance lines.
- **Tests:** the section table (a start with and without a `channel` capture; an explicit `sectionEnd`; lines before the first section; a section that runs to the end; an undeclared channel; a missing channel; a document with no match → `none`); **four alternating sections** (`G13`/`G14`/`G13`/`G14`) give two channels with two ranges each in document order, and `channelAt` answers correctly in the second range of each; a channel whose ranges are **not contiguous** with a `sectionEnd` between them puts the lines in between into `outside`, and a shared subprogram after the last `sectionEnd` is in `outside` too; a `channel` capture naming three channels through `sectionSeparator` lists the range under each; aliases resolve a section start (`G13` → channel `1`), a marker and a `line` partner piece, and an alias colliding with another channel's id is rejected at validation; sibling names (a shared `fileName`, a per-channel `list[].fileName`, a pair where one name has no channel token, no match, a stem with a dot, a three-channel set, case folding, a template that cannot be derived → reported); marker wins over the file name and a disagreement is reported; marks (prefix with and without `idDigits`, a regex without a `mark` capture rejected at validation **except for a `count` rule**, a mark in a comment ignored, a mark outside every section keeps `channel: ''`, `all`/`fixed`/`line` partners, an unknown channel in a `line` capture, two rules matching one line → the first wins); the caps and the 500 ms abandon; 300k lines with two rules resolved in ≤ 150 ms in node (assertion < 500 ms).
- **Acceptance:** G10 on the fixtures and their goldens.
- **Covers:** AD-32 (resolution); X12 (a, b).

#### WP12.2 The wait-code check

- **Owns:** `src/lib/core/channels/check.ts` and its test, `tests/fixtures/channels/check/**`.
- **Depends on:** P12.
- **Deliver:** `checkSyncMarks(perChannel, params, o)` → `SyncFinding[]`, pure, deterministic, sorted by channel then line. It compares **sequences, not sets**: a repeated id is normal in a correct program (the Siemens two-channel shape waits on the same id three times in each channel), so the id set of a channel says nothing on its own. Per sync rule and per ordered channel pair, the §7.17 algorithm — one pass into id → positions, ordinal pairing, one linear diff over the two id sequences — produces:
  - **count mismatch** — the two channels carry a different number of this id ("channel 2 has this wait twice, channel 1 three times"). For a `count` rule (no id at all) this is the whole check, and it is the documented Okuma `M100` rule: the same number of them must appear on both turret sides.
  - **missing** — a mark whose **ordinal** partner is absent (the 3rd `80` of channel 1 against a channel 2 that has two). Reported on the line of the unpaired mark.
  - **out of order** — the two ordered id sequences of a channel pair disagree, found by one linear diff, not by scanning id pairs. Reported once per channel pair with both lines on both sides; at most one inversion per pair plus the count of the rest.
  - **not increasing** — an `ordered` rule only: an id smaller than the previous one in the **same** channel. Such a rule never compares the two channels, so an id present on one side only is legal — the Okuma manual's own correct example runs P10/P20/P40 against P10/P30/P40, and `missing`/`unmatched` on it would be a false finding on a textbook program.
  - **unknown channel** — a mark's partner set names a channel the machine does not declare. Only a `line` partner rule can produce it: `all` and `fixed` resolve inside the declared list by construction.
  - **unmatched** — a blocking `rendezvous` mark whose partner set names only its own channel. **Precedence:** a mark that already produced a `missing` or `count-mismatch` finding is not also reported as unmatched, so one defect gives one finding.
  - **outside channels** — a `SyncHit` with `channel: ''` (§7.17): reported as "a wait outside every channel", never silently keyed to a channel.
  - **Order not checked** — the order of a pair is reported as not checked, with the reason in the finding, when a **jump target or a backward jump** lies between the two marks: a label that some `GOTO`/`GOTOB`/`GOTOF`/`M99 P`/`M98 Q` in the same channel names, or the line of a backward jump. **A label alone is not one**: in a real Siemens program every wait line carries its own label (`NN8010: WAITM(10,…)`), so suspending on any label would turn the check off for the whole program — the rule is a *target*, not a label, and a label on the mark line itself is ignored. `o.jumpLines` carries the target and backward-jump lines per channel; WP12.5 computes them from the outline and the profile's `numbering.references`.
  - Two ids are the same id when their text is the same and, when both are all digits, when their values are (`101` = `0101`); nothing else is normalized, and findings quote the ids as they are written (§7.17).
  - `blocking: false` rules are **never** checked (D60); their marks are carried for the map and the navigation only.
  - Findings carry `document` and `line` (F58), so one report jumps across two channel documents. Past the §7.17 budget the report is truncated and says so.
- **Tests:** a golden per kind on two- and three-channel inputs; **a clean pair produces no finding**, including a pair in the shape of a real two-channel program — a label on every wait line, one id repeated twice and another three times in *both* channels, same counts and same order — which the set-based draft would have reported eight times; a `count` rule with equal counts is clean and with unequal counts gives exactly one finding; an `ordered` rule with ids present on one side only (P10/P20/P40 against P10/P30/P40) is clean, and one decreasing id gives exactly one finding; a jump **target** between two ids suspends exactly that pair and nothing else, while a plain label between them (and a label on the mark line) suspends nothing; a mark that is `missing` is not also `unmatched`; `unknown-channel` only from a `line` partner rule; determinism (the same input twice, and the channels in the other order, give byte-identical findings); an id present in three channels where one is missing; **performance at the contract cap**: 20,000 marks over 8 channels and over 32 channels within the §7.17 budget in node (the old 2,000-over-three measurement stays as the typical case).
- **Acceptance:** G10 (what the check claims and what it does not).
- **Covers:** AD-32 (check); X12 (a, b).

#### WP12.3 Channel parameters in the machine configuration

- **Owns:**
  - `src/lib/core/machines/validate.ts` (the `channels` rules) and its test, `src/lib/core/machines/channelFields.ts` and its test
  - `src/lib/components/dialogs/ChannelsForm.svelte` and its test
  - `src/lib/i18n/en/machines.ts`, `tests/fixtures/machines/files/channels-{valid,bad-pattern,unknown-channel,custom}.json`
- **Depends on:** P12.
- **Deliver:**
  - Validation of a `channels` block against §7.17, every problem with a JSON path: at least two channels, ids `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,15}$`, unique ignoring case, names 1–32 characters; **aliases** ≤ 8 per channel, 1–32 characters each, unique across the whole list ignoring case and never equal to another channel's id; `layout` one of `single-file` / `multi-file`; the patterns the layout needs are present and the others are not; every pattern compiles, is ≤ 1,000 characters and passes the P1 AD-11 subset scan (standing rule 15); a `single-file` `sectionStart` without a `channel` capture is allowed only when the channel count equals the number of starts it is expected to find — otherwise it must capture; `sectionSeparator` is a single non-word character; a `multi-file` set is addressable — a `fileName` with both `stem` and `channel`, **or** a `list[].fileName` template for every channel, **or** a `marker`, and when none is given the block is still valid but the page says that the channels can only be assigned by hand; a `{{…}}` template uses `{{stem}}` and `{{channel}}` only; every sync rule has a unique id, `semantics` one of `rendezvous` / `count` / `ordered` (default `rendezvous`), a prefix or a compiling regex **with a `mark` capture — required except for `count`, which has no id**, partners that resolve (`fixed` names declared channels or aliases; `line` has a `channels` capture), and `blocking` defaults to `true`. **A problem invalidates the block, not the record:** the block is kept verbatim, listed on the page with its JSON path, dropped to `layout: 'none'`, and the machine stays selectable with every other parameter in force (§7.15, X12 d).
  - **The owner's plain forms (2026-10-07, §10.1 M12-b; P12 contracts §7.16 #154):** the rule form leads with **"Wait codes"**, a text field for the `codes` list (`M100-M199, M300 M350`), validated as the user types with `parseWaitCodes` and the profile's `waitLetters` (each error quotes the item in plain words) and a live preview from `describeWaitCodes` ("M100 … M199, M300 — 101 codes"); **"Channels named by"** as a `choice` — every channel / these channels / the `P` word as path numbers ("`P12` = channels 1 and 2", `0` = channel 10) / the `P` word as a bit sum ("`P3` = channels 1 and 2 (1 + 2)") — with "a wait without `P` means" as a second `choice` (no partner / all channels / channels 1 and 2); **"Stops and ends count as waits"** as a `bool` on the layout step; an **Advanced** disclosure (closed by default, the regex help page linked) for the `regex` match, the `line` partners and the section, file-name and marker patterns; **"Start from a preset"** offering the profile's `machineParams.channels.presets` with their labels and the "verify" marker, copying the whole preset into the machine.
  - `channelFields(params)`: the `FieldSpec[]` for one sync rule (including `semantics` as a `choice` whose labels say what each kind checks) and for the layout step, in existing `FieldType`s only (`text`, `choice`, `integer`, `bool`) — **no new `FieldType`** (F46).
  - The **Channels step** of the machine form, a page-owned control modelled on the `scripts.folders` list (F46): the channel list (add, remove, rename, reorder — the order is the display order — aliases and the optional per-channel file template), the layout choice with only its own patterns shown, and the sync-rule list, each rule edited through `modals.form`.
  - The **tester**, the profile pattern tester's shape (D40) one dialog closer: a paste box, or "Take the active document", and a live table of what the rules matched — each channel with **how many sections it has and which ranges** (a channel with four sections is a row, not a warning), the lines that belong to no channel, the marks with their ids, their rule's semantics and their partner channels, the problems, and the time each rule took, with a warning above 50 ms. It runs the same pure functions as the app, never a second implementation. The same report is available outside the dialog as `channels.testOnDocument` in the Results panel, like `profile.testOnDocument`.
- **Tests:** each validation rule with one failing case; an alias that collides with another channel's id is rejected; a `count` rule without a `mark` capture is accepted and a `rendezvous` rule without one is rejected; a `multi-file` block with neither `fileName` nor templates nor `marker` is valid and flagged as assign-only; a block with a bad pattern round-trips byte-stable through parse and serialize, **is reported, and the record is still offered for a document with its other parameters unchanged**; `channelFields` types; the form steps with a fake `MachineService`; the tester's table on inline text, including a four-section channel, a rule that matches nothing and a rule over the time warning.
- **Covers:** AD-32 (configuration); X12 (d); "Full settings dialog" (the Machines page gains its Channels step).

#### WP12.4 Rust: the sibling lookup

- **Owns:** `src-tauri/src/channels.rs` and its tests.
- **Depends on:** P12.
- **Deliver:** `channel_siblings(path, names)` per §7.10 and the §4 row: `fs_scope().is_allowed(path)` or `Err`; ≤ 32 names per call, each validated exactly as the §4 row states it — **a plain file name, 1–255 characters, no path separator, no `:`, no wildcard (`*`, `?`), no control character, not `.` and not `..`** — or that entry is `Err` on its own; each resolved against the **parent of `path`**; the answer is `{ name, exists, bytes, modified }` — no content, no new grant, no directory listing (standing rule 14). The `:` and the wildcards are not decoration: on Windows `a:b.nc` is an alternate data stream and `C:x.nc` is a drive-relative path, and a wildcard is a name no file has but some APIs expand.
- **Tests (cargo):** a not-allowed path refused; the whole §4 character set, one case each — `/`, `\`, `..`, `.`, a NUL, `a:b.nc`, `a*.nc`, `a?.nc`, a name holding `\x01`, an empty name, a 256-character name; more than 32 names refused; an existing and a missing sibling; a symlinked sibling reported as it is and never followed out of the folder; the command grants nothing (the scope is unchanged afterwards).
- **Covers:** AD-32 (sibling lookup); X12 (b).

#### WP12.5 The channel service, the status item, the map and the navigation

- **Owns:**
  - `src/lib/stores/channels.ts` and its test
  - `src/lib/contrib/channels.ts` and its test, `src/lib/components/status/ChannelStatus.svelte`
  - `src/lib/app/outlineService.ts` and its test, `src/lib/components/panels/ProgramMapPanel.svelte`
  - `src/lib/app/dialogs.ts` and its test (the one-line `defaultPath` pass-through of the §7.14 `DialogService` row; the interface change itself is P12's, which alone edits `app/types.ts`)
  - `src/lib/i18n/en/channels.ts`
- **Depends on:** P12 (the `ChannelService` contract and stub).
- **Deliver:**
  - `ChannelService` per §7.17: `forDoc(docId)` in the AD-32 order (the document's effective machine → its `channels` block → resolution; no block, a broken block or no match → `layout: 'none'`), re-resolved on a content change (debounced with the outline's 150 ms aggregation, never on the keystroke path), on a machine change (`machines.revision`) and when a sibling document opens, closes or is assigned; `channelAt(docId, line)`, `marks(docId)`, `siblings(docId)` (through `channelSiblings`, once per open and on demand, never on a timer), `assign(docId, channelId)`, `check(docId)` over the open channels, `revision`.
  - **One machine decides a check.** `check(docId)` resolves every member with the *initiating* document's effective machine (§7.17). A sibling carrying a different machine, or none, is still checked, and the report header names it ("channel 2 is set to machine 'Lathe 2'") with a one-click "use this machine for channel 2"; the rules of two machines are never mixed in silence, and a sibling that merely opened with the profile's default machine no longer turns the whole report into "missing".
  - The status item (`status-item`, `data-item="channel"`, next to the machine item, hidden for `layout: 'none'`): "Channel: <name>" — the channel at the cursor — for one program with sections, and "<name> (1 of 2)" for one file per channel, with "channel 2 not open" / "channel 2 not found" in `multi-file` mode (§7.16 #169 F6), and "the channel rules of this machine are broken" when the block did not validate; the tooltip lists the layout, every declared channel with where it was found (pattern, marker or the user's assignment) and how many sections it has, and the sync rules in force with their semantics. A click runs `channels.select`: a quick pick that reveals a section (`single-file`), focuses a sibling's tab, offers **"Assign this document to channel N"** for an open document no pattern claimed (persisted through M7's per-file memory, which is also what covers a set whose two paths share one name in different folders), or offers "Open channel 2…" — which opens the **file dialog** in the document's own folder (`DialogService.openFiles` `defaultPath`, §7.14) with multi-select (P1 F3), so one dialog opens every channel. **gEdit opens nothing by itself** (standing rule 14).
  - Program map grouping in `outlineService`: `items(id)` returns one `channel` row per channel with that channel's items — **from every one of its ranges, in document order** — as `children` and the blocking marks as `sync` rows (past 250 waits in a channel, one `sync` row with their number, §7.16 #169 F1), plus one **"Outside the channels"** group listing the `outside` ranges (the header, anything between a `sectionEnd` and the next start, the trailing lines), so a shared subprogram in a concatenated file is visible instead of lost; `toolLines(id)` is unchanged and `toolLines(id, channelId)` is added, so F7/Shift+F7 keep their P1 behaviour and "next tool change in this channel" becomes possible. `itemAt` resolves through the groups. It also computes `jumpLines` per channel for the check (labels that a `GOTO`/`GOTOB`/`GOTOF`/`M99 P`/`M98 Q` in the same channel actually targets, plus backward-jump lines) from the outline and `numbering.references`.
  - Commands (§7.13): `channels.nextSyncPoint` / `channels.prevSyncPoint` (the marks of the channel at the cursor, wrapping like bookmarks), `channels.gotoPartner` (the same id in the next channel that has it, switching document when it must and saying so when it cannot because that channel is not open), `channels.checkSync` (the WP12.2 findings into the Results panel, with the report's title naming the machine and the channels checked, not checked and checked under another machine's name), `channels.splitToDocuments`, `channels.assign`, `channels.testOnDocument`.
  - `channels.splitToDocuments`: one new **untitled** document per channel, holding that channel's ranges in document order **behind the program's own header lines** (the `%` leader, the `O` number or `%_N_…_MPF` / `;$PATH=` block), so each result is a program and not a fragment; named after the source file plus the channel id (`part — channel 1`), nothing written to disk. It is a **one-way copy for reading**: a confirmation before the first run and the status message afterwards both say that edits do not come back (the two-way mapping is Phase 4, §11 items 27 and 28), and the command is **disabled in `multi-file` layout**, where the channels are already separate documents.
  - Ribbon: the check on the Tools tab next to the M10 checks; the navigation in a `channels.group` on the NC tab. Test ids of §7.12.
- **Tests:** the resolution order with a fake `MachineService` (no machine, a machine without channels, a machine whose channel block is broken, a machine with channels and no match, a match); re-resolution on a content change, a machine change, a sibling opening and an assignment; the status text for each case; `check` with the sibling on **no machine** and on a **different machine** (both checked under the initiating document's machine, both named in the header); the grouped tree (a channel with several ranges, the "outside" group holding an inter-section subprogram, marks, a channel with no tools) and that `toolLines(id)` is byte-identical to M11's; `jumpLines` names a targeted label and not a plain one; the navigation decision functions (wrapping, the last mark, a partner in a document that is not open); `splitToDocuments` produces the golden texts **including the header**, touches no file, and is unavailable in `multi-file`; the file dialog receives the document's parent folder as `defaultPath` (fake dialog); the check report's title, its "not checked" list and its other-machine line.
- **Covers:** AD-32 (service, UI, navigation, assignment, split); X12 (a, b, c).

**Wave B**

#### WP12.6 Channels in the script context and the tool list

- **Owns:**
  - `src/lib/core/scripting/context.ts` and its test, `src/lib/app/scripts.ts` and its test
  - `src-tauri/resources/scripts/{_nc_channels.py,gedit_nc.py,tool_list.py}`
  - `tests/python/{test_channels.py,test_tool_list.py}`, `tests/fixtures/scripts/tool_list/channels/**`
- **Depends on:** WP12.1, WP12.5.
- **Deliver:**
  - `ScriptContextV2.channels` per §7.17, filled by `buildContext` from `BuildContextInput.channels`: the layout, which channel this document is, every declared channel with **its line ranges** (`single-file`; a channel may hold several) or file name, path and open flag (`multi-file`), the ranges that belong to **no** channel (`outside`), and the **blocking marks found in this document** with their ids and partner channels. Additive, `contract` stays 2 (F50); a document with `layout: 'none'` has no member at all, so every M5–M11 script and every user script is unaffected.
  - Python: `gedit_nc.channels(ctx)`, `channel_of(ctx, line)`, `channel_lines(ctx, lines, channel_id)`, `outside_lines(ctx, lines)` and `sync_marks(ctx)` in `_nc_channels.py`, re-exported by the facade; `channel_lines` concatenates every range of the channel in document order — what the control itself does when it separates a program into one program per turret — and never returns a line from `outside`. Each answers an empty result for a context without the member, so a script written against M11 keeps working and a script written against M12 runs on a single-channel document.
  - `tool_list.py`: when the context has sections, the rows are grouped by channel, a **Channel** column comes first and the summary names the channels; in `multi-file` mode the summary names the channel this document is and says that the other channels are separate runs. A combined list over several channel files needs multi-document input (P1 D16, §11 item 11) and is **not** claimed.
  - The API text for `src-tauri/resources/scripts/README.md` in the hand-off note (WP12.7 writes it).
- **Tests:** the context shape for each layout and for `none` (a pure `buildContext` test, no editor); every P1–M11 Python golden unchanged; the tool list on a two-section fixture (golden rows with the channel column), on a **four-section alternating** fixture (a channel's rows come from all of its ranges, in document order), on a `multi-file` channel (golden summary) and on a plain single-channel document (byte-identical to the M11 golden); `channel_lines` skips the `outside` ranges between two sections; `channels({})` and a context without the member answer empty.
- **Covers:** AD-32 (scripts); X12 (a).

#### WP12.7 Documentation

- **Owns:** `docs/user/channels.md` (new), `docs/user/{README,machines,scripts,shortcuts}.md`, `docs/planning/README.md`.
- **Depends on:** Wave A.
- **Deliver:** `channels.md`: what a channel is here and what gEdit does **not** do with it (no execution model, no reordering, no synchronization); how to set the two layouts up, with a worked example of each and of the three ways to say which channels a mark makes wait; that a channel may hold **several sections** and what happens to the lines between them; **aliases**, and why one channel usually needs more than one name; the **three sync semantics** (`rendezvous`, `count`, `ordered`) with one sentence each on what the check may conclude, and the warning that naming the wrong one produces findings on a correct program; how to write the patterns (with the note that they are the user's, and that gEdit ships none because the wait codes differ per machine and builder, D58); **what the check checks and what it cannot** — written ids and written order, not machine semantics, with jump targets and backward jumps named as the case it refuses to judge, and "a clean result is not a proof"; how to open the other channels, how to **assign** a document to a channel when no pattern fits, and why gEdit does not open files by itself; the split command **stated as a one-way copy for reading**; the navigation keys. In `machines.md`, the Channels step and the tester, and the note that a broken channel pattern costs the channels and not the machine's other settings; in `scripts.md`, `gedit_nc.channels` and the tool list's channel column with the multi-file limit; in `README.md`, the "What gEdit does not do" list gains the side-by-side channel view as a Phase 4 item, honestly worded, and the split's one-way nature.
- **Covers:** G8 docs check.

#### H12 Harness M12

- `m12-channels-config`: Settings ▸ Machines ▸ Channels: add a two-channel `single-file` machine with aliases and one sync rule, use the tester on pasted lines (it shows a channel with four sections as four ranges, not as a problem), save; `h_config_read('machines.json')` equals the golden; a rule with a pattern that does not compile is refused in the form and, when written into the file by hand, makes **the channel block** invalid — reported on the page, byte-stable after the next save — while the machine stays selectable and the document's number reading is unchanged (X12 d).
- `m12-channels-single`: the single-file fixture with that machine: the channel item reads "Channel: <name>" (§7.16 #169 F6) and follows the cursor across **both** of channel 1's sections; the map shows both channel rows with their tools and marks and an "Outside the channels" group holding the shared subprogram; the tool list has the channel column; "Split into channel documents" opens two untitled tabs with the golden text **including the header**, warns once that it is a one-way copy, and writes no file (`h_stat` on the folder); in the multi-file fixture the command is disabled.
- `m12-channels-multi`: `twin_CH1.nc` alone: "channel 2 found, not open"; the pick opens it through the dialog, which starts in the document's own folder; both open, the item names the channel per tab; with the sibling removed from the folder the item reads "not found"; a pair whose names match no pattern is tied together with "Assign this document to channel 2" and the assignment survives a restart.
- `m12-channels-check`: the mismatch fixtures: the golden findings in the Results panel, each row jumping to the right line **in the right document**; a clean pair reports no finding, **including the fixture with a label on every wait line and ids repeated in both channels**; the count and the ordered fixtures behave per their semantics; the report names the channels it did not check and, when the sibling carries another machine, says so and offers to use one machine for both.
- `m12-channels-nav`: next/previous sync point wraps inside the channel and crosses that channel's second section; "Go to the matching mark" crosses the section and, in multi-file mode, the document; with the partner channel closed it says so and moves nothing.
- `m12-channels-off`: the same fixtures with no machine and with a machine without channels: no channel item, the P1 map, and `m6-lathe-nav` unchanged.
- `m12-perf`: a generated **over-300,000-line** two-section document (the threshold that actually matters, F54) with a channel machine: open ≤ 2 s (the P1 budget), first resolution ≤ 150 ms, typing p95 unchanged, and the check at the contract cap (20,000 marks) inside the §7.17 budget.

**Integration I12:** merge Wave A; regenerate `tests/fixtures/resolved/**` (§5.2 rule 3) for the new goldens that name a machine; check that WP12.5's service runs against WP12.1's resolution and WP12.2's check (all three were built against the P12 contract); make every exhaustive `switch` over `OutlineKind` handle `channel` and `sync`; apply the G10 *changed* items.

Contract changes §7.16 #167. The R5 remainder notes above were corrected for this: the partner-set comparison and `SETM` answering are built, as the rule flags `samePartners` and `answers`. Not done: NC-05 (the profile has no macro-call code list to read), NC-15 (needs a manual sentence), NC-09 and NC-14 (deferred).

**Gates:** G0–G6 (`m0`–`m12`); G7 (the budgets above, plus: the map aggregation of a grouped over-300k-line document stays inside the P1 outline budget; the wait-code check **at the contract cap** — 20,000 marks over 8 and over 32 channels — inside the §7.17 budget, not only at the 2,000-mark typical case; `channel_siblings` for 32 names ≤ 20 ms); G8 (capability diff empty, standing rules 6, 8, 14 and 15, `channels.rs` takes no folder, the pattern-subset scan covers `machines.json` patterns, and the `channel_siblings` name validator accepts exactly the character set the §4 row states); G10 (the check's semantics — the three `SyncSemantics` kinds against what each actually computes — and its stated limits, the fixtures and their goldens, and the confirmation that **every built-in preset is `verify`, sourced and offered only, never applied without a machine**, §8.9); G11 on the owner's machine if they have local multi-channel programs.

### M12.5: Real programs, second pass

**Status (2026-10-08): on `main`; it ships in the one release at the end of the session (the owner's decision, 2026-10-09).** All of the prelude, WP-RP1 to WP-RP6, H-RP and I-RP are in. **Reviews of the integrated branch (clean-room):** 12 NC findings and 12 code findings plus 2 clean-room findings (CODE-CR-1, CODE-CR-2), then a skeptic pass: 17 confirmed, 3 downgraded, 3 refuted (NC-11, CODE-7, CODE-9) and a few sub-claims refuted (NC-9(b), NC-3's second fix option, NC-7's narrowing to builder ranges), 1 plausible (CODE-12 `SETVN`, not done), 2 with no action (CODE-11; CODE-CR-1, the owner kept the preset). **Two fix batches**, each fix with a regression test that fails without it (§7.16 #190): the NC batch (the bare `L` before a cycle definition in `spindleOff`; Okuma `G13`/`G14` at weight 8 with vetoes for `G28` with an axis word and a lone `G15`; no turret word inside a comment; `MT=`/`OS=`/`HP=` with a whole number and not on a `DEF` line; a tool part of zeros is no tool; the control's own `G107`, `G112`, `G113`, `G250`, `G251` stay tool changes; `T<a> T<b> M6` on the lathe profile; Sinumerik end check for a trailing jump, a commented subprogram header and an archive's data section; an untitled document is not judged; Klartext `TOOL CALL "name"` with a digit straight behind it) and the code batch (the uncertain flag cleared by a profile change, a machine pick and Save As; the sync-code hover wording; the contract text of #177; Save As with a remembered machine; neutral wording throughout and the evidence gate `tests/unit/evidenceGate.test.ts`). **Deviations from the skeptic's text:** NC-9(c) is done in `program_checks.py` (a commented subprogram header on the first line of a file), not in `sinumerik.program.start`, which split tagged files at every commented header and added false rows; NC-10 uses `\d+(?:\.\d(?!\d))?` and not a `(?![\d.])` guard, which dropped `TOOL CALL <n>.<3 decimals>` altogether. CODE-4: a machine is still remembered when the document is not flagged. **Regression found by the runtime suite and fixed:** the first switch away from a 10 MB document took 140–157 ms (budget 100). Bisected to the 2026-10-08 Fanuc tool pattern in the map (`toolCall.ignore` with a lookahead, run on every line of the outline in WebKit); the outline now asks `toolCall.ignore` only on lines that trigger or name a tool, with a call-count test (§7.16 #191). **Gates:** `npm run check` 0 errors and 0 warnings (937 files); 6,789 unit tests (1 skipped), including the evidence gate; Python 890 tests on 3.13, 3.12 and 3.9; 288 Rust tests (fmt and clippy clean); the build, the licenses and versions check; G11 on the owner's machine (17 programs: all checks 17 of 17 except the one known unknown-token failure and the known round-trip case). The runtime suite `m0`–`m12` and `rp` (112 scenarios) ran locally; on the GitHub runner: after landing on `main`.

**Goal:** gEdit reads a large body of real CAM output the way M9 made it read the owner's own programs: the right dialect, or a plain warning when no dialect fits; every token of the four supported dialects; the right tool in the map and the tool list; no check that calls a correct program wrong; and channel presets that match how real multi-channel programs are written. It is a fix milestone: no new feature area, every change is a finding with a count behind it and a regression test that fails without the fix.

**Placement (owner decision of 2026-10-08, §10.1):** after M12, before M13, with the same protocol (prelude, Wave A, merge, Wave B, integration, reviews, gates); it ships **with v0.6** together with M12 (§5.3). The roadmap is not changed by this milestone (the owner is reorganising it).

**Where the work comes from.** Two clean-room surveys of 2026-10-08 ran gEdit `main` (`2268e43`) read-only over a corpus of real CAM output that is not the owner's and is never copied, named or quoted (the clean-room rules below): 401 files, 394 non-empty, of which 347 are programs in the four supported dialects (151 Klartext, 66 Fanuc mill, 54 Fanuc lathe and mill-turn, 46 Sinumerik turning, 21 Sinumerik milling, 9 Okuma) and 43 are programs in seven dialects gEdit has no profile for. Survey A (detection, tokenizer, round trip, scripts, map, check false alarms, performance) reported findings F1–F15; survey B (multi-channel programs and other assumptions) reported recommendations 1–9. Every fact the decisions below rest on that the surveys did not settle was checked in the manuals (TNC 640 user and cycle manuals, Sinumerik 840D sl programming manual 06/2019, Fanuc 30i manuals, Okuma OSP-P200L and OSP-P300 manuals, and a Fanuc-based two-head mill-turn programming guide from the manuals folder). The owner has no machines of their own: facts come from manuals and real programs.

**The clean-room rules (the owner's, binding on every WP, reviewer and commit of this milestone):** the survey corpus is never copied, referenced or named. Nobody reads it unless a fact is missing, and then only to extract the fact. In the repository — code, tests, fixtures, goldens, docs, this plan, commit messages, CHANGELOG, comments — nothing names it, its folders, files, customers, machines or builders, and no program line from it is pasted. Every test line and fixture is **synthetic, written for gEdit in our own words** (the surveys' synthetic lines may be used). Evidence is cited only as "real CAM output" with counts, never with names. The commercial reference editor the plan was modeled on is never named.

**What the surveys confirmed (no change):** a Klartext `BEGIN PGM` header is decisive (151 of 151, margin ≥ 56); a Sinumerik `%_N_…_MPF`/`;$PATH=` header settles Siemens; detection does not depend on the open document; 391 of 394 files save byte-exact (the 3 others end in half a CRLF, below); scale feed/speed at 100 % and address arithmetic with 0 are byte-identical on every file; the tool list and the map agree on every correctly detected program; `T<a> T<b> M6` loads the first `T` (45 of 45 lines, and in 23 of 23 consecutive pairs the next change loads the second); 24 Siemens main programs without `G90` write no `G91`, `=IC(` or `=AC(` (M10-1); a lone Sinumerik name is a call (1,135 lone-name blocks; three exceptions below); a `#` in cycle 7 is the table row (109); no line is over 1,000 characters (longest 542); every file is pure ASCII with CRLF; detection takes ≤ 7.6 ms even on a 72 MB file and tokenizing is linear (about 1.4 µs a line).

#### Triage

Every finding and recommendation lands in exactly one of three places: **in** this milestone (with its WP), **deferred** (to TODO with the reason), or **not a defect** (with the reason).

#### Decisions (the prelude's; evidence as counts of real CAM output only)

1. **The five-digit Fanuc lathe `T` (owner, 2026-10-08, revising 2026-09-27).** The split is a machine setting in the existing variant `machineParams.variants[toolWord]` of `fanuc-lathe.json` (a machine stores `params.variants.toolWord`; AD-31). The choices become: **`byLength` (new, the default)** — one or two digits are the station (D49), three or four digits are tool + **two**-digit offset (`T101`, `T0101`, as built), five digits are a **two**-digit tool + **three**-digit offset (`T12012` = tool 12, offset 12); `offset2` — always the last two digits (`T12345` = tool 123, offset 45: the owner's 2026-09-27 reading, the "3 + 2" machine setting); `offset3` — always the last three (`T01001` = tool 1, offset 1: the "2 + 3" machine setting, which reads a four-digit `T0101` as tool 0 and so is never the default); `offset1` unchanged. **Why not `offset3` as the default** (the earlier sketch): it would read every four-digit `T0101` — 91 words in real lathe output, 81 of them "offset = tool" under 2 + 2 — as tool 0, offset 101. **Why no variant detection:** the word's length already decides, and nothing in a program can prove 3 + 2 against 2 + 3 (the only evidence, "offset = tool", supports the default: 62 to 0). The tool pattern of `byLength` is one group, Python-compatible: `(?<![A-Z])T(?<tool>\d{2}(?=\d{3}(?!\d))|\d{1,2}?(?=\d{2}(?!\d))|\d{1,2}(?!\d))\d{0,3}(?!\d)` (WP-RP3 pins it with the cases above). A machine without a `toolWord` value now reads five-digit words 2 + 3 (it read 3 + 2): an intentional change. **And on every choice:** a zero offset part (`T21000`, `T0100`) stays "no tool change" (an offset cancel) **unless the block also writes `M6`** — then it is the tool loaded into the milling spindle of a mill-turn (`M06 T21000` = tool 21; 116 such words in 9 files of real output); a `T` in a block with a three-digit `G` code (`G1xx`–`G9xx`, a builder cycle whose `T` is a parameter) is no tool change (F15/B-7; Okuma's `toolCall.ignore` already does this for `G7x`/`G18x`).
2. **"No profile fits well" (owner, 2026-10-08).** Detection answers how sure it is: `detectResult(…)` → `DetectResult` (`core/profiles/detect.ts`, §7.16 #177). A detection is **uncertain** when the answer came from the content (not a folder, not the user's remembered choice), no decisive or certain rule of the winner matched, at least 5 non-empty lines were scored, and the **family margin** — the winner's score minus the best score of a profile of another `grammar` — is below `UNCERTAIN_FAMILY_MARGIN`, **5 (calibrated once by WP-RP1, an absolute margin; the prelude's 10 was a stub value)**. The family margin, not the plain one, because a Fanuc mill against the Fanuc lathe or the two Siemens profiles is a machine-type question that F2/F3 settle, not a dialect question. The committed fixtures' family margins (the margin table, G10) are 3 for the two inconclusive `nc/ambiguous` fragments, 5 for the Fanuc mill saved as `.MIN`, and 10 and up otherwise (seven short channel fixtures at exactly 10). F1 gives Okuma the numbered-line rule, which raises Okuma's score on every Fanuc program and so lowers the Fanuc family margins: the form of the rule (an absolute margin, or a margin relative to the winner's score, which a 20-line fixture and a 400-line program need) was WP-RP1's to choose from the table **after** F1; it chose the absolute margin 5, because a margin relative to the winner's score did not separate better (a 400-line program carries as few markers as a 40-line one), as long as `DetectResult.uncertain` keeps the meaning above. WP-RP1 calibrates it **once**, against that table and against the clean-room aggregates the orchestrator reruns (counts only), with the targets: no correctly detected supported program flagged beyond 2 %, and as many of the 24 programs of the unsupported dialects that are not Fanuc-compatible as the rule can catch; the final value and both counts go into §7.16 #177. **What the user sees** (plain words for NC programmers): the dialect item in the status bar shows the guess with a question mark ("Fanuc T?") and the tooltip "Dialect uncertain: this program fits none of the dialects well. It is read as Fanuc (ISO) lathe, the closest guess. Click to choose the dialect, or keep the guess."; on open the status line says it once; the dialect picker opens with "The control that wrote this program is not clear. Read it as…" and a first entry "Keep Fanuc (ISO) lathe (the guess)", which remembers the profile for the file (per-file memory, M7) so the warning does not come back. Picking any dialect clears it. Nothing else changes: the guess is applied as today, no command is blocked (the R1 contradiction guard is unchanged). New profiles for the unsupported dialects stay a roadmap decision.
3. **Channel presets (data, the user's plain form; §8.9 table).** Added, all `verify: true` with their sources: on `fanuc-lathe` — **`fanuc-2path-nop`** (two paths, waits `M900-M999` written without `P` = paths 1 and 2, no file-name rule: real output names a path-1 file `O<nnnn>` with no extension and writes only path 1, so the files are tied by hand); **`fanuc-3path-digits-nop12`** (three paths, waits `M190-M199`, `P` as path numbers, **no `P` = paths 1 and 2** (`whenAbsent: fixed [1, 2]`), file name `^(?<stem>.+)_(?<channel>\d{1,2})\.[A-Za-z]+$`; the label warns that such a rule also catches version files); **`fanuc-2head-m100`** (two paths, waits `M100-M197`, no `P` = paths 1 and 2, files tied by hand; the label says that on this kind of machine `M198` is an external-program call, not a wait; source: the two-head mill-turn programming guide, operation synchronisation); on `sinumerik` (and so `sinumerik-mill`) — **`sinumerik-2channel-archive`** (`single-file`, `sectionStart ^%_N_(?<channel>\d+)_0_MPF\b`, `sectionEnd ^%_N_(?!\d+_0_MPF\b)`, the `WAITM`/`SETM`/`CLEARM` rules of `sinumerik-2channel`), **`sinumerik-2channel-c`** (`multi-file`, `fileName ^(?<stem>.+)_C(?<channel>\d{1,2})\.MPF$`), **`sinumerik-tagged`** (`single-file`, `sectionStart ^<PROG_BEGIN_C(?<channel>\d+)>`, `sectionEnd ^<PROG_END_`). **Relabelled:** every `M900-M999` preset says "one builder's range; your machine's range is parameters 8110/8111". **Corrected:** §8.9's claim that every `M9xx` wait carries a `P` (true of the owner's local set; in real CAM output all 26 real `M9xx` waits carry none, and the only `P` waits are 2 `M19x` words in digit form). The shipped three-path presets keep "no `P` = no partner" (the manual's rule for three or more paths); the new preset is the builder variant. Not a preset, a user-guide example (RP7): sync through a builder cycle, as two Advanced rules (a bare call = `count`, a numbered call = rendezvous with its id).
4. **A machine's wait codes win over the code database** (B-5). When the document's machine lists a code word in a plain `codes` rule (`M198` in `M190-M199`), hover presents it as "Wait code on this machine (<machine>): <rule label>" and leaves out the database's explanation and its required-word note (`ChannelService.waitCodeRule`, §7.16 #178; the `assistant.hover.waitCode*` keys). A `regex` rule never answers (the database describes `WAITM` correctly). The program checks read the same fact from the script context (`channels.marks`): no `requiredWords` or `aloneInBlock` row on a line that holds one of the machine's marks. Completion is unchanged.
5. **Siemens mill tool change without `M6`** (F5). The Sinumerik programming manual (§2.4, "Werkzeugwechsel") says the machine builder sets whether `T` changes the tool directly (turret, or tool-change mode with `T`) or only selects it for `M6`. So `sinumerik-mill.json` gains a variant **`toolChange`**: **`m6`** (default, as built: `M6` loads the tool selected with `T`) and **`t`** ("`T` changes the tool": the turning profile's `T` rule, `toolFrom: same-line`). Detection, scored once per file as every variant: `M0*6` at 5 for `m6`, a `T` word alone in its block (with an optional `D`) at 3 for `t`; so a program with any `M6` stays `m6` (margin 2 at most for `t`), and only a program that selects tools and never writes `M6` reads `t` (margin 3). A machine says which, and wins. **Without guessing:** the map lists the tool at its `T` block; it never claims that the builder's subprogram call (`L6`) is the change — a user profile (M13) can name that subprogram as the trigger.
6. **Klartext cycle 19 switched off by an empty `19.1`** (B-3). The TNC 640 cycle manual (cycle 19, working plane, "reset": enter 0° for every rotary axis, then define cycle 19 again and answer the dialog with NO ENT) makes an empty `CYCL DEF 19.1` the switch-off. New database member `CodeEntry.frameEmptyCloses` (§7.16 #180), `"1"` on `CYCL DEF 19`: a `19.1` block that writes none of `A`/`B`/`C` closes the tilt group; `19.0` still changes nothing, and a partly written `19.1` keeps the M10-2 reading. `PLANE` is unchanged (`PLANE AXIAL` all zero is followed by an explicit `PLANE RESET` in about 650 of 827 cases, so "all zero does not end `AXIAL`" stays).
7. **Tokenizer additions (TS and Python on shared goldens; §7.5, §7.16 #179).** A new token kind **`text`** (text the control keeps and shows but does not execute) and six optional `syntax` fields: `freeText` (patterns with a `text` group: Klartext cycle names behind `CYCL DEF <n>` up to `~`/`;`/end, program names between `BEGIN PGM`/`END PGM` and `MM`/`INCH`, `FN 16: F-PRINT` and `CALL PGM` paths), `colonWords` (Klartext `VCONST`, `VC`, `HSC-MODE`), `callTargets` (Okuma `CALL`/`MODIN` + `O[A-Z0-9]{1,4}` → `programMarker`), `labelAfter` (Sinumerik `GOTO`, `GOTOF`, `GOTOB`, `GOTOC` → the target is a `label`), `declareAfter` (Sinumerik `DEF`: the names declared on that line are `variable`), `plainTextRun` (Fanuc mill and lathe: 3 — a run of three or more letters with no digit that is no keyword or function is one `unknown` token), and `tapeMarker: false` (Klartext has no tape; a `%` in a comment is text). Keywords: Sinumerik `SBLOF`, `SBLON`, `DISPLOF`, `DISPLON`, `NORM`, `KONT`, `KONTC`, `KONTT` (with database entries); Okuma `DRAW`, `CLEAR`. Okuma system variables gain the tool-data family `VTL…` (`VTLL`, `VTLD`; OSP-P200L system variable table). A name used on a later line than its `DEF` stays one `unknown` token (the tokenizer reads one line at a time; G11 counts a name declared in the same file as known).
8. **Program checks (Python): the false alarms.** `blockWords` accepts two `T` words in a block that also has the tool-change code when the effective tool rule takes the first (`T<a> T<b> M6` is legal, owner 2026-10-08 M9-1); `spindleOff` counts a block as a cut only when it writes an axis word (a bare `L` is no move); `jumpTargets` does not judge a target written as an expression (a string, `<<`, a variable); the tape rules follow the tokenizer (`tapeMarker: false` turns them off for Klartext). Free text read as `T` words disappears with decision 7's plain-text run.
9. **Program checks (Python): the end of a program** (F10). `programFrame` reports, at the last line, a Klartext program whose `BEGIN PGM` has no `END PGM` (and an `END PGM` whose name differs), and a Sinumerik main program (`MPF`, or no header) whose last code block is no end code (`M30`, `M2`, `M02`, `M17`, `RET`) — a subprogram (`SPF`, `PROC`) is not judged. Severity error, as the Fanuc tape rule.
10. **Detection weights** (F1, F2, F3, F13, F14): as in the triage table; the patterns are WP-RP1's, held by the margin table before and after (G10) and by the synthetic files below. Every detection pattern must also compile in Python (`_nc_lex.py` compiles `detect.content`).

**Intentional behavior changes in M12.5** (H-RP and the integration may update earlier goldens and scenarios only for these): detection of the F1/F2/F3/F13/F14 shapes; the dialect item's question mark and the picker's first entry on an uncertain document; five-digit lathe `T` words without a machine read 2 + 3; `M6` with a zero-offset `T` loads the tool; no tool in a three-digit-`G` lathe block; Siemens mill programs without `M6` list their `T` tools; Klartext `TOOL CALL "name"` at the line end is a tool change; the new token kinds and fields of decision 7; the check rows of decisions 8 and 9; an empty `CYCL DEF 19.1` closes the tilt; the hover of a machine's wait code; the new presets and labels.

**Prelude (this commit):** the decisions above; §7.16 #177–#184; §8.9 corrected and its table extended; §10.1 (2026-10-08, second set); the stubs and pins: `DetectResult`, `detectResult` (real id, `uncertain` always `false`), `UNCERTAIN_FAMILY_MARGIN`, `UNCERTAIN_MIN_LINES` in `core/profiles/detect.ts`; `ProfileRegistry.detectResult` (implemented in `stores/profiles.ts`); `DocMeta.dialectUncertain`; `ChannelService.waitCodeRule` (stub answering `null`); `TokenKind` `'text'`; the `syntax` fields `freeText`, `colonWords`, `callTargets`, `labelAfter`, `declareAfter`, `plainTextRun`, `tapeMarker`; `CodeEntry.frameEmptyCloses`; the i18n keys `profiles.uncertain.*` and `assistant.hover.waitCode*`; the margin table (`tests/unit/helpers/margins.ts`, `tests/unit/detectMargins.test.ts`: `GEDIT_MARGIN_TABLE=<file>` writes it; a pin holds `detectResult(…).id` to `detectProfile` on every fixture). No data file changes in the prelude. No command id is added.

**The regression-test rule (every WP):** each fix comes with a test that fails without it (run it against the prelude commit before the fix); a WP that changes a golden lists each changed golden and why in its hand-off; a WP never edits another WP's goldens. Every test line is synthetic (the clean-room rules).
