### M13: Your own dialects and codes, and the Phase 2 exit

**Goal:** a user sets up a machine's dialect details (folder, numbering, builder M-codes) as a user profile, next to that machine's M6 machine configuration, and types without accidental lower case or merged blocks; Phase 2 closes with its exit criteria and the 1.0 release. (The templates half of the old M12 moved to Phase 3: see the end of this section.)

**Status (2026-10-09): on `main`; Phase 2 is done, and the one release, 1.0, follows the owner's bundle check (§5.3).** The prelude P13, WP13.1–WP13.5, H13a, H13b and I13 are in. **Prelude decisions:** the owner decided for this session (2026-10-09) that there is **one release at the very end** (no tag before it; the "Unreleased" changes of v0.6 go into it), that `spike/harness-ci` may be force-pushed before every landing, and that `roadmap.md` is updated as milestones land; and upper case on every control, Okuma, Sinumerik and Klartext included (P13 decision 1, AD-30). **Reviews of the integrated branch:** 14 NC findings (NC-1 to NC-14) and 20 code findings (CODE-1 to CODE-20), then a skeptic pass over all 34: 16 confirmed with their severity, 1 confirmed with a wider scope (CODE-3: Python scripts opened in the editor were hit too), 9 confirmed with a lower severity, 6 only plausible (NC-14, CODE-7, CODE-12, CODE-14, CODE-15, CODE-18), 1 refuted (CODE-11), 1 merged (CODE-16 into NC-2), and 4 sub-claims refuted (NC-2's "detection twin", NC-8's "the golden is wrong", NC-11 part 3, CODE-2's "Reload cannot recover"); four findings stayed High (NC-1, NC-2, CODE-1, CODE-3) and three Medium (NC-4, CODE-2, CODE-6). **The owner's decisions of 2026-10-09 on the findings:** empty lines can be deleted (a line of blanks counts as empty); the word-delete chords (Ctrl, Alt or Cmd with Backspace or Delete) are refused at a join of two text lines, like the plain keys; a Fanuc `<name>` and Klartext program and cycle names keep their case as typed; **a user code entry merges member by member** with the entry it lays over, `"replace": true` replaces it whole, and every change of meaning is a notice (AD-17, §7.16 #203); and **a user profile never takes a program from any built-in on a tie**: it is picked automatically only through rules it adds itself, and by hand (remembered for the file). **Two fix batches** (§7.16 #203), each fix with a regression test that was checked to fail without it: the NC batch (code entries merge by member and the variant decides the codes it defines differently; one notice per overlay that reaches another machine type; a transactional code reload; detection ties; a subprogram behind the main program in the tool list and the extents; an unreadable number named in address arithmetic, scale feed and scale speed; Klartext name patterns and the Fanuc `<name>`; renumber says where a jump now lands; the tool label in the extents; the channel prefix of a mark in the check; a thread's `F`; word-delete chords at a join; the exit2 fixtures made realistic) and the code batch (pattern caps and the repeated-group check for user patterns, the nesting depth cap, no typing in `.json` and `.py`, listed names in Rust, a cap on problems and Results rows, the lost-profile `restore`, a time budget and chunks for the tester, the New Profile From… failure path, the size check before a read, `Object.hasOwn`, a Monaco registration that survives one bad profile, name caps, notices apart from problems, vetoed detect rows). **Left optional on purpose:** CODE-15, CODE-18, NC-14(b) (an imported `defaults[profileId]` is not checked against the machine's profile chain; nothing breaks, because `compatible()` ignores it), H13a-6, and a note on `syncRowsOf` in `app/outlineService.ts` (it caches by the set object alone, so the same marks with other `params` or `ruleLabel` in one set would return the old texts; harmless while a machine change always yields a new set). **The disk-full interruption:** the first run of the code batch ran into a full disk (two G11 tests failed on it; they pass alone and in the full run), and H13b had to delete the Rust build output of the harness to finish its run; the code batch was gated again once the space was back, and this integration ran its Rust gate in a build directory that it deleted afterwards. **Runtime scenarios** (H13b): the new `m13-typing`, `m13-user-profile`, `m13-machines-io`, `exit2-x1`, `-x2`, `-x5`, `-x6`, `-x8`, `-x9`, `-x9-ties`, `-x11-1`, `-x11-2`, `-x12` and `exit2-nopython`; the suite is now `m0`–`m12`, `rp`, `m13`, 126 scenarios; `m2-settings`, `m6-machines-broken` and `m6-machines-manage-1` changed for the Profiles tab and the Import and Export buttons. Local run before the review fixes: 125 of 126, the one failure `exit2-x9-ties`, the tie bug that the NC batch fixed (NC-2); the scenario is expected to pass since, and the suites were not run again locally after the fixes. **Gates on the integrated branch (`m13/int`, before the records commit):** `npm run check` 969 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings; `CI=1 npm test` 193 files, 7,243 passed, 1 skipped, the evidence gate and G11 included, no timing failure; Python 903 tests, OK on `python3` (3.13), `/usr/bin/python3` (3.9) and `python3.12`; Rust `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 318 passed, 1 ignored; `npm run build`, `licenses:check` and `versions:check` (0.5.0) pass; `rustfmt --check` on the harness passes; G11 on the owner's 17 programs: 17 of 17 detected, noCrash 17 of 17, the only failure the known `B-?` token (`unknownTokens #12:2752`). **G10 sign-off for Phase 2:** the six built-in profiles (`fanuc-gcode`, `fanuc-lathe`, `heidenhain-klartext`, `okuma-osp`, `sinumerik`, `sinumerik-mill`), their `machineParams` and every built-in code database (the `fanuc-lathe-b` variant included) were each covered by the NC-correctness review of every milestone that changed them (M6 `fanuc-lathe` and the mill changes, M8 `okuma-osp` and `sinumerik`, M9 `sinumerik-mill` and the changes to the others, M10 the checks, M11 search, M12 and the post-M12 pass the channel presets, M13 the user-profile, code-entry and variant rules); the findings and their fixes are recorded in each milestone's Status above, and no separate consolidated sign-off document exists. **Runtime suite on the GitHub runner: 126 of 126, none flaky (run 37939999013, after a first run that found one stale scenario check and a second that confirmed the by-hand-pick fix).** (the orchestrator fills this in after the landing).

**What P13 decided (2026-10-09; hand-off `SP/handoff/m13-p13.md`):**

1. **Typing on every control (AD-30; the owner's answer of 2026-10-08, §10.1).** Every built-in profile carries `"editing": { "forceUppercase": true, "preventLineJoin": true }`, written in each of the six files (the children `fanuc-lathe` and `sinumerik-mill` too, so each file says it); `heidenhain-klartext` changes from `false` to `true` (§7.16 #193). A letter is upper-cased only outside the token kinds `comment`, `string` and `text` (M12.5's kept text: Klartext program and cycle names, `FN 16` paths; Sinumerik `MSG("…")`, `T="name"`), decided on the line as it reads with the character typed, so an opening quote or comment start typed just before counts. `validateProfile` checks the two booleans (`editing.forceUppercase`, `editing.preventLineJoin`) and carries any other member (`tabWidth`) unread; `Profile.editing` is typed (`ProfileEditing`, §7.1). One more intentional change follows from the flag's P1 meaning ("the control expects upper case"): **Convert Case to lower case now asks first on every built-in**, Okuma, Sinumerik and Klartext included (`convertCase.test.ts` updated).
2. **The folders and the names (§4, §7.10).** `kind` is `profiles` or `codes`; the folders are `<config>/profiles` and `<config>/codes`, created by `ensure_dirs` at every start, and **`ConfigPaths` gains `profilesDir` and `codesDir`** (§7.16 #194) so the webview can tell a document saved there and a runtime scenario can write user files. Names: `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}\.json$` and no DOS device name on any platform (`con.json`); lower case only, so one name is one file on every platform. `user_files_list`: at most 64 files by name, each ≤ 1 MiB of UTF-8, a link is never followed (listed with an error), a file that cannot be read is listed with its reason and never dropped silently, a missing folder lists as empty. `user_file_import` lower-cases the source's file name and refuses one that still does not pass. `user_file_delete` backs a **Remove…** on the Profiles page (with a question), for user files only. The pure rules (`Kind::parse`, `validate_name`, the caps) are implemented in `userfiles.rs` with their tests; the five commands are registered stubs.
3. **Code files (AD-29; §7.16 #195).** A file's `dialect` member must equal its file stem (`fanuc-lathe.json` holds `"dialect": "fanuc-lathe"`), so two files never claim one database. **Same id as a built-in database: an overlay**, laid over it before its children resolve (what a user adds to `fanuc-lathe` reaches `fanuc-lathe-b` too) and used by every profile on that database. **A new id: a database of its own**, which must `extends` a built-in or another user database and is used by a user profile that names it in `codes`. **A user database follows the variant:** when a machine's variant switches the document to a database that descends from the one the user file extends (`fanuc-lathe` → `fanuc-lathe-b` under system B), the effective database is the user file's own entries laid over the variant's database, with the `extends` merge rules (AD-17), cached per pair; so a builder M-code table survives a system-B machine.
4. **User profiles.** A user id equal to a built-in id is refused with a problem (`id`); of two user files with one id the first by name loads and the second is reported; `extends` may name a built-in or a user profile; `ProfileInfo.origin` is `'user'` and `file` the file name.
5. **The reload chain (§7.3; §7.16 #192, #197).** `userConfig.load()` lists both folders, does nothing more when every file is byte-identical to the last load, otherwise runs `codes.reload(codeFiles)` **then** `profiles.reload(profileFiles)` (a profile is validated against the databases that exist after the code reload), re-detects every open document whose profile no longer exists (one status message), and puts the problems into the Results panel when there are any. `profiles.revision` bumps on **every** `reload`; it is the one signal for both kinds of file. Its subscribers: the Monaco languages, grammars and themes (`monaco/languages.ts`), the hover, completion, symbol and folding providers registered per profile id (`contrib/assistant.ts`, `contrib/programMap.ts`: a new id gets them once, a vanished one keeps them harmlessly), the outline (`app/outlineService.ts`), the machines (`stores/machines.ts`: every record re-validated, every open document re-evaluated) and the Profiles page. The contract `UserConfigService` (`files`, `problems`, `load()`, `kindOf(path)`) and `UserFileEntry` are new in `app/types.ts`; `bootstrap.ts` awaits `loadUserConfig` after the settings and the UI state and before the machines (its unit test pins the order).
6. **Who owns what (§7.16 #196).** WP13.2 also owns `contrib/assistant.ts` and `contrib/programMap.ts` (the per-id provider registrations above). The save hook (a save of a file in either folder reloads, AD-29) is WP13.3's, in `contrib/userConfig.ts`, beside the commands, the way `contrib/machineSelect.ts` holds the machines file's; it asks `userConfig.kindOf(path)`. `machines.import` and `machines.export` are registered in `contrib/userConfig.ts` too (WP13.3 owns `stores/machines.ts`, not `contrib/machineSelect.ts`). The prelude alone edits `app/types.ts`, `bootstrap.ts`, `platform/commands.ts` (the five wrappers are in) and `contrib/README.md` (the rows of `typing.ts` and `userConfig.ts` are in).
7. **Commands (§7.13).** The nine rows of WP13.3 and WP13.4 are pinned `pending` in `tests/unit/commandPins.test.ts`, none with a default key, `findConflicts` clean on both platforms: `profile.newFrom` "New Profile From…", `profile.open` "Open Profile File…", `profile.import` "Import Profile File…", `profile.export` "Export Profile File…", `profile.reload` "Reload Profiles", `profile.testOnDocument` "Test Profile on Document" (category `userConfig.category` "Profiles"; the tester also on the Tools tab, group `userConfig.toolsGroup`, after the Channels group), `machines.import` "Import Machines…", `machines.export` "Export Machines…" (category `machines.category`), all owned by `contrib/userConfig.ts`; `edit.toggleForceUppercase` "Upper-Case Typing", Home tab, group `typing.group` "Typing" after the Edit group, category `typing.category`, owned by `contrib/typing.ts`. `profile.newFrom`, `profile.open`, `profile.import` and `profile.export` take an optional `{ kind: 'profiles' | 'codes' }` and ask for it from the palette. The i18n stubs `i18n/en/{userConfig,typing}.ts`, `machines.import`/`machines.export` and `settings.categories.profiles` hold exactly these.
8. **Settings and state (§7.11).** M13 adds **no settings key** (`SETTINGS_VERSION` stays 1, `MACHINES_VERSION` stays 1): the two typing options are profile values, and `edit.toggleForceUppercase` holds for the session only, never saved. `state.json` gains only `ui.lastParams['userConfig:newFrom']` (`{ kind, parent }`, the last choice of "New Profile From…", through `uiState.setLastParams`). The Settings dialog gains the category `profiles`.
9. **Test ids (§7.12).** `profile-row` gains `data-kind=profile|codes` and `data-file`; new `profile-action` with `data-action=new|open|import|export|reload|test|remove`; `machine-action` `import|export` as §7.12 already says. `h.app.ctx.userConfig` is in; scenarios write user files to `paths.profilesDir` / `paths.codesDir` of `config_load`.
10. **The exit fixtures.** `tests/fixtures/exit2/README.md` is the plan of the folder: per criterion the synthetic inputs, the goldens and the scenarios that already prove it, the fixed order, and what the no-Python run saves. H13 writes the files with a generator (`gen-exit2.py`), as `exit/gen-exit.py` writes the Phase 1 set.

**Prelude P13:** the reload additions of §7.3 with `revision`/`reload`/`problems` stubs in `stores/{profiles,codes}.ts`; the `user_file*` Rust stubs in `src-tauri/src/userfiles.rs` (registered); `paths.rs` `ensure_dirs` for `<config>/{profiles,codes}`; the stub `app/userConfig.ts`; the `editing` blocks of `okuma-osp.json` and `sinumerik.json` (TODO decision "Upper-case typing on Okuma and Sinumerik", recommended yes), so AD-30 has a value on every profile it names; **the start-up order in `src/lib/app/bootstrap.ts`** — `await userConfig.load()` before `machines.load(configLoad)`, wired here against the stub, because the prelude is the only WP that edits `bootstrap.ts` (P1 §4.2) and WP13.2 only fills the body; `tests/fixtures/exit2/README.md`; `h.app.ctx.userConfig`; commit.

**Intentional behavior changes in M13:** typing in a document of any built-in profile (Fanuc, lathe, Okuma, Sinumerik and, since P13, Klartext) is upper-cased outside comments, strings and kept text, and Backspace at column 1 or Delete at a line end no longer joins blocks (H13 updates the P1 scenarios that typed lower case or joined lines; the harness's `insertText` is no keystroke and is not affected); Convert Case to lower case asks first on every built-in (P13 decision 1).

**Wave A**

- **Owns:** `src/lib/contrib/userConfig.ts`, `src/lib/core/profiles/testReport.ts` and its test, `src/lib/components/dialogs/{SettingsDialog,MachinesPage}.svelte` and their tests, `src/lib/stores/machines.ts` and its test (M13: import/export and re-validation on `profiles.revision`), `src/lib/i18n/en/{userConfig,settings,machines}.ts`.

**Integration of Wave A (2026-10-09; branch `m13/int`; hand-off `SP/handoff/m13-int.md`; commit `I13`).** WP13.1–WP13.4 merged without conflict; what the integration did, by the hand-offs' notes:

- **`ProfileProblem.kind`** (`'profiles' | 'codes'`, §7.16 #198): set by every producer (`core/profiles/resolve.ts`, `stores/profiles.ts`, `stores/codes.ts`, `app/userConfig.ts`), so a profile file and a code file of one name never show each other's problems on the Profiles page, in the import message or in `looseProblems`. Tests: `ProfilesPage.test.ts` (`rowsOf`, `looseProblems`), `app/userConfig.test.ts`, `contrib/userConfig.test.ts`, and the integration test below.
- **`MachineService.importMachines` and `MachineImportSummary`** are in `app/types.ts` (§7.16 #199); the local `MachineTransfer` of `stores/machines.ts` is gone.
- **`i18n/en/userConfigLoad.ts` is folded into `i18n/en/userConfig.ts`** (`userConfig.load.redetected`, `userConfig.load.report.*`); the old file is deleted, no key outside those two namespaces moved.
- **`profile.manage` "Manage Profiles…"** (title `userConfig.manage`, category `userConfig.category`, no key; §7.13, §7.16 #200) opens the Settings dialog on the Profiles tab. It is registered in `contrib/settings.ts`, not `contrib/userConfig.ts`: the Profiles page imports `contrib/userConfig.ts` for its helpers, and `userConfig.ts` importing the dialog would close an import cycle (`SettingsDialog` ▸ `ProfilesPage` ▸ `userConfig`). Pinned in `commandPins.test.ts` (owner `settings.ts`) with `findConflicts` clean on both platforms.
- **`EditorService.onDidAttach`** (§7.16 #201): fired by `attach()` after it has built and bound a new editor instance (the first, and each remount of `EditorHost`, e.g. after the compare overlay). `contrib/typing.ts` hooks the keyboard on it and on activation only; the four-event workaround (`ready`, activation, model creation, every cursor event) is gone. Tests in `editorService.test.ts` (two attaches, same container, a failed attach, unsubscribe) and `typing.test.ts`.
- **A profile's name.** `ProfileInfo.name` is the file-dialog filter name, which a user profile inherits from its parent (§7.2, unchanged); what a user calls a dialect is `Profile.name`, as the picker already uses. The Profiles page rows, the "Start from" choices and the Test report's title and detection line now show `Profile.name` too (`displayName` in `contrib/userConfig.ts`; §7.16 #202). Found by the integration test: a profile renamed in the editor kept the parent's filter name on the page.
- **Checked against the real Rust commands** (`userfiles.rs`, WP13.1): argument names (`kind`, `name`, `text`, `src`), the camelCase `UserFile`, the name rule and device names (the TypeScript rule is the same, and stricter on a trailing dot), `create` answering the full path and granting that one file, `import` answering the lower-cased name and granting nothing (Open goes through `user_file_path`), a 65th file or an unreadable one listed with `text: null` (a problem with `kind`, never a profile), and the capability file (custom commands need no entry; the fs and dialog permissions already cover the picked and exported files). Checked against WP13.2's reload with the real stores: `src/lib/app/userConfig.integration.test.ts` runs the real `userConfig`, `profiles`, `codes`, `machines`, `docs`, `files`, `settings`, the platform wrappers and the contribution's commands with only `invoke` (an in-memory model of the five commands, the machines file and `files_stat`), `plugin-fs` and `plugin-dialog` faked. It covers the load chain, detection by a user profile's folder, problems with their kind, same-named files, machines going inactive and active with the profile, the cached view of an open document, a vanished profile with subscribers asking for the document in the middle of the reload (none fails), New → edit → save → reload, the overlay and own-database code files, import and export of a profile and of machines, and the tester.
- **Not changed, on purpose:** `machines.effective` stays throwing for an unknown profile id (every subscriber of `profiles.revision` that runs between the registry swap and the re-detection is the outline, which guards, and `machines`, which only invalidates); the integration test pins that no subscriber meets it.

**Wave B**

#### WP13.5 Documentation

- **Owns:** `docs/user/**`, `README.md`, `docs/planning/{README,roadmap}.md`.
- **Deliver:** a full pass: own profiles and code files (an example user profile and an M-code table), how a user profile and a machine configuration divide the work, machine import/export, typing options; the roadmap marks Phase 2 done, adds machine configurations **and multi-channel programs** as Phase 2 rows, moves its backlog line "Split multi-channel lathe programs by channel and check wait codes (generic patterns only)" into Phase 2 as shipped, adds the **side-by-side channel view** to Phase 4 (§11 item 27), and narrows the "Not planned" entry so that it reads as it is meant: gEdit ships no machine-builder-specific sync **code set**, while the user's own channel and sync patterns are a supported machine parameter (D58, §8.9). The deferred rows move to Phase 3, Phase 4 or the backlog as §11 says, and the release notes of 1.0 say what Phase 2 did not do.

#### H13 Harness M13 and the Phase 2 exit criteria

- **Owns:** `tests/runtime/scenarios/**`, `tests/runtime/suites/m13.txt`, `tests/fixtures/exit2/**`.
- `m13-user-profile` (the user-profile half of X9), `m13-typing`.
- `m13-machines-io`: export the machines file, remove every machine, import it back; the list and the per-file choices are as before.
- **`exit2-x1` to `exit2-x12` (planned as `m13-exit-criteria`):** X1, X2, X5, X6, X8, the user-profile half of X9, X11 and X12 over `exit2/*`, each saving at the end with golden bytes. **The order matters and is fixed here and in `tests/fixtures/exit2/README.md`:** X1 and X2 run **first, while no machine exists at all**, because they check what gEdit shows with nothing chosen (the detected G-code system, the assumed `DIAMON`); then X5, X6 and X8; then X9, X11 and X12, which create machines. X11(a) clears the default machine it set before X11(b) reads "Machine: none", and X12 runs **last** because its two machines carry channel blocks that every earlier criterion must not see — X12(c) re-checks one earlier fixture with those machines defined but not chosen.
- **`exit2-nopython` (planned as `m13-exit-criteria-nopython`):** the parts that need no Python (detection, navigation, renumber with rewrite, compare, search, the machine choice and the whole of X12 except the tool list's channel column) with `GEDIT_PYTHON=/nonexistent`; the script commands show the P1 "Python not found" message.

**Release:** 1.0, after the owner's bundle check on the three platforms (§5.3, §13).

### Designed in Phase 2, moved to Phase 3: understand a block and templates

These designs were written for the old M11 (without address arithmetic, now WP10.4) and the old M12 (its templates half). The third revision (2026-09-30, D69) moved them to the new **Phase 3, "Understand and write"**: they help the owner understand and write a program, they do not make CAM output safer, and the TypeScript modal interpreter they share had no consumer in Phase 2.

**Moved on 2026-10-09 (the Phase 3 prelude, P3a):** the designs that stood here — the Phase 3 prelude, P3.1 to P3.6, the intentional behavior changes, the documentation and harness paragraph and the gates — are now in the Phase 3 plan of record, [phase-3-implementation.md](phase-3-implementation.md) (§5: P3a "Understand a block", P3b "Write with templates"), together with the rows that had no design yet (motion-mode line coloring as P3.7; cycle forms, formula parameters and the template manager as P3.8 and P3.9) and the exit criteria (X7, the templates half of X9, X14–X18). The contracts they rely on stay in this plan and are cited from there: AD-19 (its TypeScript half), AD-27, AD-28, the `ModalService` of §7.3, the TypeScript half of §7.4, §7.8, the Phase 3 rows of §7.12 and §7.13, §8.6. Where Phase 3 changes one of them, the Phase 3 plan's §7 records it, numbered after §7.16 (#204 onwards).

**What stayed true in Phase 2 because of the move:** the Insert tab keeps the Phase 1 blocks, which exist for the mill and Klartext only (F34, D13 stays open); hover explains codes and addresses as in Phase 1, without the modal context and the effective values; G11 checks the Python modal state only, and the per-line TS/Python parity waits for P3.1.

## 7. Shared contracts (binding; written by the preludes)

P1 §7 stays binding: a type is imported only from its home file; a later prelude replaces a placeholder at the same path; a WP keeps a pinned signature and replaces only the body; `app/types.ts` stays the home of the service interfaces. Everything below is **additive** unless §7.14 says otherwise.

### 7.1 Profile additions: `src/lib/core/profiles/types.ts` (P6, P8, P9, P11, P12, P13)

```ts
export type MachineType = 'mill' | 'lathe';
export type FeedUnit = 'per-minute' | 'per-rev' | 'per-tooth' | 'inverse-time' | 'unknown';

export interface Profile {
  // … every P1 §7.4 field …
  /** P6. Parent profile id; resolved before validation (AD-16), kept on the result for display. */
  extends?: string;
  /** P6. Default 'mill'. Scripts, hover and the inspector read it (auto options, diameter wording).
   *  Named `machineType` so it is never confused with a machine configuration (AD-31). */
  machineType?: MachineType;
  /** P8 widens the P1 union. */
  grammar: 'iso' | 'klartext' | 'okuma' | 'sinumerik';
  /** P6. The power-on state. `initial`: modal group → canonical code in force at the top of a program.
   *  `units`, `diameter` and `sources` are written by `applyMachine` (AD-31) from the machine or the
   *  defaults in `machineParams`; a built-in JSON writes only `initial`. All are applied as "assumed",
   *  and `sources` tells the interpreter where each came from (AD-19 rule 8): a key per modal group
   *  plus 'units' and 'diameter'. */
  modal?: { initial?: Record<string, string>; units?: 'mm' | 'inch'; diameter?: 'on' | 'off';
            sources?: Record<string, ParamSource> };
  /** P6. What a machine configuration of this profile may set, with the documented defaults (AD-31, §7.15, §8.8).
   *  Absent: the profile has no machine parameters (Klartext) and no machine item. */
  machineParams?: MachineParamsDecl;
  addresses: {
    // … P1 fields …
    /** P6. Incremental address → the axis it moves: { U: 'X', W: 'Z' }. */
    incremental?: Record<string, string>;
    /** P6. Addresses written as a diameter while the diameter mode is on: ['X', 'U']. */
    diameter?: string[];
    /** P6. Rotary axes, whose number class is `angle` (AD-31): ['A', 'B', 'C']. */
    angular?: string[];
    /** P6. Words that set the feed unit (Klartext { FU: 'per-rev', FZ: 'per-tooth' }); a plain feed word returns to the group's unit. */
    feedUnitWords?: Record<string, Exclude<FeedUnit, 'unknown'>>;
    /** P8. Assignment words whose value is a spindle-speed clamp (Sinumerik ['LIMS']). */
    speedLimitWords?: string[];
  };
  toolCall: {
    // … P1 fields (trigger, tool, toolFrom) …
    /** P6. A trigger line whose masked text also matches this is not a tool change (`T0100`; an Okuma `T` in a cycle block). */
    ignore?: Pattern;
  };
  syntax: {
    // … P1 fields …
    /** P8. `N` + a letter-led name (Okuma NLAP1) is a `label` token, never a block number. */
    sequenceNames?: boolean;
    /** P8. An address matching this at a word start takes `=` + expression (Okuma SB=, Sinumerik CR=, S3=, R1=). */
    assignment?: Pattern;
    /** P8. A label definition at block start (Sinumerik `NAME:`); named group `name`. */
    labels?: Pattern;
    /** P8. An identifier directly followed by `(` is one `call` token up to the matching `)`. */
    calls?: boolean;
    /** P8. System variables: `\$[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*`, Okuma `V[A-Z][A-Z0-9]{3}`. */
    systemVariables?: Pattern;
    /** P8. A first-line header read as one `programMarker`: `^\$[^%]*%` (Okuma), `^%_N_\w+_(MPF|SPF)` (Sinumerik). */
    header?: Pattern;
    // M8 and the decision batch added `names`, `continuationStart`, `decimalSeparatorAlt`, `programNames` (§7.16 #10, #27, #34, #36).
    /** P9 (§7.16 #50). The prefix of a main block number (Sinumerik ':'), on `blockNumber`: a `blockNumber`
     *  token with this `address`, never the `:1234` tape marker; renumber keeps the prefix. */
    blockNumber: { /* … P1 fields … */ mainPrefix?: string };
    /** P9 (§7.16 #51). An assignment word may carry one bracket index before its `=` (`LIMS[2]=1800`):
     *  one `word`, `address` the identifier, `index` the bracket text (§7.5), `valueText` the right-hand side. */
    assignmentIndex?: boolean;
    /** P9 (§7.16 #52). The control's own multi-letter addresses among the assignment identifiers
     *  (Okuma `[ADFIKLRSTUWXZ][AB]|BC|BR|QA|CL|C[PQR]|S[XYZ]|T[LDS]`), matched against the whole
     *  identifier; every other one is a local variable. Tokens unchanged; grammar and hover read it. */
    extendedAddresses?: Pattern;
    /** P9 (§7.16 #53). The letters of an exponent inside a number (Sinumerik 'EX'): `1.5EX3` is one value
     *  of its word, `valueText` the whole text, `value` null (nothing computes with it yet). */
    exponentMarker?: string;
    /** 2026-10-08 (M9-2, §7.16 #172). One-character marks that address the value packed behind them
     *  (Klartext `#` in `CYCL DEF 7.1 #5` / `#Q5`); read where words are separated, `# 5` unchanged. */
    symbolAddresses?: string[];
    /** 2026-10-08 (§7.16 #175). What Remove Spaces may do: 'refuse' (Sinumerik) or 'keepAroundLongAddresses'
     *  (Okuma: the blank on both sides of a word whose address has more than one letter stays). */
    removeSpaces?: 'refuse' | 'keepAroundLongAddresses';
  };
  numbering: NumberingOptions;           // P1; `references` entries gain `rewrite`
  /** P11 reads it (P1 carried it). `tolerance` is ignored if present (§2.1). P11: + `keepComments` (§7.16 #134). */
  compare?: Partial<CompareOptions> & { keepComments?: Pattern[] };
  /** P13 reads it (P1 carried it). */
  editing?: { forceUppercase?: boolean; preventLineJoin?: boolean };
}
export interface NumberingOptions {
  // … P1 fields …
  /** P6: `rewrite` defaults to true; false = report only (Fanuc `M99 P`). */
  references?: { trigger: Pattern; addresses: string[]; rewrite?: boolean }[];
}

// The machine-parameter declaration (P6). NumberInput and NumberClass are imported from core/machines/types.ts (§7.15).
export interface NumberInputPreset {
  id: string;                         // 'is-b', 'calculator', 'okuma-10um'
  label: string;                      // display text (data, untranslated)
  value: NumberInput;
  /** Where the notes say so ('syntax-okuma.md §3.3'); absent together with `verify: true` = a documented default only. */
  source?: string;
  verify?: boolean;
}
/** A profile overlay: merged like `extends` (AD-16), limited to these members (validated). */
export type ProfileOverlay = Partial<Pick<Profile, 'modal' | 'toolCall' | 'numbering' | 'addresses'>>;
export interface VariantChoice {
  value: string;                      // 'A'
  label: string;                      // 'G-code system A'
  /** The code database for this choice (an AD-17 child of the profile's `codes`); absent = `codes`. */
  codes?: string;
  overlay?: ProfileOverlay;
  /** Scored on the first 400 masked lines, only when no machine is chosen. Unlike `detect.content`,
   *  each pattern scores its weight **once** (presence), so a marker a post repeats in every block
   *  cannot outvote a single decisive one (WP6.1). */
  detect?: { pattern: Pattern; weight: number }[];
  /** Vetoes (M9 review NC-F3, §7.16 #101): when any pattern matches a scanned line, this profile scores 0
   *  for the file. A folder match still wins outright. Read by `detect.ts` (rule 5, `detectScores`), the
   *  compiler, the validator and Python `_nc_lex.compile_profile`. */
  vetoes?: Pattern[];
}
export interface VariantDecl { id: string /* 'gcodeSystem' */; label: string; default: string; choices: VariantChoice[] }
export interface MachineParamsDecl {
  /** Absent: numbers are read as the profile's JSON says, with no choice (Klartext). */
  numberInput?: { default: string /* preset id */; presets: NumberInputPreset[] };
  units?: 'mm' | 'inch';              // power-on default; absent = 'mm'
  diameter?: 'on' | 'off';            // lathes only; absent = not a lathe parameter
  /** Modal groups whose power-on code a machine may set (the dialog offers their codes). */
  modalGroups?: string[];
  variants?: VariantDecl[];
  /** M12, AD-32. Ready-made channel setups a user can start from, never applied without a machine.
   *  P12 (the owner 2026-10-07, §7.16 #154): `fanuc-lathe`, `okuma-osp` and `sinumerik` declare
   *  presets from the manuals, every one `verify: true` with its source (§8.9); `waitLetters` are
   *  the address letters the control's wait codes may use (`parseWaitCodes` refuses others). */
  channels?: { waitLetters?: string[]; presets: { id: string; label: string; source?: string; verify?: boolean; value: ChannelParams }[] };
}

// M10 (§7.16 #114, #130): members of the existing `syntax` and `program` objects, validated in validate.ts
//   syntax.maxWordDigits?: number;   // the most digits a word may carry (Fanuc: 8, the lathe through `extends`)
//   syntax.maxMCodes?: number;       // the most M codes in a block (Okuma: 8)
//   syntax.blockSkip.plainLevel?: '0' | … | '9';   // the level a bare mark is ('1' on Fanuc, '0' on Sinumerik)
//   program.endRecord?: boolean;     // a line that matches `end` is the file's closing record (Klartext END PGM), not a block

// core/profiles/resolve.ts (implemented by P6; WP6.1 owns it afterwards)
export interface ProfileSource { raw: unknown; origin: 'builtin' | 'user'; file?: string }
export interface ResolvedProfile { profile: Record<string, unknown>; origin: 'builtin' | 'user'; file?: string; chain: string[] }
export interface ProfileProblem { origin: 'builtin' | 'user'; file: string | null; profileId: string | null; path: string; message: string }
export const MAX_EXTENDS_DEPTH = 4;
export function mergeProfile(parent: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>, child: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): Record<string, unknown>;
export function resolveProfiles(sources: readonly ProfileSource[]): { resolved: ResolvedProfile[]; problems: ProfileProblem[] };

// core/profiles/compile.ts: CompiledProfile.re gains toolIgnore? (P6); assignment?, labels?, systemVariables?, header? (P8)
// data/profiles/index.ts (P6)
export const BUILTIN_PROFILE_SOURCES: readonly ProfileSource[];
export const BUILTIN_PROFILE_JSON: readonly unknown[];   // the resolved built-ins, registry order
```

#### The M9 syntax pins (P9)

**The rule.** For M9, the `syntax` section and the outline **call rules** (the `subprogram-call` patterns) of every built-in profile — the five existing ones and `sinumerik-mill`, which inherits its syntax — are pinned at the P9 commit, as P8 pinned its two. The pin is a record and a test: `tests/fixtures/pins/m9-syntax.json` holds each resolved profile's `syntax` and call rules, and `tests/unit/syntaxPins.test.ts` fails when a built-in differs from it. WP9.3 and the content WPs test against exactly this data. A WP that needs another value writes a hand-off note instead of editing it; the M9 integration applies the note, updates the record and regenerates what it changes.

**Deferred pins.** Some values P9 decided change an existing token golden or grammar snapshot the moment they are written, and a prelude leaves every earlier test unchanged (§5.2 rule 2). They are pinned in the record's `deferred` list instead, and **WP9.3 writes exactly these values** into the profile JSON together with the golden and snapshot changes they cause (those members are WP9.3's in Wave A); the test accepts either the pinned or the deferred value of such a member until I9 folds the deferred values into the record.

**What P9 decided for each R4 case** (new field, data in an existing field, or code only):

| Case | Decision | Pinned value | Written | Reader |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fanuc macro functions and print commands (`FIX[`, `POPEN`, `DPRNT[`) | data: `syntax.keywords` (a keyword already ends where a letter does not follow it, and `[` starts an expression of its own) | + `SIN COS TAN ASIN ACOS ATAN ATN SQRT SQR ABS BIN BCD ROUND RND FIX FUP LN EXP POW ADP AX AXNUM POPEN PCLOS DPRNT BPRNT` (syntax-fanuc §3.5) | deferred (the `iso` grammar snapshots change) | WP9.3, no code |
| Okuma two-letter `=` words (`TL=`, `CL=`, `CP=`) | **new field** `syntax.extendedAddresses` (Pattern): the tokenizers already read every such word as one `word` with its address; what was wrong is the grammar's own constant, which painted the option addresses as local variables, and a user profile (M13) must be able to add its machine's | `[ADFIKLRSTUWXZ][AB]\|BC\|BR\|QA\|CL\|C[PQR]\|S[XYZ]\|T[LDS]` (syntax-okuma §3.2) | P9 (inert until read) | WP9.3: `okuma.ts` and hover read it instead of `EXTENDED_ADDRESS` |
| Okuma four-digit option M codes (`M1292`) | **code only**: the tokenizers read `M1292` as one word today; the Okuma grammar's code rule stops at three digits | – | – | WP9.3: `okuma.ts` rule 13 takes up to four digits for `M` (syntax-okuma §3.2: option functions use four) |
| Sinumerik `:123` main block | **new field** `syntax.blockNumber.mainPrefix`, not `altPrefixes` (a renumber rewrites an alternative prefix to `N`, which would turn a main block into an ordinary one) | `":"` (syntax-sinumerik §3.1) | P9 (inert until read) | WP9.3 (tokenizers: a `blockNumber` with address `:`, and the `:1234` tape marker yields to it); WP9.5b (renumber keeps the prefix) |
| Sinumerik call-rule exclusions (`CUT3DCC`, `ORIVIRT1`, `ORIRESET(`, `FGROUP(`, `WAITS(`, `INIT(`, `START(`) | data: the profile's `subprogram-call` outline rules (the tokens stay `call` and `unknown`: a predefined procedure is a call of the control, not a subprogram) | + `WAITS INIT START ORIRESET FGROUP` in front of `(`; + `CUT3DCCD CUT3DCC ORIVIRT1 ORIVIRT2` as bare names | P9 (no golden changes) | WP9.3 tests it; WP9.5b the other map items |
| Klartext `TIP-TIP` and the `PLANE` words | data: `syntax.keywords` (multi-word keywords already allow blanks between their parts) | + `PLANE PROJECTED`, `PLANE EULER`, `PLANE VECTOR`, `PLANE POINTS`, `PLANE RELATIV`, `PLANE AXIAL`, `MOVE`, `TURN`, `STAY`, `TABLE ROT`, `COORD ROT`, `SEQ+`, `SEQ-`, `MB`, `MAX`, `F TCP`, `F CONT`, `AXIS POS`, `AXIS SPAT`, `PATHCTRL AXIS`, `PATHCTRL VECTOR`, `REFPNT TIP-TIP`, `REFPNT TIP-CENTER`, `REFPNT CENTER-CENTER` (syntax-heidenhain §3.2); `SPA`/`SPB`/`SPC` and `DIST` stay value words | deferred (four lines of `tokens/heidenhain-klartext.json` and the grammar snapshot change) | WP9.3, no code |
| Sinumerik `LIMS[2]=`, `S[n]=`, `M[n]=`, `T[n]=` | **new field** `syntax.assignmentIndex` and the token member `index` (§7.5) | `true` | P9 (inert until read) | WP9.3 (tokenizers); WP9.5a (`scale_speed` reads the spindle from `index`) |
| `1.5EX3` | **new field** `syntax.exponentMarker` | `"EX"` (syntax-sinumerik §3.3) | P9 (inert until read) | WP9.3 (tokenizers; the grammar already reads `EX`) |
| Several skip levels (`/1 /3`) | **code only**: `blockSkip.levels` already says a level may follow the mark; each mark at the head of a block is a `skip` token of its own | – | – | WP9.3 |
| Sinumerik short header `%NAME_MPF` | data: `syntax.header` | `^%(?:_N_)?\w+_(?:MPF\|SPF)` (syntax-sinumerik §2.2) | deferred (the `sinumerik` grammar snapshot changes); folded into the pin file by I9 | WP9.3; `program.start` and the map's program rule read the same shape since WP9.5b. Detection still weighs only the long form `%_N_…` as decisive (§7.16 #43) |

**Validation (M9 review, §7.16 #95).** `blockNumber.mainPrefix` is refused when it is one of `( ) ; / " ' % $ < > + - . , = [ ] # *` or the first character of the profile's own comment starts, block-skip characters or continuation mark; `exponentMarker` must be at least two letters (one letter is an address). The three deferred values were folded into `pinned` at I9 and `deferred` is empty; the mechanism stays for the next deferral.

Klartext decimal comma and Fanuc program names were done before M9 (§7.16 #34, #36). No new field reaches a profile that does not set it: each is opt-in like the AD-24 fields, so every profile without it tokenizes as before.

### 7.2 Code database additions: `src/lib/core/codes/types.ts` (P6, P8, P9, P10; templates in Phase 3)

```ts
/** P6. What a code switches on; read by the modal interpreter (AD-19). */
export interface CodeSets {
  feedUnit?: 'per-minute' | 'per-rev' | 'per-tooth' | 'inverse-time' | 'travel-time';  // 'travel-time': Sinumerik G931 (M9 review, §7.16 #96)
  speedUnit?: 'rpm' | 'surface';
  distance?: 'absolute' | 'incremental';
  units?: 'mm' | 'inch';
  plane?: 'XY' | 'ZX' | 'YZ';
  /** A cycle entry starts or cancels a cycle; a non-modal start applies to its own block only.
   *  P9 (§7.4, §7.16 #49): 'define' stores the cycle without running it and replaces the earlier
   *  definition; 'call' runs the defined cycle once and ends a modal call; 'call-modal' runs it
   *  after every positioning block until a 'call' or the next 'define' (Klartext CYCL DEF / CYCL CALL, M99 / M89). */
  cycle?: 'start' | 'cancel' | 'define' | 'call' | 'call-modal';
  /** The S word of this block is a spindle-speed clamp (Fanuc A G50, B G92; Sinumerik G26). */
  speedLimit?: boolean;
  /** P9 (§7.16 #48): which side `speedLimit` bounds; absent = 'upper' (every entry before M9). Only with
   *  `speedLimit: true`. A 'lower' limit (Sinumerik G25) is neither a speed nor the clamp in force. Filled by WP9.5a. */
  speedLimitBound?: 'upper' | 'lower';
  /** Switches diameter programming (Sinumerik DIAMON 'on', DIAMOF 'off', DIAM90 'absolute-only'). */
  diameter?: 'on' | 'off' | 'absolute-only';
  /** P10 (decision of 2026-10-04, §7.16 #107): tool centre point control on or off — G43.4, G43.5, M128,
   *  FUNCTION TCPM, TRAORI 'on'; G49, M129, FUNCTION RESET TCPM, TRAFOOF 'off'. It is NO frame: under it X/Y/Z
   *  are the tool tip in the workpiece. The modal `tcp` follows it (§7.4 rule 14). */
  tcp?: 'on' | 'off';
  /** P10 (§7.16 #108): the bare axis letter of the block names the tool axis and the plane (Klartext
   *  TOOL CALL 1 Z → XY; Y → ZX; X → YZ; another letter → unknown). Only `true`. §7.4 rule 15. */
  planeFromAxisWord?: boolean;
  /** M10 (WP10.2, §7.16 #114): the code starts ('on': M3, M4; Klartext M13, M14) or stops ('off': M5) the spindle
   *  the plain speed word drives; `toolSpindle` is the same for a driven tool's own spindle (Okuma M13, M14 / M12). */
  spindle?: 'on' | 'off'; toolSpindle?: 'on' | 'off';
  /** M10: the code moves at rapid (G0) or at the feed (the path and thread moves, the Klartext path functions). */
  motion?: 'rapid' | 'feed';
  /** M10: cutter or nose radius compensation (G41/G42, RL/RR on; G40, R0 off); tool length offset (G43, G44, G43.4, G43.5 on; G49 off). */
  radiusComp?: 'on' | 'off'; lengthComp?: 'on' | 'off';
  /** M10: leaving this modal code sets the spindle speed to zero (Sinumerik G331, G332). */
  exitSpeed?: 'zero';
  /** M10: from this code on the control reads its ISO dialect ('iso': Sinumerik G291) or its own language ('native': G290). */
  language?: 'iso' | 'native';
}
// CodeParam (P1 §7.4) gains, P6:
export interface CodeParam {
  // … P1 fields (address, label, required, min, max) …
  /** How a value of this parameter is read (AD-31; it wins over every other rule). Absent: by the
   *  address's class. 'increment': a whole number of least increments, whatever the address suggests
   *  (§8.2: Fanuc G74/G75 P/Q, G76 Q, G83/G87 Q, in µm);
   *  'count': a plain integer no reading touches (dwell ms, repeat counts K/L, block and program
   *  numbers). The complete list per code is the table in §8.2, which WP6.2 and AD-31 both read. */
  unit?: NumberClass | 'increment' | 'count';
  /** P10 (roadmap R8, accepted 2026-10-01; §7.16 #106): what the parameter is to a program shift.
   *  'tool-axis' = an absolute coordinate on the tool axis (Z while the plane is XY; another plane refuses),
   *  moved with the axis words by a shift on the tool axis (Fanuc mill drilling R, Klartext Q203, Sinumerik
   *  RTP/RFP/DP/FDEP); 'none' = reviewed, no absolute position; 'other' = an absolute position the role does
   *  not cover: the block is refused whenever its entry has one (CYCLE800 points, CYCL CALL POS, CYCL CALL PAT);
   *  'mode' = absent, empty or 0 keeps the reading, any other value refuses (Sinumerik _GMODE/_DMODE/_AMODE/_AXN).
   *  Absent = not reviewed: a cycle call that writes it is refused. An axis word carries no role and moves as an
   *  axis word. The list is tests/fixtures/codes/positions.json (TS and Python). */
  position?: 'tool-axis' | 'none' | 'other' | 'mode';
  /** M10 review (NC-3, §7.16 #125): the parameter is the absolute coordinate of this axis (one letter) — the Sinumerik CIP
   *  point I1= / J1= / K1= — so address arithmetic moves it with the axis and the three points of an arc stay one circle. */
  axis?: string;
  /** P11 (§7.16 #136): the value names a program by its number, so search for `O2000` finds the call. 'packed' (Fanuc
   *  M98 P): digits in front of the last four may be a repeat count, so a long value is found under both readings.
   *  Only search reads it; a replace never follows it. The list is src/lib/data/codes/programNumbers.test.ts. */
  programNumber?: 'plain' | 'packed';
}
export interface CodeEntry {
  // … P1 fields (code, aliases, group, modal, pitchFeed, pitchFeedAmbiguous, label, description, params, verify) …
  sets?: CodeSets;                  // P6
  /** P8. The F word of this block is a time, not a feed (Okuma G04 F, Sinumerik G4 F). */
  fNotFeed?: boolean;
  /** dec/scaling (§7.16 #37): every word of the block is data (G65, G66, G10). P9: its axis words too. */
  wordsAreData?: boolean;
  /** P9 (roadmap R3, §7.16 #47). The axis words of the block are 'data' (a coordinate set, a shift, a
   *  rotation centre, a frame origin: G92, G52, lathe-A G50 X Z, G68, CYCL DEF 7) or a 'machine' position
   *  outside the program's frame (G53, M91/M92, G75, SUPA); its feed and speed words keep their meaning.
   *  Absent: positions in the program's frame. Never 'machine' together with wordsAreData (load.ts). */
  axisWords?: 'data' | 'machine';
  /** P9 (roadmap R3, §7.16 #47). The code opens or closes a coordinate frame that is not the program's own
   *  (G68, G68.2–G68.4, PLANE SPATIAL, cycle 19, CYCLE800, TRAORI / G69, G69.1, PLANE RESET, TRAFOOF).
   *  A code whose values decide (CYCLE800() with zero angles) is 'open', the safe reading.
   *  M9 (§7.16 #57): tool centre point control (G43.4, G43.5, G49, M128, M129, FUNCTION TCPM) is NO frame,
   *  because a block under it can be judged. P10 (2026-10-04, §7.16 #107): TRAORI is no frame either (it is
   *  `sets.tcp: 'on'`); TRAFOOF keeps 'close' for TRANSMIT, TRACYL and TRAANG.
   *  P10 (§7.4 rule 13, §7.16 #109): a 'close' ends the open frames of its own `group` only. */
  frame?: 'open' | 'close';
  /** P10 (§7.16 #109): written without values (CYCLE800(), TRANS / ROT / SCALE / MIRROR alone) the code closes
   *  the frames of its group, as the Siemens manuals say; with any value `frame` applies. Only 'close'. */
  frameWithoutValues?: 'close';
  /** 2026-10-08 (M10-2, §7.16 #171): the angle words of a tilt that reads as none when every one of them is zero. */
  frameZeroWords?: string[];
  /** M10 (WP10.2, §7.16 #114): the states in which the control refuses this code: `tcp`, `radiusComp`, `lengthComp`, `cycle`,
   *  `surfaceSpeed`, `feedNotPerMinute`, `frame:<group>`; a leading `!` turns one round ("refused unless"). */
  conflicts?: string[];
  /** M10: the code stands in a block of its own (Fanuc G53.1, Okuma M110); a `wordsAreData` code may carry its arguments. */
  alone?: boolean;
  /** M10: the block has to write at least one of these addresses or keywords (Klartext PLANE … MOVE, TURN or STAY). */
  requires?: string[];
  /** M10: the blocks from an 'open' to the next 'close' describe a contour a later cycle machines (Okuma LAP G81 … G80). */
  contour?: 'open' | 'close';
  /** M10 review (NC-2, §7.16 #124): the circle centre or pole of a dialect that writes it in a block of its own (Klartext CC):
   *  'set' on the code whose axis words are the pole, 'use' on the codes that move around it (C, LP, CP, CTP). The entry keeps
   *  `axisWords: 'data'` for readers that only ask whether the block moves; address arithmetic moves an absolute pole with its
   *  axes and extents work the polar moves out from it. */
  pole?: 'set' | 'use';
  /** M10 review (NC-6, §7.16 #128): the code runs another program and hands it the block's words as arguments (Fanuc G65). */
  call?: boolean;
  /** M10 review (NC-7, §7.16 #128): the code's axis words shift or set the coordinate system (G52, G92, the lathe G50, G10,
   *  Sinumerik TRANS / ATRANS, Okuma G50); the extents start a group of their own, a multiply or divide refuses it. */
  shift?: boolean;
}
// core/codes/lookup.ts (P9; WP9.2 owns it from Wave A on), Python twins in gedit_nc (axis_words_of, frame_of,
// speed_limit_bound_of):
export function axisWordsOf(entry: CodeEntry | null | undefined): 'data' | 'machine' | null;     // wordsAreData → 'data'
export function frameOf(entry: CodeEntry | null | undefined): 'open' | 'close' | null;
export function speedLimitBoundOf(entry: CodeEntry | null | undefined): 'upper' | 'lower' | null; // null: no limit
// P10, Python twins gedit_nc.position_of / tcp_of (and names_main_spindle, §7.4):
export function positionOf(param: CodeParam | null | undefined): 'tool-axis' | 'none' | 'other' | 'mode' | null; // null: not reviewed
export function tcpOf(entry: CodeEntry | null | undefined): 'on' | 'off' | null;
export interface CodeDb {
  // … P1 fields …
  templates: TemplateDef[];         // Phase 3 (P3.4); [] until then
}
/** P6. The file format; `CodeDb` is what loading and resolving give. */
export interface CodeDbFile {
  dialect: string; version: 1;
  extends?: string;                 // parent dialect id
  remove?: string[];                // parent codes this database does not have
  addresses?: CodeDb['addresses']; codes: unknown[]; templates?: unknown[];
}
// core/codes/resolve.ts (implemented by P6)
export function resolveCodeDbs(files: Record<string, unknown>, onProblem?: (dialect: string, p: CodeDbProblem) => void): Record<string, CodeDb>;
```

A `group` stays free text (P1). The modal interpreter treats every `modal: true` entry's `group` as a modal group; the content rules (§8) fix the names: `motion`, `plane`, `units`, `distance`, `feedmode`, `spindlemode`, `spindle`, `coolant`, `compensation`, `lengthComp`, `offset`, `cycle`, `cyclereturn`, `diametermode`. A machine's `modalInitial` may only name groups its profile lists in `machineParams.modalGroups`.

### 7.3 Registries and services (P6, P12, P13; `ModalService` in Phase 3)

**Phase 3 (2026-10-09):** the `ModalService` below is extended by the Phase 3 plan, [§6.2 of phase-3-implementation.md](phase-3-implementation.md#62-the-modal-service-srclibappmodalservicets-p2-73-extended) (`statesAfter`, `whenReady`, `AppContext.modal`; its §7 #205).

```ts
// app/types.ts — ProfileInfo (P1 §7.2) gains, P6:
export interface ProfileInfo { /* … */ machineType: MachineType; origin: 'builtin' | 'user'; parent: string | null; file: string | null;
  chain: string[] /* own id first, then the parents */; hasMachineParams: boolean }

// ProfileRegistry (P1 §7.2) gains, P6 (WP6.1 owns it from Wave A on):
//   effective(id: string, eff: EffectiveMachine): EffectiveProfile;   // applyMachine → validate → compile; cached by eff.key
//   detectVariants(id: string, text: string): Record<string, { value: string; margin: number }>;
// CodeDbService (P1 §7.3) gains, P6:
//   byId(dialect: string): CodeDb;                                    // a resolved database by id (variant databases)

// ProfileRegistry (P1 §7.2) gains, P13:
export interface UserFileText { name: string; text: string }
export interface ProfileRegistry {
  /* … P1 … */
  readonly revision: Readable<number>;              // bumps on every reload that changed the set
  problems(): ProfileProblem[];
  reload(user: UserFileText[]): ProfileProblem[];   // built-ins + user files, resolved again; never throws
}
// CodeDbService (P1 §7.3) gains, P13:
export interface CodeDbService {
  /* … P1 … */
  // No templates(profileId): a document's templates come from its EFFECTIVE database
  // (`machines.effective(docId).codes.templates`), because the variant is chosen per document (AD-28, AD-31).
  reload(user: UserFileText[]): ProfileProblem[];   // user code files merged over the built-in of the same dialect
}
// OutlineService (P1 §7.3) gains, P12 (AD-32):
//   toolLines(id: DocId, channelId?: string): number[];    // without a channel: the P1 list, unchanged
//   `items(id)` may return `channel` groups (the items of ALL of that channel's ranges as `children`,
//   its blocking marks as `sync` rows) and one "outside the channels" group for the ranges no channel
//   owns (header, inter-section, trailing); `itemAt` resolves through the groups. `OutlineIndex` is unchanged,
//   so every committed outline golden stays as it is. The grouping comes from `channels.forDoc(id)`.

// Phase 3 (P3.1; P11 before the third revision): app/modalService.ts → export const modal: ModalService
export interface ModalService {
  stateAfter(id: DocId, line: number): ModalState | null;   // null until the document's index is ready
  readonly changed: Readable<number>;
}
```

### 7.4 Modal interpreter (types P6; Python WP6.4, the Klartext defined cycle P9 and WP9.5; TS Phase 3, P3.1)

**Phase 3 (2026-10-09):** the TypeScript classes below are extended by the Phase 3 plan, [§6.1 of phase-3-implementation.md](phase-3-implementation.md#61-the-modal-interpreter-and-its-index-srclibcorencmodalts-deltas-to-p2-74) (`ModalIndex.stateAfter` may answer `null`, `buildSome`, `statesAfter`; its §7 #204). The state, the rules and the golden format here stay binding for both languages.

```ts
// core/nc/types.ts (P6)
export interface ModalValue { code: string; line: number /* 0 = assumed at the top */; assumed: boolean;
  /** For an assumed value: where it came from (§7.15 ParamSource: the document's machine, a detected
   *  variant's overlay, or the profile's default). Read from `modal.sources` (AD-19 rule 8). */
  from?: ParamSource }
export interface WordSeen { valueText: string; line: number; variable: boolean }   // `variable`: a variable or expression
export interface ModalState {
  /** Modal group → the code in force ('motion' → 'G1', 'feedmode' → 'G99'). */
  groups: Record<string, ModalValue>;
  feedUnit: FeedUnit;
  speedUnit: 'rpm' | 'surface' | 'unknown';
  /** 'absolute' without a line on a database that has no distance codes at all (AD-19 rule 8). */
  distance: 'absolute' | 'incremental' | 'unknown';
  /** The power-on value is assumed (machine, detected variant or profile); `G20`/`G21` set it with their line. */
  units: { value: 'mm' | 'inch' | 'unknown'; line: number; assumed: boolean; from?: ParamSource };
  plane: 'XY' | 'ZX' | 'YZ' | 'unknown';
  /** Diameter programming of the `addresses.diameter` words; null on a profile without the parameter (a mill).
   *  Whether one word is a diameter or a radius value also depends on `distance` (AD-19 rule 11). */
  diameter: { mode: 'on' | 'off' | 'absolute-only'; line: number; assumed: boolean; from?: ParamSource } | null;
  /** The tool of the last tool line: `station` as the profile's `tool` group captured it, `written` the whole word. */
  tool: { station: string; written: string; line: number } | null;
  feed: WordSeen | null; speed: WordSeen | null;
  /** Last clamp value (an S in a `sets.speedLimit` block, or a `speedLimitWords` assignment). */
  speedLimit: WordSeen | null;
  /** The modal cycle every positioning block runs ('start' until 'cancel'); P9: on a defining dialect,
   *  the defined cycle while a modal call is in force, and null otherwise. */
  activeCycle: { code: string; line: number; pitchFeed: boolean } | null;
  /** P9 (the "defined cycle", below): what the last `sets.cycle: 'define'` stored; null on every ISO database. */
  definedCycle: { code: string; line: number; pitchFeed: boolean } | null;
  /** P9: the code that switched the modal call on ('call-modal', M89) and its line, until a 'call' or a 'define'. */
  modalCall: { code: string; line: number } | null;
  /** P10 (rule 13): the innermost coordinate frame in force; null outside every frame. */
  frame: { code: string; line: number } | null;
  /** P10 (rule 14): the code that switched tool centre point control on; null while off (the power-on reading). */
  tcp: { code: string; line: number } | null;
  pitchFeedAmbiguous: string | null;
  /** Flags of the block just applied only. `cycle`: the cycle that RUNS in this block — a cycle entry of
   *  the block, or (P9) the defined cycle where the block calls it; a 'define' block runs nothing. */
  block: { cycle: string | null; pitchFeed: boolean; speedLimit: boolean; fNotFeed: boolean; toolChange: boolean };
}

// core/nc/modal.ts (Phase 3: the prelude's stub, P3.1)
export class ModalInterpreter {
  constructor(cp: CompiledProfile, db: CodeDb);         // the EFFECTIVE compiled profile and database (AD-31)
  reset(): void;                                        // modal.initial, modal.units, modal.diameter applied as assumed
  update(tokens: NcToken[], line: number, masked: string): void;   // one line; `masked` for the tool rule
  state(): ModalState;                                  // a frozen copy
  snapshot(): unknown; restore(s: unknown): void;
}
export class ModalIndex {
  constructor(cp: CompiledProfile, db: CodeDb, o?: { every?: number /* 1000 */ });
  reset(lineCount: number, getLine: (n: number) => string): void;
  applyChange(firstChangedLine: number, lineCount: number, getLine: (n: number) => string): void;  // drops snapshots ≥ first line
  stateAfter(line: number): ModalState;                 // 1-based
  ready(): boolean;
}
```

Golden format, shared by Python (M6) and vitest (Phase 3, P3.1), `tests/fixtures/modal/<profileId>/<case>.json`:

```json
{ "input": "../../nc/fanuc-lathe/l05-system-b.nc",
  "machine": { "variants": { "gcodeSystem": "B" }, "modalInitial": { "feedmode": "G95" } },
  "states": [ { "line": 12, "after": { "groups": { "feedmode": "G95", "spindlemode": "G96" }, "feedUnit": "per-rev",
                                       "tool": "01", "speed": "220", "speedLimit": "2500" } },
              { "line": 3,  "after": { "groups": { "plane": "=G18", "feedmode": "=G95@machine" }, "diameter": "=on" } },
              { "line": 20, "after": { "activeCycle": null, "block": { "cycle": "G71" } } } ] }
```

**The defined cycle (P9, AD-19; WP9.5b implements it in `_nc_modal.py`, the TypeScript interpreter follows the same goldens in Phase 3, P3.1).** Some controls run a cycle where it is written and keep it until it is cancelled (Fanuc `G81` … `G80`, Sinumerik `MCALL`): that is `activeCycle`, from `sets.cycle: 'start'`/`'cancel'`. Klartext **defines** a cycle and **calls** it later, and the two models differ in every rule a script relies on, so the state carries both, driven by the database's `sets` and by no dialect name:
1. A `'define'` entry (`CYCL DEF 200`) stores its cycle as `definedCycle` (`code` the entry's canonical code, `pitchFeed` the entry's flag) and **replaces** any earlier definition. It runs nothing: the block's `block.cycle` is null.
2. **No call and no tool change ends a definition**; only the next `'define'` replaces it. A tool call with a cycle defined is normal and changes nothing here.
3. A `'call'` entry (`CYCL CALL`, `CYCL CALL POS`, `CYCL CALL PAT`, `M99`) runs the defined cycle once: `block.cycle` is the defined cycle's code. It also ends a modal call in force. With no cycle defined it runs nothing (`block.cycle` null; the program check of M10 reports it).
4. A `'call-modal'` entry (`M89`) sets `modalCall`; **its own block** runs the defined cycle (the cycle manual says to write the first call with `M89`; M9 deviation, §7.16 #76), and so does every positioning block after it (a block with an axis word of the profile's `addresses.axes` that has a value, in a block whose codes do not make the axis words data: `CYCL DEF 7.1 X+5`, `CC` and `BLK FORM` are no positioning blocks; polar moves carry no axis word and are none either yet, §10.2). `activeCycle` is the defined cycle — the same meaning `activeCycle` has for Fanuc. A `'call'` or the next `'define'` ends it (`modalCall` and `activeCycle` null). The notes make `M89` modal only where a machine parameter says so (syntax-heidenhain §6.1), so its entry keeps `verify` until the owner confirms it.
5. A cycle that takes effect where it is defined (a datum shift, cycle 19, cycle 247) carries none of the three values, is never called and never replaces `definedCycle`.
6. A database without `'define'` entries (every ISO dialect) never sets `definedCycle` or `modalCall`, so every earlier golden reads the same.
7. A defined cycle never makes a block's `F` a thread lead: a Klartext cycle takes its values from its own parameters, and `block.pitchFeed` stays false on a call block (§7.16 #77). `FeedModeTracker` keeps its Phase 1 reading (§7.16 #78).
8. A `CYCL CALL` after a definition the database lacks (`CYCL DEF 202`, `208`, `262`) reads as "nothing defined": the generic `CYCL DEF` entry carries no cycle value on purpose (§7.16 #79). The program check of M10 must not report that as an error (§10.2).
The golden keys: `"definedCycle"` is the defined cycle's code or `null`, `"modalCall"` the code that switched the modal call on or `null`; `block.cycle` as before.

**The frame in force, tool centre point control and the Klartext plane (P10, 2026-10-04; Python in P10, the TypeScript interpreter follows the same goldens in Phase 3).** The interpreter numbers its rules after AD-19 (1–12; in `_nc_modal.py` rule 11 also covers the defined cycle and rule 12 the speed-limit bound, M9); these three follow it as rules 13–15:

13. **Frames** (`CodeEntry.frame`, `frameWithoutValues`). An `'open'` code is in force from its block on; writing an open code again moves it to the end instead of counting it twice. A `'close'` ends the open frames **of its own `group`**, and a close without a group those without one: `G69` ends `G68`–`G68.4` but not the scaling of `G51` (the Fanuc frame families are groups of their own: `frame`, `scaling`, `mirror`, `polar`, `cylindrical`), `PLANE RESET` ends a `PLANE` and cycle 19 (TNC 640 user's manual) but not the mirror of cycle 8, `TRAFOOF` ends `TRANSMIT`/`TRACYL`/`TRAANG` but not `CYCLE800` (group `tilt`). A code with `frameWithoutValues: 'close'` written **without values** — an empty argument list (`CYCLE800()`), or a block with no value word besides its codes (`TRANS`, `ROT`, `SCALE`, `MIRROR` alone) — closes its group instead. A code with `frameZeroWords` (owner decision of 2026-10-08, §7.16 #171: Klartext cycle 19, `PLANE SPATIAL`, `PROJECTED`, `EULER`) is open while any of those angle words stands at a value other than zero and closes its group once all of them are zero; a block that writes none of them changes nothing, and an angle not written keeps its value. A close that matches nothing changes nothing: when in doubt a position is inside a frame. `frame` is the last open one (`{code, line}`), Python also has `open_frames`; the golden key `"frame"` is its code or `null`.
14. **Tool centre point control** (`CodeSets.tcp`). `'on'` keeps the code and its line, `'off'` ends it, and a modal code of the **same group** as the code that switched it on ends it too (`G43` after `G43.4`). Off at power-on. It is never a frame: under it `X`/`Y`/`Z` are the tool tip in the workpiece. The golden key `"tcp"` is the code or `null`.
15. **The plane from the tool axis** (`CodeSets.planeFromAxisWord`). In such a block (Klartext `TOOL CALL`) the first word of an axis letter without a value names the tool axis: `Z` → `XY`, `Y` → `ZX`, `X` → `YZ`, any other axis letter → `unknown`; a block without one keeps the plane (`TOOL CALL S5000`).

**A speed limit's bound (P9, §7.2):** `speedLimit` in the state is the **upper** limit in force, the clamp; a block whose limit is `speedLimitBound: 'lower'` (Sinumerik `G25`) sets `block.speedLimit` (its `S` is not a speed) but not `speedLimit`. WP9.5b. `speed_limit_of()` is unchanged on purpose; the scripts read `speed_limit_bound_of`.

**Indexed assignments (M9 review, §7.16 #94).** A word with `NcToken.index` (`S[2]=`, `M[2]=`, `LIMS[2]=`) is an assignment word everywhere (modal state, `tool_list`, `scale_feed`, hover): it is never a code or the main clamp, and `S1=` and `S[1]=` read alike. **P10 (decision of 2026-10-04, §7.16 #112):** a speed word that names the **main** spindle by its number — `S1=` and `S[1]=` while `addresses.mainSpindle` is 1 (`gedit_nc.names_main_spindle`) — is the speed in force in the modal state and a tool's speed in `tool_list`, as it already was in `scale_speed`; every other numbered or lettered spindle word stays another spindle's. The modal state still does not follow `SETMS`: a plain `S` is the master spindle's speed, whichever spindle that is.

Only the listed keys are compared. `machine` (optional) is a partial `MachineParams` applied as the document's machine (source `machine`); without it the golden runs with no machine (profile defaults, source `profile`, variants as **detected**, source `detected`). A group value is the code, or `=<code>` for an assumed one, with the source appended when it is not the profile: `=<code>@machine`, `=<code>@detected`; `units` and `diameter` are the value with the same prefixes; `tool` is the station; `feed`/`speed`/`speedLimit` are the value text. Python exposes the same camelCase keys.

Both languages read the golden's **effective profile** from the generated `tests/fixtures/resolved/effective/**` (P6 item 12) — TS may also apply `applyMachine` itself, Python never merges — so a golden that names a machine or relies on variant detection runs against exactly one merge result. A golden whose effective profile has not been generated yet is a spec golden (§5.2 rule 3) and fails with "run `UPDATE_RESOLVED=1 npm test -- resolved`".

```python
# _nc_modal.py, re-exported by gedit_nc (P6 stub, WP6.4); standard library only, Python 3.9 syntax
class ModalInterpreter:
    def __init__(self, cp: "CompiledProfile", codes: "Sequence[dict]") -> None: ...
    def reset(self) -> None: ...
    def update(self, tokens: "Sequence[Token]", line: int, masked: str = "") -> None: ...
    @property
    def state(self) -> dict: ...        # the ModalState keys above
    # conveniences: feed_unit, speed_unit, tool, active_cycle, pitch_feed, pitch_feed_ambiguous,
    #               block_speed_limit, block_f_not_feed
class FeedModeTracker: ...              # P1 §7.10, now a wrapper; attribute values unchanged
def machine_type_of(profile: dict) -> str: ...       # 'mill' | 'lathe' (not the machine configuration: see machine_params, §7.15)
def incremental_axes(profile: dict) -> dict: ...     # {'U': 'X', 'W': 'Z'}
def diameter_axes(profile: dict) -> list: ...
```

### 7.5 Tokens (P8)

```ts
// core/nc/types.ts — P1 §7.4 TokenKind gains:
export type TokenKind = /* P1 kinds */ | 'label' | 'call';
// label: Okuma sequence name (`NLAP1`), Sinumerik `NAME:`; `address` = the name, upper case.
// call:  `CYCLE81(…)`, `MSG("…")`, `L10(1)`; `address` = the identifier, `valueText` = the argument text; not tokenized inside.
// assignment words: kind 'word', `address` = the letters ('SB', 'CR', 'S3'), `valueText` = the right-hand side; `value` null unless a plain number.
// P9 (§7.16 #51): NcToken gains `index?: string` — the bracket index of an assignment word without the brackets
// ('2' for `LIMS[2]=1800`, 'SPI' for `S[SPI]=300`) where `syntax.assignmentIndex` is set; absent on every other token.
// Python `Token.index` (default None) comes with WP9.3, together with the field list in tests/python/test_helpers.py.
```

The Python `Token.kind` takes the same strings; the token golden format is P1 §7.4's.

### 7.6 Search (P11): `src/lib/core/search/types.ts`

```ts
export type WordOp = '=' | '!=' | '<' | '<=' | '>' | '>=';
export type SearchQuery =
  | { kind: 'word'; address: string; op?: WordOp; value?: string /* decimal text; absent = any value */ }
  | { kind: 'text'; text: string; regex: boolean; caseSensitive: boolean; inComments: boolean };
export interface SearchHit { line: number /* 1-based */; start: number; end: number /* 0-based columns */; text: string }
export function parseQuery(input: string, cp: CompiledProfile, o: { wholeAddress: boolean; regex: boolean; caseSensitive: boolean; inComments: boolean }): SearchQuery | { error: Msg };
export function findInLines(lines: string[], cp: CompiledProfile, q: SearchQuery, o?: { max?: number /* 10000 */; codes?: CodeDb /* P11, §7.16 #136 */ }): { hits: SearchHit[]; truncated: boolean };
export function replaceInLines(lines: string[], cp: CompiledProfile, q: SearchQuery, replacement: string): { lines: string[]; count: number };
export function wholeAddressRegex(address: string, value: string): string;   // '(?<![A-Z_])G\\+?0*1(?:\\.0*)?(?![\\d.])' (P11, §7.16 #136, #143)
```

Value comparison is decimal: `1` = `01` = `1.` = `1.0`. A word query never matches inside a comment or string. On the program-number address it also finds the calls that name the program (`CodeParam.programNumber`, §7.16 #136); a replace never follows them. The types and the rules are in `src/lib/core/search/types.ts` (P11).

### 7.7 Compare (P11): `src/lib/core/compare/types.ts` and `CompareService`

```ts
export interface CompareOptions { ignoreBlockNumbers: boolean; ignoreWhitespace: boolean; ignoreComments: boolean; ignoreCase: boolean; ignoreNumberFormat: boolean; ignoreCycleNames: boolean /* §7.16 #148 */ }
export function compareDefaults(p: Profile): CompareOptions;
export function offeredOptions(p: Profile): (keyof CompareOptions)[];   // §7.16 #148: the toolbar's toggles for this profile
export interface Normalized { text: string /* LF */; lineMap: Int32Array /* normalized index → original 1-based line */; notes: Msg[] /* P11 */ }
export function normalizeLines(lines: string[], cp: CompiledProfile, o: CompareOptions, machine?: EffectiveMachine | null /* P11 */, over?: NormalizeOverrides /* §7.16 #144 */): Normalized;
// P11 (§7.16 #135): the steps of normalizeLines, exported for WP11.3 and the tests
export function pointSignificant(p: Profile, machine?: EffectiveMachine | null): boolean;
export function prepareNormalize(lines: readonly string[], cp: CompiledProfile, machine?: EffectiveMachine | null, over?: NormalizeOverrides /* §7.16 #144 */): NormalizeContext;
export interface NormalizeOverrides { pointSignificant?: boolean }   // §7.16 #144: one decimal-point rule for both sides
export function normalizeLine(line: string, cp: CompiledProfile, o: CompareOptions, ctx: NormalizeContext, prev?: LineState): { text: string | null; state: LineState };
export function unifiedDiff(a: { name: string; lines: string[] }, b: { name: string; lines: string[] }, o?: { context?: number /* 3 */ }): string;

// app/types.ts — CompareService (P1 §7.3) gains:
export interface CompareService {
  /* … P1 … */
  readonly mode: Readable<'raw' | 'review'>; setMode(m: 'raw' | 'review'): void;
  readonly options: Readable<CompareOptions>; setOptions(p: Partial<CompareOptions>): void;
  copyChange(direction: 'toModified' | 'toOriginal', o?: { lineOnly?: boolean; thenNext?: boolean }): Promise<boolean>;  // raw only; one undo step in the target
  exportDiff(o: { normalized: boolean }): Promise<DocId | null>;
  openFiles(a?: string, b?: string): Promise<boolean>;   // picks what is missing, opens both as documents, compares
}
```

`profile.compare` is `ProfileCompare` (P11): the toggles, optional, plus `keepComments?: Pattern[]` (§7.16 #134) and `cycleNames?: Pattern[]` (§7.16 #148: each with a `(?<name>…)` group whose span `ignoreCycleNames` drops; a profile without it is not offered that toggle); a carried `tolerance` is accepted and ignored. What each toggle may do, and what it never does, is written at the top of `src/lib/core/compare/types.ts`; the defaults per profile are §8.11. The P1 controller's raw view options are `view` since P11 (§7.16 #138), and the saved options are `ui.lastParams.compare` (§7.11, §7.16 #137).

### 7.8 Templates (Phase 3, P3.4): `src/lib/core/templates/types.ts`

```ts
export interface TemplateParam {
  id: string; label: string; type: 'number' | 'integer' | 'text' | 'choice';
  help?: string; required?: boolean; min?: number; max?: number; default?: string | number;
  choices?: { label: string; value: string }[];
  prefix?: string; suffix?: string;
  decimals?: 'as-entered' | 'min1' | number;
  digits?: number; plusSign?: boolean; uppercase?: boolean;
  comment?: boolean;                // wrap in the profile's comment delimiters
  remember?: boolean;
}
export interface TemplateDef {
  id: string; label: string; group: string; description?: string;
  toolbar?: boolean; snippet?: boolean; body: string; params?: TemplateParam[];
}
export interface TemplateEnv {
  cp: CompiledProfile; prevBlockNumber: number | null; numbered: boolean;
  sys: { date: string; time: string; file: string; stem: string };
}
export function loadTemplates(raw: unknown, onProblem?: (p: { path: string; message: string }) => void): TemplateDef[];
export function templateFields(t: TemplateDef): FieldSpec[];
export function validateTemplateValues(t: TemplateDef, values: Record<string, unknown>): Record<string, Msg>;   // never corrects
export function renderTemplate(t: TemplateDef, values: Record<string, unknown>, env: TemplateEnv):
  { ok: true; text: string } | { ok: false; errors: Record<string, Msg> };
```

### 7.9 Documents, recovery, session and memory (P7)

```ts
// app/types.ts — DocMeta (P1 §7.2) gains:
readOnly: boolean;
readOnlyReason: 'attribute' | 'user' | null;

// FileOps (P1 §7.2) gains:
restoreDocument(o: { path: string | null; title: string; profileId: string; machineId?: string | null; encoding: FileEncoding; eol: Eol;
                     nul: NulInfo; textLF: string; diskStamp: DiskStamp | null }): DocId;  // dirty; bound to `path` only when allowed
setReadOnly(id: DocId, readOnly: boolean): void;
onWillClose(cb: (id: DocId) => void): Disposable;                                        // before the model is disposed

// BookmarkService (P1 §7.3) gains:
set(id: DocId, lines: number[]): void;

// stores/fileMemory.ts → export const fileMemory: FileMemoryStore
export interface FileMemo { line: number; column: number; top: number; bookmarks: number[]; profileId?: string;
  /** AD-31: a machine id, or null for an explicit "none"; absent = follow the profile's default machine. */
  machineId?: string | null;
  /** AD-32, written by M12's `channels.assign`: which channel of a `multi-file` set this document is,
   *  when no pattern says. Additive to the M7 record; an M7 build keeps it verbatim like any unknown
   *  member, and a channel id naming a channel the current machine does not declare is ignored. */
  channelId?: string | null; at: number }
export interface FileMemoryStore {
  get(path: string): FileMemo | undefined;
  profileFor(path: string): string | undefined;
  machineFor(path: string): string | null | undefined;
  remember(path: string, patch: Partial<Omit<FileMemo, 'at'>>): void;   // LRU 500; bookmarks ≤ 200
  forget(path: string): void;
}
// app/session.ts → export const session: SessionService
export interface SessionService { start(): Disposable; restore(): Promise<number /* documents opened */> }
// app/recovery.ts → export const recovery: RecoveryService
export interface RecoveryEntry { session: string; key: string; path: string | null; title: string; profileId: string;
  machineId?: string | null; encoding: FileEncoding; eol: Eol; nul: NulInfo; diskStamp: DiskStamp | null; savedAt: number; bytes: number;
  /** G8 M7: true when the sidecar was never written, so everything above it is Rust's
   *  stand-in (`recovery.rs::orphan_meta`) and the text is all there is. */
  metaLost?: boolean }
export interface RecoveryService {
  start(): Disposable; flushNow(): Promise<void>;
  leftovers(): Promise<RecoveryEntry[]>; restore(e: RecoveryEntry[]): Promise<DocId[]>; discard(session: string): Promise<void>;
}
// UiState (P1 §7.3) gains:  files: Record<string, FileMemo>
```

### 7.10 Tauri commands (all structs `camelCase`; TS wrappers in `platform/commands.ts`)

```rust
// M6 quit.rs
pub struct QuitGuard { dirty: AtomicBool }                     // Default: false
#[tauri::command] fn quit_guard_set_dirty(guard: State<QuitGuard>, dirty: bool);
pub fn install(app: &AppHandle);                               // macOS: adds applicationShouldTerminate:, re-sets the delegate, logs on failure; elsewhere no-op
pub fn decide(dirty: bool) -> u64;                             // NSTerminateNow = 1, NSTerminateCancel = 0

// M6 machines.rs, config.rs, paths.rs
pub const MACHINES_VERSION: u32 = 1;                           // must match MACHINES_VERSION in core/machines/types.ts
pub const MACHINES_FILE_NAME: &str = "machines.json";          // paths.rs; AppDirs::machines_file() = <config>/machines.json
// config.rs: ConfigLoad gains `machines: Value` (always an object; `{}` when missing or unusable) and `machines_error: Option<String>`
pub fn read_json_object_versioned(path: &Path, name: &str, version: u32) -> JsonFile;   // P1 read_json_object = (…, SETTINGS_VERSION)
pub fn save_json_object_versioned(path: &Path, name: &str, object: Map<String, Value>, current: &JsonFile, version: u32) -> Result<(), String>;
#[tauri::command] fn machines_save(app: AppHandle, machines: Value) -> Result<(), String>;
//   a JSON object ≤ 1 MiB; `$version` stamped; atomic; an unusable previous file kept as machines.json.bak; refused when the file on disk is newer
#[tauri::command] fn machines_open_file(app: AppHandle) -> Result<String, String>;     // creates `{"$version":1,"machines":[],"defaults":{}}` if missing; grants that one file

// M7 backup.rs, session.rs, recovery.rs
#[tauri::command] fn files_backup(app: AppHandle, path: String) -> Result<Option<String>, String>;
//   Err unless is_allowed; Ok(None) when files.backup = off or the file does not exist yet
#[tauri::command] fn session_save(app: AppHandle, paths: Vec<String>, active: Option<u32>) -> Result<(), String>;  // keeps is_allowed, ≤ 50
#[tauri::command] fn session_load(app: AppHandle) -> SessionState;   // { paths: Vec<String>, active: Option<u32> }
pub fn grant_on_startup(app: &AppHandle);
#[tauri::command] fn recovery_put(app: AppHandle, request: tauri::ipc::Request<'_>) -> Result<(), String>;
//   raw body = UTF-8 text (≤ 64 MiB); header x-gedit-recovery = RecoveryMeta JSON (≤ 8 KiB) with the key ^[a-z0-9-]{1,64}$
#[tauri::command] fn recovery_drop(app: AppHandle, key: String) -> Result<(), String>;
#[tauri::command] fn recovery_clear_current(app: AppHandle) -> Result<(), String>;   // after the quit decision only
#[tauri::command] fn recovery_list(app: AppHandle) -> Vec<RecoveryEntry>;            // leftover sessions only
#[tauri::command] fn recovery_read(app: AppHandle, session: String, key: String) -> Result<tauri::ipc::Response, String>;  // raw bytes
#[tauri::command] fn recovery_discard(app: AppHandle, session: String) -> Result<(), String>;
pub fn start_session(app: &AppHandle);   // session folder (0700), heartbeat, pruning

// M12 channels.rs
```
