| `sinumerik` | **Header** (`G18 G90 G95 DIAMON`), **Tool change** (`T="…"`, `D1`, `G96 S… LIMS=… M4`), drilling `MCALL CYCLE83(…)` … `MCALL`, **Program end** |

Every parameter has a sensible default, `required` where the control needs it, ranges (a depth `max: 0`, a feed `min: 0`, a station an integer 1–99), and the number style of the dialect (`min1` decimals on Fanuc). Each template has a golden in `tests/fixtures/templates/<db>/<id>.json` (values → expected text).

### 8.7 The NC-correctness review (gate G10)

1. **Input:** the resolved JSON (`tests/fixtures/resolved/**`), the syntax notes, the fixtures and their goldens, the script diffs, and the intake findings (§9.2).
2. **Reviewer:** an agent that did not write the content.
3. **Per database entry:** the meaning matches the cited section of the notes; from M10 every `CodeParam.position` (R8) agrees with §8.10 and the cycle's description — an absolute coordinate on the tool axis is `'tool-axis'`, a distance from another parameter `'none'`, an absolute position the role does not cover `'other'`, a mode argument that can change the others `'mode'`; `group`, `modal`, `pitchFeed`, `sets`, `fNotFeed` agree with it (tapping and threading only for `pitchFeed`; one-shot cycles not `modal`; every modal group name from §7.2); the text is our own wording and short; a fact the notes mark **(verify)** carries `verify: true` or has a recorded owner confirmation; no machine-builder codes in a built-in.
4. **Per profile:** every pattern has at least one positive and one negative fixture line; the detection margin is ≥ 3 on every fixture, printed; the tool rule (trigger, `ignore`, `tool` group) is checked against the notes' tool-change section; every reference rule says whether it rewrites.
5. **Per script change:** every edge case of `nc-transformations.md` for that script maps to a test, and every "never scale" rule has a golden.
5a. **Per machine-parameter declaration** (§8.8): every preset and default is labelled as a default and cites its source or carries `verify: true`; each preset's `mode` per class is checked against the manual it comes from (a control whose unit parameter scales numbers **with** a point is `scale`, never `increment`); the default preset agrees with the profile's `syntax.decimalPointSignificant` (`true` only for an `increment` length class); each variant's detection margin is ≥ 3 on every fixture where it is `detected` (printed, with the presence scoring of WP6.1); overlays touch only `modal`, `toolCall`, `numbering`, `addresses`; the effective profile of every preset and variant (`tests/fixtures/resolved/effective/**`) is read; the parameter units of §8.2 are complete for every cycle the database defines; the number rules (`numbers.json`) agree with F52 and the per-class tables.
5b. **Per channel work** (M12, §8.9): **every built-in channel preset is `verify`, cites its source and is never applied without a machine** (since P12, 2026-10-07; the reviewer checks each against §8.9's table and `channelPresets.test.ts`); every fixture is synthetic and its channel and sync patterns are written for gEdit, never copied from a machine or a builder's manual (F61); the check's claims are read against what it computes — written ids and written order only, sequences and not sets, the jump rule as *targets and backward jumps* rather than labels, `blocking: false` never checked — and the user text says the same in the same words (`docs/user/channels.md`); **each of the three `SyncSemantics` kinds is checked against the mechanism it claims to model** (the reviewer reads the `count` rule against the "same number on both sides" rule and the `ordered` rule against the "smaller number first, both sides need not carry the same numbers" rule, and confirms that neither can emit `missing` or `unmatched`); every sync rule of every fixture machine has at least one positive and one negative line; a mark inside a comment is in the negative set; a correct program that repeats an id in both channels is in the clean set.
6. **Output:** `SP/review/<wp>.md` with *accepted*, *changed* and *needs owner*; the integration agent applies *changed*; the review table goes into the milestone commit body. *Needs owner* items go to §13 and never into hover; they block the milestone only when they decide a default behavior (for example, which code is per-revolution). A machine-dependent default never blocks: it ships as a documented default the machine configuration can override.

### 8.8 Machine parameters per profile (the defaults "none" assumes)

Every value here is a **documented default, not a fact** about the owner's machines (§8 header). "As written" = `calculator`: a number is read in mm, inch, degrees or seconds with or without a point. "In increments" = `increment`: only a number **without** a point is a count of increments. "Scaled" = `scale`: **every** number, with or without a point, is multiplied by the unit (Okuma). With no machine, a word the presets read differently gets no value at all (AD-31 "No machine, no guess"), so these defaults decide the wording gEdit shows, not the number it computes.

| Profile | `numberInput` presets (**default**) | `units` | `diameter` | `variants` | `modalGroups` | `modal.initial` (assumed) | Written by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fanuc-gcode` (mill) | **`is-b`**: lengths **and angles and the dwell `X`** in increments (0.001 mm / 0.0001 in; angles 0.001°, dwell 0.001 s, verify — Fanuc's calculator-type switch covers mm, inch and seconds together, so a control that reads `X50` as 0.050 mm reads `G04 X2500` as 2.5 s), feed per minute in 1 mm/min steps (0.01 in/min; the same number as written) and feed per revolution in 0.01 mm/rev steps (0.0001 in/rev) — **M9 replaced "feed per revolution as written"**, §7.16 #86; `is-c`: 0.0001 mm / 0.00001 in, 0.0001°, 0.0001 s, same classes; `calculator`: everything as written. Source: syntax-fanuc §3.2 (both readings occur; parameter-dependent). The default stays `is-b` because X10 pins every Phase 1 result to it. | mm (syntax-fanuc §4.2: the last state is kept, verify) | – | – | `feedmode`, `distance`, `plane` | `feedmode G94` | P6, WP6.2 review |
| `fanuc-lathe` | presets inherited from `fanuc-gcode` (M9: a point-less feed per minute counts in 1 mm/min and a point-less feed per revolution in 0.01 mm/rev, inch 0.01 in/min and 0.0001 in/rev, on IS-B and IS-C; source the 30i manual, parameters 1404 and 1405 at their defaults; **verify**; with no machine a point-less feed per revolution has no value; the mill's `addresses.angular` is `A B C`, so `C90000` is 90°), **default `calculator`** (and `syntax.decimalPointSignificant: false`): the owner's 18i-T system-B manuals write `G0 X40 W–40` for 40 mm and `G4U2` for two seconds, which is calculator type throughout (**verify per machine**; §8.1, D56). The lathe profile is new in Phase 2, so no Phase 1 result depends on it. | inherited | **`on`** (X and U as diameters) | `gcodeSystem`: **A** (`fanuc-lathe`) / B (`fanuc-lathe-b`, overlay `feedmode G95`) | `feedmode`, `spindlemode`, `plane` | A: `G99`, `G97`, `G18`; B: `G95`, `G97`, `G18` | WP6.2 |
| `heidenhain-klartext` | none declared: values are mm or inch as written (`BEGIN PGM … MM`/`INCH`); no machine item | – | – | – | – | none | – |
| `okuma-osp` | The control's unit parameter (UNIT) **scales every number, with or without a decimal point** (F52, OSP-P200L §2-3), so all three presets give `decimalPointSignificant: false`. **`okuma-1mm`** (default, verify): `calculator` — lengths 1 mm, feed per revolution 1 mm/rev, feed per minute 1 mm/min, angles 1°, time 1 s (inch system: 1 in). `okuma-1um`: `scale` — lengths 0.001 mm (inch 0.0001 in), feed per revolution 0.001 mm/rev (0.0001 in/rev), feed per minute 0.1 mm/min (0.01 in/min), angles 0.001°, time 0.01 s; so `X10000` is 10 mm and `F234.56` is 0.23456 mm/rev. `okuma-10um`: `scale` — lengths 0.01 mm, feed per revolution 0.01 mm/rev, feed per minute 1 mm/min, angles 0.01°, time 0.1 s; `X0.1` is 0.001 mm, `X1000` is 10 mm, `F23.456` is 0.23456 mm/rev (the control has no 10 µm inch system: inch is 1/10000 in or 1 in, so an inch value on this preset is **verify**). `S` is never converted | mm | **`on`** | – | `feedmode`, `distance`, `spindlemode` | `feedmode G95`, `distance G90` | P8 (skeleton), WP8.3 |
| `sinumerik` | **`calculator`**: as written (syntax-sinumerik §3.3), the only preset: the owner decided on 2026-10-08 (D54) that Sinumerik keeps "as written", so no IS-B or IS-C preset is offered and a point-less word has its value without a machine | mm | **`on`** (`DIAMON` default for turning, owner decision D35) | – | `feedmode`, `plane`, `distance`, `spindlemode` | `plane G18`, `feedmode G95`, `distance G90` (owner, 2026-10-08, M10-1; `sinumerik-mill`: `plane G17`, `feedmode G94`, `distance G90`) | P8 (skeleton), WP8.5 |

**The M9 variants (R6, smallest version; ids, choices and defaults pinned by P9, the data written by WP9.4).** Both are `machineParams.variants` entries whose choices carry an overlay only (AD-31): no `detect` (nothing in a program tells them apart reliably, so with no machine the default is assumed and shown with its source), no `codes`, no new consumer. The default choice of each reproduces today's reading exactly, so every earlier golden stays as it is.

| Profile | Variant `id` | Choices (`value`: what it means) | **Default** and why | Source | Overlay (WP9.4) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fanuc-lathe` | `incrementalAddresses` ("U, W, V and H move incrementally") | `uw`: `U` and `W` are incremental `X` and `Z`; `uwvh`: also `V` (incremental `Y`) and `H` (incremental `C`), the full system-A set; `none`: none of them is incremental — a system-B or -C machine whose parameter refuses them, where `G91` is the only incremental mode | **`uw`**, `verify`. It is the reading of both G-code systems' defaults, so the default follows the G-code system without depending on it: system A has no `G91` and moves incrementally with `U`/`W` by definition; in system B the owner's 18i-T manuals write `G0 X40 W-40` (D56), while two 31i system-B setups of the notes refuse them, which is what `none` is for. `uwvh` is not the default because `H` and `V` read as incremental would change today's reading; the owner has no data on their lathes (2026-10-03) | syntax-fanuc §4.1 (the `U`/`W`/`V`/`H` row), §3.2 | `addresses.incremental` (and, for `none`, `addresses.diameter: ["X"]`). Overlays merge by key (AD-16) and cannot remove one, so WP9.4 moved `addresses.incremental` out of the base profile into the choices (the resolved `fanuc-lathe` has none any more; `incremental_axes(profile)` and `numbers.ts` read the **effective** profile, §7.16 #71): `uw` writes exactly today's `{ "U": "X", "W": "Z" }`, and the effective profile of every document (no machine included: the default choice applies) is unchanged |
| `fanuc-lathe` | `toolWord` ("Tool word: offset digits") | `byLength` (M12.5): one or two digits are the station alone, three or four digits are tool + two-digit offset (`T101` tool 1 offset 01, `T0101` tool 1 offset 01), five digits are a two-digit tool + three-digit offset (`T12012` tool 12 offset 012); `offset2`: always the last two digits (`T12345` tool 123 offset 45, the "3 + 2" machine setting); `offset1`: the last digit is the offset (`T11` tool 1 offset 1, `T123` tool 12 offset 3); `offset3`: the last three digits (`T01001` tool 1 offset 1, the "2 + 3" machine setting, which reads a four-digit `T0101` as tool 0). On every choice a zero offset part (`T0100`, `T21000`) is an offset cancel and no tool change, unless the block also writes `M6` (`M06 T21000` loads tool 21), an all-zero `T` is never a tool, and a `T` in a block with a three-digit `G` code is no tool change | **`byLength`**, `verify` (M12.5, owner decision of 2026-10-08; before it the default was `offset2`, D49). The word's length decides, so no variant detection: nothing in a program proves 3 + 2 against 2 + 3, and the only evidence (offset equals tool) supports the default. `offset3` is never the default because it would read every four-digit `T0101` as tool 0. A machine that stored no `toolWord` now reads a five-digit word 2 + 3 (it read 3 + 2) | syntax-fanuc §5.2 ("1, 2 or 3 digits … a parameter of the control"); owner decision 2026-10-08 for the five-digit form | `toolCall` (`trigger`, `ignore`, `tool`) |
| `sinumerik-mill` | `toolChange` ("Tool change") | `m6`: `M6` loads the tool selected with `T` (the `T` in the same block or the last one before it); `t`: a `T` alone changes the tool, there is no `M6` (the turning profile's `T` rule) | **`m6`**, `verify`: the milling profile's reading since M10. The programming manual says the machine builder sets the type of tool change, so it is a machine choice. Variant detection (no machine): `M0*6` outside strings weighs 5 for `m6`, a lone `T` selection (optional `D`, a number or `T="name"`, never `T0`) weighs 3 for `t`; a program with any `M6` stays `m6`, a program without one reads `t`. The map never claims `L6` is the change | syntax-sinumerik programming manual §2.4 (tool change; "the type of tool change is set by the machine builder") | `toolCall` (`trigger`, `tool`, `toolFrom`) |
| `okuma-osp` | `toolWord` ("Tool word: offset digits") | `offset2`: two-digit offsets (`T0101` station 1 offset 01; `T010101` nose radius, station, offset — today's rule); `offset3`: three-digit offsets on machines with 200, 500 or 999 offsets (`T2000` is station 2, and the 3-digit form `T100` is station 1). As shipped, `offset3` reads 3, 4, 5 and 7 digits; a six-digit word is not a tool change (nothing says whether it is an old post's `rr tt oo`) | **`offset2`**: the owner's Okuma lathes have about 64 offsets and use two or three (2026-10-03), which is two-digit offsets; the three-digit split is an option, not the default | syntax-okuma §5.1 (offset sets of 32/64/96, or 200/500/999 with three digits) | `toolCall` (`trigger`, `ignore`, `tool`) |

Each choice's effective profile is generated into `tests/fixtures/resolved/effective/**` (`variant-incrementalAddresses-uw.json` …) at I9 and read by G10 (§8.7 item 5a); `incremental_axes(profile)` and the outline's `T` split are tested under each choice (WP9.4). The rest of R6 — extra axis letters, an IS-A preset, the ×10 option, the point-less feed-per-revolution unit — stays with §11 #26.

What a machine can change that is **not** in this table: nothing, except the `channels` block of §8.9, which is the user's own and which no profile enumerates. A new parameter, a new preset or a new variant is a data change to this table and the profile JSON, reviewed by G10; no consumer names a profile.

### 8.9 Channels and sync marks: what ships, and what does not (M12, AD-32)

**Revised by P12 (2026-10-07), on the owner's two decisions of that day (§10.1):** M12 starts before D64 with documented, manual-backed defaults per control, as machine settings, so the owner's later answer is data and not code; and the wait codes are something the user enters in plain terms — a list such as `M100-M199, M300`, the `P` word as a choice, stops and ends as a checkbox — with a regular expression only as the Advanced form. So three profiles now offer **presets** (`machineParams.channels.presets`, §7.1, §7.16 #154). A preset is a starting point offered on the Channels step of the machine page; **it is never applied without a machine**, a machine keeps its own copy, and every preset is `verify: true` with its source. Until the owner confirms one for their machine it is a documented default, exactly like a number-input preset (§8 header). No preset is a builder's whole sync code set: each holds the control's documented mechanism, and the one value that is a builder's choice — the Fanuc range of wait M-codes — is marked as such in its label and comes from the owner's own local program set (aggregate), not from a guess.

| Profile | Presets (`machineParams.channels`) | Wait letters | What each preset says | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fanuc-gcode` (mill) | none | – | – | multi-path waits exist on the mill too, but none of the owner's machines is a multi-path mill | – |
| `fanuc-lathe` | `fanuc-2path` | `M` | `multi-file`, paths `1`, `2`; file name = the path number at the end of the extension (`^(?<stem>.+)\.[A-Za-z]*-?(?<channel>\d{1,2})$`); one rule `wait`: codes `M900-M999`, rendezvous, partners from the `P` word as a **bit sum** (`P3` = paths 1 and 2), no `P` = paths 1 and 2; stops and ends not waits | Fanuc 30i/31i user manual, the multi-path waiting function: the wait M-codes are a range set by parameters 8110/8111 (the manual's example is M101–M103); the `P` word is a bit sum when parameter 8103 bit 1 is 0 (the default) and path numbers when it is 1; without `P` paths 1 and 2 wait; a wait M-code stands in a block of its own. The range M900–M999 and the one-file-per-path layout with the path in the extension: the owner's local multi-path set (aggregate: every `M9xx` there carries a `P` word and stands alone). **Corrected 2026-10-08 (M12.5):** that is true of the owner's set only; in a larger body of real CAM output all 26 real `M9xx` waits (two-path mill-turns) carry **no** `P`, the only `P` waits are 2 `M19x` words of a three-path builder in digit form, and no file is named with the path at the end of the extension (0 of 140) — so the range, the `P` form and the file names are each a builder's choice, and the label says so | verify |
| `fanuc-lathe` | `fanuc-3path-digits` | `M` | as above with paths `1`–`3`, `P` read as **path numbers** (`P12`, `P123`, `0` = path 10, a leading `0` not read), a wait without `P` names no partner (→ `unmatched`) | the manual: with three or more paths a wait without `P` is an alarm; the owner's local three-path set writes digit `P` words (aggregate) | verify |
| `fanuc-lathe` | `fanuc-3path-bitmask` | `M` | as `fanuc-3path-digits` with the `P` word as a bit sum (`P7` = paths 1, 2, 3) | the manual (parameter 8103 bit 1 = 0) | verify |
| `fanuc-lathe` | `fanuc-2path-nop` (M12.5) | `M` | `multi-file`, paths `1`, `2`, **no file-name rule** (tied by hand); waits `M900-M999` (one builder's range), `P` as path numbers when written, **no `P` = paths 1 and 2**; stops and ends not waits | the manual (without `P`, paths 1 and 2 wait on a two-path system); real CAM output (aggregate: 8 two-path mill-turn programs write `M999` alone before `M30` and `M990`–`M993` around a part transfer, never a `P`; only path 1 is generated, as a file `O<nnnn>` with no extension) | verify |
| `fanuc-lathe` | `fanuc-3path-digits-nop12` (M12.5) | `M` | `multi-file`, paths `1`–`3`, file name `^(?<stem>.+)_(?<channel>\d{1,2})\.[A-Za-z]+$` (the label warns that it also catches version files); waits `M190-M199` (one builder's range), `P` as path numbers, **no `P` = paths 1 and 2** (`whenAbsent: fixed [1, 2]`) | real CAM output (aggregate: one three-path set writes `M198`/`M199` without `P` in both paths and `M191 P123`/`M192 P13` in path 1; the shipped three-path presets report the `P`-less waits as 5 `unmatched`); the manual allows "no `P`" only on two paths, so this is a builder variant | verify |
| `fanuc-lathe` | `fanuc-2head-m100` (M12.5) | `M` | `multi-file`, paths `1`, `2`, no file-name rule; waits `M100-M197`, no `P` = paths 1 and 2; the label says that on such a machine `M198` calls a program on an external device and is no wait | a Fanuc-based two-head mill-turn programming guide (operation synchronisation, "the same M-code from M100 to M197 in both head programs"; `M198` external-device subprogram call) | verify |
| `okuma-osp` | `okuma-2turret` | `M`, `P` | `single-file`; turrets `a` ("Turret A", alias `G13`) and `b` ("Turret B", alias `G14`); `sectionStart` = a `G13`/`G14` word, as often as the program needs; rule `p-code`: codes `P1-P9999`, **`ordered`** (the smaller number runs first; a number on one side only is legal); rule `m100`: codes `M100`, **`count`** (both sides need the same number); stops and ends not waits | `syntax-okuma.md` §11.7a; the OSP-P200L programming manual, programming for simultaneous cuts on two turrets (turret selection, the `P` sync code, `M100`) | verify |
| `sinumerik` (and `sinumerik-mill` by `extends`) | `sinumerik-2channel` | `M` | `multi-file`, channels `1`, `2`, **no file-name rule** (the set is tied together by hand, or the user adds a pattern, D64); rule `waitm`: `regex` `WAITM`/`WAITMC` with the mark number, partners from the line split on `,` through ids and aliases (symbolic channel names become aliases); rule `setm`: `SETM`/`CLEARM`, **not blocking** (shown, never checked); `WAITE` is **not** a mark (it waits for the end of the other program; counting it would flag the normal case) | `syntax-sinumerik.md` §7.2a | verify |
| `sinumerik` | `sinumerik-2channel-archive` (M12.5) | `M` | `single-file`: `sectionStart ^%_N_(?<channel>\d+)_0_MPF\b`, `sectionEnd ^%_N_(?!\d+_0_MPF\b)` (the tool-data and other sections of an archive fall outside); the rules of `sinumerik-2channel` | real CAM output (aggregate: two archives hold both channels as `%_N_<n>_0_MPF` sections; read so, every line falls into the right channel and 18 marks pair with 0 findings) | verify |
| `sinumerik` | `sinumerik-2channel-c` (M12.5) | `M` | `multi-file`: `fileName ^(?<stem>.+)_C(?<channel>\d{1,2})\.MPF$`; the rules of `sinumerik-2channel` | real CAM output (aggregate: one builder's two-channel centre names its channel files `<stem>_C<n>.MPF`; that builder synchronises through a cycle call, not `WAITM`, which the user guide's Advanced example covers) | verify |
| `sinumerik` | `sinumerik-tagged` (M12.5) | `M` | `single-file`: `sectionStart ^<PROG_BEGIN_C(?<channel>\d+)>`, `sectionEnd ^<PROG_END_`; the rules of `sinumerik-2channel` | real CAM output (aggregate: one tagged single file; only channel 1 present). The tag shape `<PROG_BEGIN_Cn>` / `<PROG_END_Cn>` is a marker format and the fact itself: a regex has to match what a file writes, and the owner decided (2026-10-08) to keep the preset | verify |
| `heidenhain-klartext` | not possible | – | the profile has no `machineParams` at all, so it has no machine item and no channels | – | – |

**What the user writes, and where** (WP12.3's Channels step, the owner's decisions of 2026-10-07): the channels and their names (and aliases); the layout; **the wait codes as a plain list** (`M100-M199, M300 M350`, `P1-9999`), checked as they type with plain-language messages that quote the bad item ("`M2O0`: not a number", "`M300-M200`: the range runs backwards", "`G4`: wait codes on this control use M") and a live preview ("M100 … M199, M300 — 101 codes"); **the `P` word** as a dropdown — not read / path numbers ("`P12` = channels 1 and 2", `0` = channel 10) / bit sum ("`P3` = channels 1 and 2 (1 + 2)") — and what a wait without one means; **"Stops and ends count as waits"** as a checkbox (off by default: on a Fanuc two-path control `M02`/`M30` finish only once both paths reach theirs, which a post may rely on — D64 asks); and, behind an **Advanced** disclosure with the regex help page linked, the patterns for posts whose waits are not plain code words (`WAITM(…)`) and the section, file-name and marker patterns. The presets fill all of this, so most users never open Advanced.

**G10 for this table** (§8.7): the reviewer checks every preset against the cited notes and manual pages, that each is `verify` with its source, that no preset is applied without a machine (`channelPresets.test.ts` pins the table), and that the three `SyncSemantics` of the presets are what the manuals describe: a Fanuc wait M-code is a rendezvous with an id (the M-code) whose partners are the paths of its `P` word; the Okuma `P` code orders the two turrets and is not a rendezvous; `M100` is counted. The values that are the owner's to confirm are D64 (§10.2).

**What a machine may never express:** an execution model. The channel block says where the channels are and which lines are waits. It says nothing about what a wait does, how long it takes, or what the other channel is allowed to do meanwhile — and neither does the check (AD-32, D60). The user guide says this in the same words (WP12.7).

---

### 8.10 The R8 roles (P10; the G10 table of `CodeParam.position`)

What a program shift does with a cycle parameter (§7.2, §7.16 #106). The complete list, parameter by parameter, is `tests/fixtures/codes/positions.json`; this table is what a G10 reviewer checks it against. Sources: `syntax-fanuc.md`, `syntax-heidenhain.md`, `syntax-sinumerik.md` and the cycle and programming manuals of the source review (facts in our own words).

| Database | Entries | `'tool-axis'` | `'other'` | `'mode'` | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fanuc` (mill) | `G73`, `G74`, `G76`, `G81`–`G89`, `G84.2`, `G84.3` | `R` | — | — | `R` is the R-point plane, an absolute coordinate under `G90`; under `G91` it is a distance and the whole block is incremental, which address arithmetic skips. `X`/`Y`/`Z` are axis words and carry no role; `Q` (peck, shift), `P` (dwell), `K`/`L` (repeats), `F` are `'none'`. The drilling axis is `Z` under `G17` whatever the control's drilling-axis parameter says, and not certain in another plane |
| `fanuc-lathe`, `fanuc-lathe-b`, `okuma` | none | — | — | — | the lathe's own drilling cycles replace the mill's; whether their `R` is a distance or a position is a machine parameter in G-code system A, so every lathe cycle is refused as not reviewed |
| `heidenhain` | `CYCL DEF` 200, 201, 202, 203, 205, 206, 207, 208, 209, 240, 262 | `Q203` (surface) | — | — | every depth and clearance (`Q201`, `Q200`, `Q204`, `Q379`, `Q258`, `Q259`, …) is measured from `Q203`, so they stay; feeds, dwells, pitches, angles, diameters and directions are `'none'` |
| `heidenhain` | `CYCL CALL POS`, `CYCL CALL PAT` | — | `X`, `Y`, `Z` | — | the tool-axis coordinate of `CYCL CALL POS` acts as an extra datum shift on top of the cycle's `Q203`, and a start point the cycle defines adds to its `X`/`Y`; a pattern's points stand outside the block |
| `sinumerik` | `CYCLE81`–`CYCLE89`, `CYCLE840` | `RTP`, `RFP`, `DP`; `FDEP` of `CYCLE83` | — | `_AXN`, `_DMODE`, `_AMODE`, and `_GMODE` where it changes the depth's reading (`CYCLE81`: a centring diameter; `CYCLE82`/`83`: tip or shank; `CYCLE84`/`840`/`85`: reserved) | `SDIS`, `DPR`, `FDPR`, `_DAM`, the lift-off amounts and the technology words are distances or modes of no position; a mode argument other than `0` can make `DP` incremental, choose the plane or the drilling axis |
| `sinumerik` | `CYCLE800` | — | `_X0`, `_Y0`, `_Z0`, `_X1`, `_Y1`, `_Z1` | `_ST`, `_MODE`, `_DMODE` | the first point may be in the previous swivel (`_ST` ones digit 1), the second is in the rotated frame; `CYCLE800` opens a frame anyway |

### 8.11 The review-mode defaults (P11; the G10 table of `profile.compare`)

What a comparison in review mode ignores before the user touches a toggle (AD-26, §7.7). The values are pinned resolved, profile by profile, in `src/lib/data/profiles/compareDefaults.test.ts`; the rules every toggle obeys are at the top of `src/lib/core/compare/types.ts`. **The rule behind every value: review mode never hides a difference the machine would see.** A toggle is on by default only where the control reads both spellings alike, and each one keeps what the control could read apart. Sources: `syntax-fanuc.md`, `syntax-okuma.md`, `syntax-sinumerik.md`, `syntax-heidenhain.md` (facts in our own words).

| Profile | Block numbers | Whitespace | Comments | Case | Number format | `keepComments` (a line that matches keeps its comments) | Cycle names |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fanuc-gcode` (mill) | on | on | on | **off**: the code table has no lower case and drops it on input (`syntax-fanuc.md` §2.3) | on; with no machine the decimal point counts (IS-B and IS-C are declared) | the program title in the `O` block (`O1001 (BRACKET)`, `:1001`, `<NAME>`; shown in the directory), `#3000=`/`#3006=` (the comment is the alarm or stop message), a `%` inside a comment (it ends the program on input, `syntax-fanuc.md` §2.3) | not offered (no `cycleNames`) |
| `fanuc-lathe` | inherited | inherited | inherited | inherited | inherited; the `T` word is never reformatted (a tool and an offset counted by position: `T001` is not `T1`; D49) | inherited | inherited |
| `heidenhain-klartext` | on: the number is the block's position, jumps go to labels (`LBL`) | on: runs of blanks become one, the separator Klartext requires is never removed | on: `;` comments and `*` structure blocks; the `;` labels of the cycle parameters go with them | **off**: the owner knows of no control that refuses lower case and thinks modern ones convert it to upper case on loading (M11-2, 2026-10-07); the toggle is one click | on; the decimal comma reads as a point, `X10` = `X10.` (no presets, as written) | none | **off**, offered: the name after `CYCL DEF <n>` and the label words of an old numbered cycle (`compare.cycleNames`; the owner, M11-1, 2026-10-07) |
| `okuma-osp` | on; `N` names (`NLAP1`) are labels and never dropped; a kept number compares as text (`N0123` ≠ `N123`) | on | on | **off**: open in the manual (verify) | on; every preset reads `X50` and `X50.` alike (`scale` or as written), so the point does not count; the `T` word is never reformatted | a `%` inside a comment (verify: the notes say it of Fanuc only; kept to be safe); `MSG (…)` and `G215 (…)` (the message shown to the operator; M11 review NC-7) | not offered (no `cycleNames`) |
| `sinumerik` | on (`N`, `:`); labels (`LOOP_A:`) never dropped | on; a blank between two words is never removed (`N30XNOW=62`) | on | **on**: the control does not tell case apart except in tool names, which are strings and keep their case | on; one `calculator` preset (§7.16 #25), so the point does not count | `;$PATH=` (the control files the program by it on import), `*RO*`/`*HD*` (the cycle-screen markers; exact format verify) | not offered (no `cycleNames`) |
| `sinumerik-mill` | inherited | inherited | inherited | inherited | inherited | inherited | inherited |

**Kept by every profile, whatever the toggle:** a block number that a reference of `numbering.references` in the same file points at (`GOTO 100`, `G70 P100 Q200`, `M98 Q50`), and every block number of a file with a jump whose target is not a literal (`GOTO #1`; the review bar says so) — a reference from another file (`M99 P` returning into a main program) cannot be seen, so compare raw where a subprogram returns by number; labels; strings; the block-skip `/`; program numbers and names; variables' names; the fraction of a code (`G84.2`); the decimal point where a reading that applies is an increment reading.

**Off by default, by the owner's answer (M11-1, 2026-10-07):** the Klartext cycle name after `CYCL DEF <n>` and the label words of the old numbered cycles (`CYCL DEF 9.1 DWELL 1.5`). They are dialog-language text, not comments, and whether a control ignores them on import is still open (`syntax-heidenhain.md` §10 question 8). With the default a re-post in another dialog language shows every cycle header as changed, one line per cycle with its number visible; the toggle *Cycle names* (`ignoreCycleNames`, §7.16 #148) drops exactly those words and nothing else: the cycle number, the values, the axis words, the `Q` parameters and the continuation lines stay. A trailing comment after a Sinumerik cycle call is ignored, although the notes say it breaks the control's cycle-screen view of that call: it changes how the control's editor shows the block, not what it runs.

**G10 for this table** (§8.7): the reviewer checks every value against the notes cited, and every `keepComments` pattern against a line it must match and one it must not (the cases in the golden).

### 9.2 The owner's programs: local by default (FX)

**Owner decision (D45): real programs stay local.** The repository is public; the owner's own programs are never committed, pushed or shown to CI. The owner will hand over separately a few programs that are safe to publish. Everything real-program related therefore works from a **gitignored local folder** and skips cleanly without it. P6 creates the infrastructure (§6 P6 item 13); FX owns it afterwards.

**Two sets, never mixed:**

| | Local programs | Owner-public programs |
|---|---|---|
| Where | the folder named by `GEDIT_REAL_FIXTURES`, else `tests/real/` **of the main working tree** (`<git rev-parse --git-common-dir>/../tests/real`, gitignored except `README.md`) — a gitignored folder does not exist in a `SP/wt/<wp>` worktree, so a worktree run must look there and report "not found" rather than "no programs" | `tests/fixtures/nc/owner-public/<profileId>/` (committed) |
| What goes there | any program the owner wants checked, unchanged | only files the owner explicitly hands over as public |
| Manifest and goldens | `manifest.json` in that folder, and any golden derived from a local program: **local too** (they can reveal comments, tool names, part numbers). No snapshot API in the local tests (standing rule 12): a vitest snapshot would write program text into a committed `.snap` file or into the test file itself | the usual detection, outline and token goldens, committed like any fixture |
| Who runs it | G11 on the owner's machine only | every test run and CI |
| Marker | none | none; `tests/unit/fixtures.test.ts` exempts this folder from the `WRITTEN FOR GEDIT` marker but requires a line in `tests/fixtures/README.md`: control, a generic CAM description, the hand-over date, "published with the owner's permission" (F51) |

**Local intake and G11:**
1. **Drop.** The owner copies programs into `tests/real/<control>/` (`fanuc-lathe/`, `okuma/`, `sinumerik/`, `heidenhain/`) or points `GEDIT_REAL_FIXTURES` at a folder elsewhere. `manifest.json` gives each file's expected profile, optionally its tool stations, an allow-list of expected `unknown` tokens, and the **machine** to run it with (inline partial `MachineParams`, or a machine id from a copy of the owner's `machines.json` placed next to the manifest; the tests never read the app's config folder), so G11 checks the programs the way the owner's machines read them. From M12 a manifest entry may also name a **channel set**: for a `single-file` program the expected channels and their line counts, for a `multi-file` one the other files of the set, plus the expected sync ids per channel. G11 then adds the channel checks (resolution equals the manifest, the wait-code check returns no finding on a program that ran on the machine, and a program the owner knows was wrong returns the finding they expect). Counts only, never a file name, a channel name or a mark as written (standing rule 12).
2. **Smoke (G11; a real, manifest-driven test since M9, WP9.6):** detection = manifest under the default fallback **and with every built-in profile as the open document** (a lucky detection fails), profile and the variant when no machine is named; no `unknown` token outside comments except the allow-list; outline stations = manifest; `tool_list` = outline; a byte-exact round trip; scale feed and scale speed at 100 % byte-identical; no crash; from Phase 3 (P3.1) on, TS and Python modal states equal on **every** line under the manifest's machine. Output: counts and pass/fail per check; a failure names the manifest index and the line number, never a file name or program text (standing rule 12). The commit body records "G11: n local programs, all pass", "G11 skipped: no programs in the local folder" or "G11 skipped: no local folder found" and which folder source was used (`GEDIT_REAL_FIXTURES` or the main working tree), never its path. The manifest members (`allowUnknown` with reasons, `knownGaps`), the check names and the printed and JSON form of the aggregates X13 reads are in `tests/real/README.md` (P9); the committed owner-public programs carry theirs in `tests/fixtures/expected/owner-public/known-gaps.json`. A run prints counts and fails only on a crash unless `GEDIT_G11=strict` is set; every manifest entry is counted, and a malformed one fails `noCrash` at its own index. The committed programs are checked by `tests/unit/ownerPublic.test.ts` (TypeScript half) and `tests/python/test_owner_public.py` (Python half) in CI (each remaining unknown token and map difference with its reason).
3. **Findings** from a local program become owner-visible notes (in `SP`, never in the repo) and content fixes in the next content WP, each proven by a **synthetic** reproduction written for gEdit, never by a copy or an excerpt. Where a real program contradicts the syntax notes, the content WP corrects the notes in the same milestone (it then also owns the relevant `docs/planning/syntax/*.md`), in general terms.

**Owner-public intake** (whenever the owner hands files over):
1. `node tests/gen/check-anonymized.mjs <files>` lists candidates for the owner's second look: comments with names, customers or part numbers, dates, paths, e-mail addresses and telephone numbers, non-ASCII names, `$…%` / `%_N_…` headers. Codes, numbers, spacing, encoding, line endings and a NUL leader stay byte-exact; gEdit never rewrites the files itself.
2. Only after the owner confirms, the files are copied byte-exact to `tests/fixtures/nc/owner-public/<profileId>/` with their README lines, detection and outline goldens; goldens derived from them carry `"ownerReviewed": false` until the owner confirms them.
3. The committed exit criteria never depend on these files (they stay on the synthetic `exit2/` set), so a later withdrawal of a public file costs a golden, not a gate.

**FX owns:** (in M9, WP9.6 and, for the Siemens milling programs, WP9.1 — see their Owns) `tests/real/README.md`, the `.gitignore` entries, `tests/fixtures/nc/owner-public/**` and its goldens, `tests/unit/realFixtures.test.ts`, `tests/python/test_real_fixtures.py`, `tests/gen/check-anonymized.mjs`, and the owner-public exemption in `tests/unit/fixtures.test.ts`.

---

## 10. Owner decisions (defaults in bold; the plan runs with the defaults unless the owner changes them)

§10.1 records what the owner decided on 2026-09-22. §10.2 still needs their answer; the plan runs with the defaults meanwhile. §10.3 are defaults they can simply accept, the new ones of this revision included.

### 10.1 Decided by the owner (2026-09-22)

| # | Question | Decision | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|---|
| D23 | G-code system of the Fanuc lathes (A or B)? | **It depends on the machine's setting**, so it is a machine parameter: one `fanuc-lathe` profile with the `gcodeSystem` variant A/B (databases `fanuc-lathe` / `fanuc-lathe-b`), chosen per machine configuration from a dropdown; without a machine it is detected from content, a tie reads as A. The separate `fanuc-lathe-b` profile is dropped. The `T`-word part of the old question stays open as D49. | AD-18, AD-31, §8.1, §8.8; M6 |
| D24 | The lathes' power-on/reset state (feed mode, CSS, plane)? | **Machine-dependent**: a machine configuration sets it per modal group; the profile values (A: `G99`, `G97`, `G18`; B: `G95`, `G97`, `G18`) are documented defaults, shown as assumed with their source. | AD-19 rule 8, AD-31, §8.8; M6 |
| D34 | Okuma: are numbers without a decimal point mm or µm? | **It depends on the machine's unit setting, for Okuma and for Fanuc alike** (and in principle any control): gEdit must not hardcode it but offer it as a setting, per machine configuration, selectable from a dropdown. Presets per profile (IS-B, IS-C, calculator; Okuma 1 mm / 1 µm / 10 µm, per address class); "none" assumes the documented default of §8.8 and says so. | AD-31, §7.15, §8.8; M6 (Fanuc), M8 (Okuma) |
| D35 | Sinumerik turning: `DIAMON` by default? | **`DIAMON` is the default for the turning profile** (`machineParams.diameter: "on"`); a machine may start from `off`, the program's `DIAMOF`/`DIAM90` switch it. Both `T` forms and the assumed `G95` stay as proposed (not contradicted). | §8.5, §8.8; M8 |
| D58 | Multi-channel: where do the channel definition and the sync marks live? | **In the machine configuration, and user-definable.** How a machine's channels are laid out depends on the machine's configuration exactly as the number reading does: the channels may be **sections of one file** or **one file per channel**. The channel list, the layout with its patterns **and** the synchronization marks (wait codes) are therefore machine parameters that the user writes themselves (AD-32, §7.17). **gEdit ships no machine-builder-specific sync code set**, and in Phase 2 no preset at all (§8.9); presets may follow when the owner's real programs are in (D64). Multi-channel becomes milestone **M10** [M12 since 2026-09-30] (D63), staged: the model, the check, the per-channel views and the navigation now; the side-by-side view in Phase 3 [Phase 4 since 2026-09-30] (D61). | AD-32, §7.15, §7.17, §8.9, §11 item 27; M10 [M12] |
| D45 | Real programs as fixtures: publish or local? | **Real programs stay local**: never committed or published. Intake, G11 and the parity checks work from the gitignored `tests/real/` (or `GEDIT_REAL_FIXTURES`) and skip without it; the committed fixtures are synthetic plus the few programs the owner hands over separately as safe to publish. | §4 rule 12, §9.2; P6, FX |

**Decided by the owner (2026-09-27)**, after the [source review](source-review-2026-09.md) and its run over the owner's example programs:

| # | Question | Decision | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|---|
| — | Tapping speed (§9's pending decision, reopened with evidence) | **Refuse to scale the speed of a tapping block, and report it**: `scale_speed`'s `tap_speeds` pre-pass identifies every tapping block (a tapping code, a modal tapping cycle or mode in force, a modal call of a tapping cycle while it repeats, a DEF-active Klartext cycle while its `CYCL CALL` family runs it) and leaves its speed and the speed in force when it runs, of whichever spindle, as written; both are counted and reported ("N tapping speeds left as written"). Threading keeps the existing scale-and-warn behaviour, because it is not a tap in the database's own reading. | `CodeEntry.tapping`, `scale_speed.py`; §7.16 rows below; `dec/scaling` |
| — | The owner's published example programs | **Merged without the four large 5-axis files; the machine builder's name in the Siemens `CYCLE800` string stays** (the owner reviewed it and it names no one outside the shop). `tests/fixtures/nc/owner-public/**`, `ownerReviewed` still `false` until read over. | §9.2; `dec/int` (carried from `batch/int`) |
| — | The `T`-word format of the Fanuc lathe posts, five-digit case (part of D49, §10.2) | **A five-digit `T` word is a 3-digit tool plus a 2-digit offset** (`T12345` = tool 123, offset 45) — the existing profile rule (4 digits = 2+2, 3 digits = 1+2, 1–2 digits = station only) extended by one digit, implemented generically in `fanuc-lathe.json`'s `toolCall` pattern, not as a special case. Still **no machine parameter** in Phase 2; a sixth digit satisfies no combination and stays unrecognized, per the NC-correctness rule of leaving what no rule reads alone. D49's short-word (1–2-digit) question is still open, §10.2. **Revised by the owner on 2026-10-08** (below): the five-digit split is a machine setting, 2 + 3 without a machine. | `fanuc-lathe.json`; `dec/tools` |
| — | The Sinumerik main spindle | **A plain `S` while the master spindle is the main spindle (the default, `SETMS`, or `SETMS(1)`), and `S1=`/`S[1]=`, are the main spindle's speed** (the owner confirmed `S1=` as the main spindle on 2026-09-27). Implemented as the profile default `addresses.mainSpindle: "1"` — a profile default, not a machine parameter (a variant would add a Machines-page selector for a choice nobody has asked for yet; it can become one later, data only). `S2=`, `S[n≠1]=` and a plain `S` after `SETMS(n≠1)` stay other spindles, reported and left unless "Also scale other spindles" is on. Okuma's `G141` (spindle select) is left as it is: a plain `S` after it still scales as the main spindle's, because `G141` is not a spindle-choice call the way `SETMS` is, and whether the sub spindle's plain `S` should count as another spindle needs the owner (open, TODO). | `sinumerik.json` `addresses.mainSpindle`, profile `types.ts`/`validate.ts`; §7.16 rows below; `dec/scaling` |
